# The build through `omnia`

A round of the eval, run and read under [the protocol](README.md#protocol); [the README](README.md#rounds) indexes every round.

## The omnia target's first card

The second target adapter, [`omnia`](../../targets/omnia/), lands a plan as an omnia 0.36.0 HTTP guest in Rust: a root `Cargo.toml` holding the workspace and every pin, a root crate `app/` holding the `wasip3` export and the router list, neither the workspace manifest nor the export's file edited by any slice once laid; one crate per stem under `crates/<stem>/` (`app-<stem>`), every slice a `src/<part>.rs` listed by a `pub mod` line; `#[tokio::test]` tests per scenario under the stem's `tests/`; four lists slices append to and nothing else edited — the root crate's `[dependencies]` in `app/Cargo.toml`, the router list `app/src/routes.rs`, a stem's `pub mod` list in `crates/<stem>/src/lib.rs`, a stem's `[dependencies]` — each a union-merged file holding one block slices add lines to; `Cargo.lock` kept `ours`, and everything else, the pins, the export, and the six root files laid byte for byte among it, unruled. Its slice loop is `cargo clippy -p app-<stem> --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p app-<stem>`, `cargo check --target wasm32-wasip2`; its verify holds the six root files to the layout, then `cargo clippy --workspace`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip2`, and a `find -newer` over the tree for what the checks wrote, since the rerun's reading has it that no repository is reachable from `$WORKSPACE`. Nothing of it has met the model: the prose was written from a spike laid by hand, whose figures are the only ones this section has — cold on this machine, `clippy` 43 s, `cargo test` 22 s more, the wasm32 check 9 s, about 75 s a pass, a verify's three commands about 90 s, a cold registry fetch of some 600 MB before the first, and 2.4 GB of `target/` per working copy.

The run is `--rebuild <the boundary card> --target omnia express-orders` — the boundary card being the second rerun of [the build arm under the boundary](node.md#the-build-arm-under-the-boundary), `2026-10-11T23-43-20Z` — under `export RUSTUP_HOME="$HOME/.rustup"`, `CURSOR_EGRESS=index.crates.io,static.crates.io`, `CURSOR_READ_ROOTS="$HOME/.rustup:$HOME/.cargo/bin"`, `CURSOR_WORKER_ENV=RUSTUP_HOME`, the ladder, model, and binary the boundary card's rerun used, and `RUST_LOG` carrying `emery_engine=debug` beside the runner's default. `--rebuild` rather than `--build`: the plan is `run-1`'s of that card, byte for byte, the specify columns repeat its, and the engine builds every slice over a greenfield root holding no label, so what differs from that card's `express-orders` row is the target and the toolchain under the fence and nothing of the plan, the boundary, the backend, or the model. One run, as every first build card was. It runs after the rerun and never beside it, over the same binary; a rerun that opens no completion leaves no `run-1` to build again, so this card waits on that one's last clause. The predictions and the rule below were written before the run; the paragraph above was brought to the layout as `52b6728` has it, the router list in `app/src/routes.rs`, before the component was built from that commit, and nothing of the predictions changed with it.

### Predictions

- **O1, the loop holds on the Rust layout.** Every slice merged with no conflict in the plan's waves, the waves the rerun's row records; the four lists merge clean under `union`, and `Cargo.lock` under `ours` never conflicts. A conflict names a path, read by it: at one of the four lists, the one-block rule (two insertion points fewer than four unchanged lines apart break `git merge-file --union`), which is `layout.md`'s; under another stem's crate, or at a file the layout calls a slice's own, `build.md`'s wiring rule; at a root file laid byte for byte, two slices laying it differently, which is the prose's copy of the spike's tree.
- **O2, the verdicts are the tree's.** The host's three checks over the exported label — `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip2` — agree with every verdict, `app/tests/routes.rs` among the tests (a panic there names two modules registering one route), and `host checks` reads `ok` with every test passing.
- **O3, the fence carries the toolchain.** Every `cargo` a turn runs finds the toolchain through `RUSTUP_HOME` and the two read roots, fetches the index and the crates through the `CONNECT` proxy from the two hosts named, and no completion ends `inactive` on a running `cargo`; the bridge goes through the relay, as the rerun's P2 has it. A refused `CONNECT` names a third host the fetch needs, `CURSOR_EGRESS` short and the recipe in [evals/README.md](../README.md) to correct, recorded as a finding and not patched into the run; a `cargo` that finds no toolchain, or cannot write its registry cache, is the knob set's and the same.
- **O4, the turns.** Build turns within 120–600 s: the confirmation's 62–219 s for the model's part, plus the spike's cold pass run two or three times, plus the fetch once; verify turns under 300 s; at most one correction round a turn; no `secret-detected`, since no tree holds a credential and the worker's `HOME` holds no `~/.cargo/credentials.toml`.
- **O5, the fidelity figures.** No tracked file outside the layout (the root files and the root crate's, `README.md`, `crates/<stem>/**`); at least 80% of the merged slices' scenarios have a test named by them — the first card the Rust reader grades, so the figure is a baseline and not the confirmation's 90% bar; `covered` within the rerun's `express-orders` band.
- **Observed, never decided on:** the tokens; the disk the wave's working copies take; which slice laid the root files and whether `start` is a crate of code or the root alone; the `cargo` fetch's bytes through the proxy; and the kernel's denials for `cargo`, `rustc`, and `rustup`, which no card has read — the reading is this card's baseline for the Rust toolchain, as the boundary section's `log show` is for the bridge.

### Decision rule

- **The target is confirmed** when O1 and O2 hold: every slice merged with no conflict in the plan's waves, and the host agreeing with every verdict. The card is then the arm's baseline, and a change to its prose or rules is a second card read against it, never an edit of this one's reading.
- **A conflict under O1** is read by its path, as the prediction lays them out: a list is the one-block rule, another slice's file is the wiring rule, a root file is the byte-for-byte copy, and `Cargo.lock` is the `ours` rule misapplied, the adapter's own.
- **A host check failing beside a passed verdict under O2** is `verify.md`'s first, what the verify ran read from its tool-call lines; the workspace lint failing where every slice's `-p app-<stem>` lint passed is the seam between crates, `verify.md`'s repair rule, and a second repair round is the figure for it.
- **A refused `CONNECT`, a toolchain not found, or a cache not writable under O3** is the knob set, a finding against the recipe; an `inactive` completion on a running `cargo` refutes the backend's shell-phase exemption, as the confirmation's C2 had it.
- **A build turn slower than O4's band** is read from the turn's shell commands and their durations: the fetch through the proxy, or the compile; either is a figure pointing at a shared cache root for the workers, an omnia-backends follow-up and not this adapter's.
- **A run that opens no completion** is the backend's and decided before anything above.
- **Read against the rerun's row.** The plan, the binary, the backend, the boundary, and the model are that row's, so a failure this card shows and that row does not is the target's — its prose, its layout, its rules, or the Rust toolchain under the fence — and never the boundary's; a failure both show is the boundary's and recorded there.

### Results

One card, `2026-10-12T00-42-13Z`: `--rebuild 2026-10-11T23-43-20Z --target omnia express-orders` under the knobs above, the binary and the model the rerun's, the component built from `52b6728`. The plan is the rerun's `express-orders` revision `9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves (1/2/1/2/1), so the row below reads against the rerun's `express-orders` row and nothing of the plan, the boundary, the backend, or the model differs.

| Measure | Reading |
| --- | --- |
| The loop | **7/7 merged in 5 waves, 2285 s, no conflict**, the same waves as the rerun's 1145 s. Every completion opened, twelve, every one `ok` at the first attempt, no correction round. |
| The turns | Build turns 170–370 s on six slices, 655 s on `SLICE-001` (`bootstrap-api`, 490 s of it the model's, 160 s its tools: it laid eighteen files, the eight root files, the `start` crate, and a stub crate each for `customers` and `orders`); the five verifies 91–123 s, 74–83 s of each its `cargo` checks. |
| The toolchain | 66 tunnels, 24 to `index.crates.io:443` and 42 to `static.crates.io:443`, every fetch landing in the worker's own `HOME`; one `CONNECT` refused, `crates.io:443` in `SLICE-002`'s turn, neither of `cargo`'s two hosts and so a lookup the turn made by hand, which it went on without. No completion ended `inactive`. |
| Host checks | `cargo clippy` 0, `cargo test` 101 of 101, `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip2` 0, agreeing with every verdict. 86 of 100 scenarios named (`customers` 11/12, `health` 5/6, `orders` 35/41, `start` 35/41); no file outside the layout; `covered` 89/89, the rerun's figure. |
| The verifies' repairs | Three of the five wave commits carry a repair, and none is the lock alone: wave 1 restored the root `Cargo.toml` to the layout's bytes after `SLICE-001` had added `tower-http`, `http-body-util`, and tokio's `time` to the workspace pins, moving them into `crates/start/Cargo.toml` (check 1 on its first wave); wave 2 rewrote `app/src/routes.rs` (below); wave 4 added a line to `Cargo.lock`. |

**O1 holds and the finding is beneath it.** Every slice merged in the plan's waves with no conflict, `Cargo.lock` never conflicted, and the lists merged clean under `union`. But the list merged clean because `union` keeps both sides' lines, not because both sides kept the one-block rule: `SLICE-003` (`health`) rewrote the list's first line, `Router::new()` to `Router::<Client<P>>::new()`, folding two lines into one, where `SLICE-002` had appended beneath the two as laid; the union carried both forms, and wave 2's verify, repairing the duplicate, dropped `health`'s own line with it. `SLICE-004` (`customers`) added no line at all. **At the label, `app/src/routes.rs` mounts three of the five stems' routes**: `health`'s `/health/ready` and `customers`' three routes are in their crates, tested there, 101 of 101, and reached by nothing. **O2 holds, and the same finding sits beneath it**: the host's three checks agree with every verdict, `app/tests/routes.rs` among the tests, and neither the verify nor the host's pass can see an unmounted stem, since every test mounts its module on a fresh router and the smoke test asks the application router for a 404 alone. **O3 holds**: the toolchain was found through `RUSTUP_HOME` and the two read roots, the fetches went through the proxy from the two hosts named, and the one refusal is the turn's own lookup, recorded and not patched. The kernel's reading is this card's baseline for the toolchain: the unified log over the window names `rustc`, `cc`, `clang`, `ld`, `cargo`, `clippy-driver`, and `rustup` under the same `sysctl`, `mach-lookup`, and `vfs.disk-space` kinds the bridge's readings hold, and by path only what the fence is for — `clippy-driver` reaching 187 times for the `clippy.toml` of the repository this harness stages its projects under, `cargo` twice for its `Cargo.toml` and `rustup` three times for its `rust-toolchain.toml`, every one a tool's walk up from the lend into the tree above it, refused, so a worker built under another workspace's `target/` is never configured by it; four `cargo` writes to the operator's `~/.cargo` package-cache lock and four xattr reads beside them, refused, while every fetch landed in the scratch; a `find` the model ran over the harness tree above its lend, refused directory by directory; a `git` writing the slice copy's index in the repository beneath `.emery/vcs`, refused, so the head check the engine keeps for a copy the shell moved has nothing to catch under the fence; a scratch file at `/tmp` and the operator's `~/.cargo/registry/src`, refused. **O4 holds but for one turn**, `SLICE-001`'s 655 s against the band's 600, the model's part and not the compile; the verifies sit well under 300 s; no correction round; no `secret-detected`. **O5 holds**: no file outside the layout, 86% of scenarios named against the 80% floor, `covered` the rerun's.

**Decision — the target is confirmed, and its first tuning is named.** O1 and O2 hold: every slice merged with no conflict in the plan's waves and the host agrees with every verdict, so this card is the arm's baseline. What the card found is not a conflict and not a failed check but a program two stems short at a label every check passes, and the layout's one-block rule has no check standing behind it: the verify holds the six static files to their bytes and the lists to nothing. The next card changes the prose at the seam the finding names — `verify.md` gains a check that every `pub fn mount` under `crates/*/src/` is named by a line of `app/src/routes.rs`, the smallest mechanical reading of the list, and the smoke test or the layout has each stem's routes answer through `app::router` — and `build.md`'s list rule tells the slice that a line it finds is not its to rewrite, the form the list is laid in included. Three further readings travel with it: `SLICE-001` laid stub crates for two stems it does not own and edited the pins, both caught at the seams (the stems' own slices wrote over the stubs; check 1 restored the manifest), so the first slice of a Rust tree is the one to watch for reaching past its lines; the verifies' `cargo` checks run 74–83 s a wave on this plan, which bounds how often a wave can be verified before the figure is the toolchain's; and the fence's reading of `cargo`, `rustc`, and `clippy-driver` walking up from the lend is a property worth keeping, since a project staged beneath another workspace is exactly what this harness does.

### Amendment — the lists, recorded before the second card

The protocol's rule for a defect a card exposes, as the `git status` check and the `.env` fixture were: recorded here, fixed in the prose, the component rebuilt, the card put again. Read once more from the slice commits in `target.git` for this amendment, the sequence at `app/src/routes.rs` is attributed a step earlier than the reading above has it: `SLICE-001` laid the file with its line folded into the first (`let router = app_start::bootstrap_api::mount(axum::Router::<Client<P>>::new());`), where the layout spells `let router = axum::Router::new();` and a line beneath; `SLICE-002` rewrote that line back into the layout's two and appended its own; `SLICE-003` appended beneath the folded line as it found it; the union kept both forms, five lines mounting `bootstrap_api` twice, and wave 2's verify, which no check pointed at the list, rewrote it to two lines and dropped `health`'s. Every form compiled, `SLICE-002`'s included, so nothing in the compiler asked for the fold or the rewrite, and the layout's first line stands as written; the finding is the list rule's, and the verify's, which held the lists to nothing. What changed, in the smallest words at each seam:

- `verify.md` gains a check between the static files and the lint, **the lists reach every module**: every `pub fn mount` under `crates/<stem>/src/<part>.rs` is named by one `app_<stem>::<part>::mount(` line of `app/src/routes.rs`, and every `pub mod` line of a stem's `src/lib.rs` names a file beside it, a mechanical read (`grep -l 'pub fn mount' crates/*/src/*.rs` against `grep 'mount(' app/src/routes.rs`); the repair adds the missing line at the end of the list, removes a line naming a module the tree lacks, and rewrites no other, the first line standing as the layout spells it. The later checks are renumbered, the repairs paragraph says a list is repaired by adding the line it lacks and never by rewriting one it holds, and the worked example's failure cites check 4.
- `build.md`'s list rule says a line a slice finds in a list, the router list's first line included, is not its to rewrite, reorder, or fold its own into, whatever form the compiler would also accept, since the merge keeps every form it is given and the verify then has two; a missing list file is written as the layout spells it, the slice's line alone after the laid first line. Another slice's module is not a slice's to change nor to lay: no crate, module, or `mount` for a stem its requirements do not lead with, not as a stub. The root `Cargo.toml` is copied byte for byte, and a crate it does not pin carries its version in the stem's manifest and is never added to the pins.
- `layout.md` is unchanged: the first line's form was not the compiler's ask, so a change there would move the layout to the model's habit rather than hold the model to the layout.
- The `targets/<name>/` row of `AGENTS.md` and the 0.13.0 entry of `RELEASES.md` name the new check and the three rules.

What it changes in the reading: O1's "merged clean" is no longer sufficient on its own, since the union merges a rewritten line as cleanly as an appended one, and the second card reads the list itself (M1 below); O2's "the host agrees with every verdict" is read with the same caveat, since neither the verify's checks nor the host's can see a module the list does not reach until the new check runs. O3–O5 and the rule stand as written.

## The omnia target's second card

The first card put again over the amended prose: `--rebuild 2026-10-11T23-43-20Z --target omnia express-orders`, the exact command, knobs, ladder (`1200/400`), model, and binary of the first card, over a component built from the working tree at `3417b31` plus the amendment above, uncommitted, so the plan (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, 1/2/1/2/1), the boundary, the backend, and the model are the first card's and what differs is the prose. Two readings the first card made by `git show` are the runner's from this card on, and nothing the model sees changes with them: `fidelity.md` lists the modules the layout's lists do not reach (a `pub fn mount` under `crates/<stem>/src/<part>.rs` no `app_<stem>::<part>::mount(` line of the router list names) and the `scenarios named` cell carries the count as `· N unreached`; and each wave's line in the notes says what its `Wave <k> verified` seal holds besides `Cargo.lock`, `(lock only)` or the files, since the envelope's `repaired` counts the lock rewrite. `RUST_LOG` carries `omnia_cursor=debug,omnia_confine=debug` beside the first card's, so the fence's own lines sit in the log beside the kernel's. The predictions and the rule below were written before the component was built.

### Predictions

- **M1, the lists reach every module.** At the label `unreached` is 0: every `pub fn mount` under `crates/` is named by a line of `app/src/routes.rs`, so `app::router` routes `/health/ready` and the `customers` routes, which the first card's label left in their crates and reached by nothing.
- **M2, the first slice keeps to the layout.** The first slice lays the root `Cargo.toml` byte for byte and no crate it does not own, so wave 1's check 1 has nothing to repair, and no wave's seal touches a list: the wave lines read `(lock only)` or name no list file.
- **M3, found lines stand.** No slice rewrites a line it found: the router list's first line reads `let router = axum::Router::new();` at every slice commit and every wave, and every line after it is one module's `mount`, once, in the order the slices appended them.
- **O1–O5, re-read with the first card's bands.** O1 as before, with M1 standing behind "merged clean"; O2 as before, the host's three checks agreeing with every verdict, `cargo test` passing every test; O3 as before, the two crates.io hosts alone and no completion `inactive`; O4 with the first card's figures as the band — build turns 170–655 s, the first slice's the long one, verifies 91–123 s plus seconds for the new check's two `grep`s; O5 with 86% named and `covered` 89/89 as the comparison, no file outside the layout.
- **Observed, never decided on:** the tokens; which slice lays the root files and how many files the first slice writes against the first card's eighteen; the verify's shell lines for the new check, whether it ran the `grep`s as spelled or read the files; the kernel's denials against the first card's baseline, the `omnia_confine` lines beside them.

### Decision rule

- **The amendment is confirmed** when M1 holds with O1 and O2: `unreached` 0 at a label every slice merged into with no conflict and the host agrees with every verdict. This card is then the baseline Card C reads against, and its bands are Card C's.
- **M1 failing with every verdict passed** is the mount check's: the verify's shell lines show whether it ran the check and missed a module, or did not run it, and the finding is `verify.md`'s wording either way.
- **M2 or M3 failing** is the list rule's wording in `build.md`, recorded for a third card and never patched into this run; the slice commit that rewrote, folded, or laid past its lines is named.
- **A verify repairing a list** where M1 holds is the check doing what it is for, recorded under M2 as the number of waves it repaired and the lines it added; a repair that rewrote a line rather than adding one is the repairs paragraph's.
- **Read against the first card's row.** Same plan, binary, backend, boundary, and model: a difference is the amendment's; what both cards show is the layout's or the toolchain's; a build turn outside the band is read from its shell lines as before, and a refused `CONNECT` is the recipe's.

### The first attempt, dead at wave 2

`2026-10-12T05-22-45Z` is the backend's and decided before anything above, as the rule has it for a completion that opens nothing: the relay's upstream stopped answering twice in its window, eleven `relay upstream failed upstream=https://api2.cursor.sh` lines at 18:39 local that ended `SLICE-001`'s first turn `HTTP 502` at 589 s, 112 tool calls in, which the engine put again in a fresh copy, and one `https://api.cursor.com did not answer` at 19:01 that refused wave 2's verify its agent (`CreateAgent` 500, 0 tokens, 31 s), which the engine does not put again, so the run ended `bad_gateway` with three slices merged and the label at wave 1. The first card's log holds no such line; both upstreams answered within two seconds once the run had ended. What the dead attempt showed is recorded as observation and read against nothing: `SLICE-001`'s second turn wrote twelve files against the first card's eighteen, its own crate and no stub, the root `Cargo.toml` byte-identical to the layout's fence, and the router list's first line as laid, so wave 1's verify sealed nothing (M2 and M3 at wave 1); at wave 2's merged head the list held the laid first line and one `mount` line per merged slice in order, `health`'s among them, `0 unreached` (M1 and M3 at wave 2, before any verify); the turn itself took 783 s, 203 s of it thinking, with the model writing `bootstrap_api.rs` and its test file partially through `write_files`, which replaces a file whole, and restoring them six times before its checks passed, and the agent's context compacted once mid-turn. The attempt also exposed a defect in the runner's oracle: the host's `cargo clippy` over the unverified wave-2 head failed on `std::sync::OnceLock` and `Instant::now`, which are this repository's `clippy.toml` deny-list, found by clippy's walk up from the export, which sits beneath this repository, where the worker under the fence was refused that file 187 times on the first card and so built under no configuration; the first card's tree did not trip it. The runner now lays an empty `clippy.toml` at the export's root for the checks where the tree holds none, under which that head lints clean, so the host's pass reads the tree as the fence does; nothing the model sees changed, and the card is put again.

### Results — the second card

One card, `2026-10-12T06-05-44Z`: the exact command, knobs, ladder, model, and binary of the first card, over the component built from the amended tree, run 19:05–19:58 local. The plan is the first card's (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, 1/2/1/2/1), so the row reads against the first card's `express-orders` row and what differs is the prose.

| Measure | Reading |
| --- | --- |
| The loop | **7/7 merged in 5 waves, 3117 s, no conflict**, the same waves as the first card's 2285 s. Every completion opened, twelve, every one `ok` at the first attempt, no correction round. |
| The lists | **`unreached` 0 at the label**: seven `mount` lines after `let router = axum::Router::new();`, one per slice in the order the slices merged, and the first line as the layout spells it at every slice commit and every wave; every `pub mod` list one line per module, appended in order. The six static files byte-identical to the layout's fences at the label, the root `Cargo.toml` so at every slice commit. |
| The turns | Build turns 177–503 s on six slices, 796 s on `SLICE-001` (160 s of it the model's thinking, 112 tool calls, 26 of them `write_files`: it laid thirteen files, twelve of the layout's and one stray, its own crate and no stub). The five verifies 86, 153, 233, 531, and 135 s: the first and last the checks alone, the middle three each a repair. |
| The toolchain | 59 tunnels, 24 to `index.crates.io:443` and 35 to `static.crates.io:443`, no `CONNECT` refused, no completion `inactive`. The kernel's reading repeats the first card's by kind and by path: `clippy-driver` reaching 219 times for this repository's `clippy.toml` above the lend, `rustup` once for its `rust-toolchain.toml` and the model's `rg` and `grep` three times more, every `.git` of a working copy refused to the bridge and to `git`, a `find` the model ran over the harness tree refused directory by directory, and no write to the operator's `~/.cargo`. New this card: `/tmp` refused to `mkdir` and `touch` three times, each a turn's own habit against the `$TMPDIR` the prose names, which the turns went on without. |
| Host checks | `cargo clippy` 0, `cargo test` 101 of 101, `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip2` 0, agreeing with every verdict. 86 of 100 scenarios named, the first card's figure stem for stem; `covered` 89/89; **one tracked file outside the layout**, `.probe/Cargo.toml`, empty. |
| The verifies' repairs | Wave 1 and wave 5 sealed nothing. Wave 2 sealed `crates/start/Cargo.toml`, `crates/start/src/bootstrap_api.rs`, and its test; wave 3 `bootstrap_api.rs` again; wave 4 `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` and `bootstrap_api.rs` a third time, 178 lines added and 166 removed. None touched the router list or a `pub mod` list. |

**M1 holds.** `unreached` is 0 at the label: `app::router` mounts every module, `/health/ready` and the three `customers` routes among them, which the first card's label left in their crates and reached by nothing. The verify ran the new check as spelled in the two turns whose shell lines the log holds whole (`grep -l 'pub fn mount' crates/*/src/*.rs` against `grep 'mount(' app/src/routes.rs`, waves 1 and 2); from wave 3 on every command sits behind a `cd "<the lend's absolute path>" &&` and the log's subject, cut at eighty characters, shows the `cd` alone, so whether the later verifies ran it is not in the record. **M3 holds**: no slice rewrote a line it found; the first line reads `let router = axum::Router::new();` at every one of the seven slice commits and at the label, each line after it one module's `mount`, once, in slice order. **M2 holds in its first half and fails in its second.** The first slice laid the root `Cargo.toml` byte for byte, which every later slice commit holds unchanged, laid no crate it does not own, and wave 1's seal is empty, so check 1 had nothing to repair. But two wave seals touch a stem manifest, and the rule reads each by what it did: wave 2 added one line to `crates/start/Cargo.toml` (`app-health` among the dev-dependencies), a line the repair below forced and not the list rule's; wave 4 removed three from `crates/orders/Cargo.toml`, which merged with `serde_json`, `tokio`, and `tower` each named twice under its dev-dependencies, so `cargo` refused the manifest. `SLICE-005` and `SLICE-006` are both `orders`, both in wave 4, and both created the manifest from a head holding no `orders` crate: one kept the layout's dev-dependency block, the other added `omnia-wasi-sql`, `app-customers`, and `app-start` to it and a `[target.'cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))'.dependencies]` table beside it, and `union` over two creations keeps both versions of the hunk that differs, the lines common to both included. The one-block rule holds for a line appended to a found list and not for two slices of one stem creating the list in one wave; the second slice's departure from the fixed block is `layout.md`'s, the duplication is the merge's, and the repair caught it because `cargo` refuses a duplicate key out loud.

## Card C — the omnia target at scale

The second card's component over the boundary card's other plan: `--rebuild 2026-10-11T23-43-20Z --target omnia nestjs-pair`, the exact command, knobs, ladder (`1200/400`), model, binary, and component of the second card, so what differs from that card is the plan and nothing of the prose, the boundary, the backend, or the model. Five slices are `auth`'s and three `users`', across waves 5–9 and 7–9, so a stem's manifest and `pub mod` list are appended to by a slice that finds them in four waves, the case the second card's M3 held; no wave holds two slices of one stem, so the manifest two slices create at once, the second card's M2 finding, cannot arise here, and a duplicate key in a manifest is another cause. Through `node` the boundary card landed this plan 18/18 in 1343 s, `covered` 208/221, the thirteen uncovered being framework-vocabulary requirements the specify produced (`FileInterceptor`, `AuthGuard('jwt')`) that no target covers, 94% of scenarios named under the TypeScript reader. A conflict at a static file is the byte-for-byte copy failing in one of the two, named by its slice; a conflict at a list file is a departure from the laid form around the line, which the second card's amendment forbids, read from both sides' files.

### Decision rule

- **The target holds at scale** when N1 and N6 hold with every slice merged in the plan's nine waves and no conflict: the root files laid at once by two slices merge as the layout says, and the label is a program every list reaches and the host's checks pass. The card is then the arm's figure for a tree of eleven crates, and its bands stand beside the second card's.
- **A conflict under N1** is read by its path as the first card's rule had it: a static file is the byte-for-byte copy, a list file is the laid form around the line, both `layout.md`'s or `build.md`'s and named by the slice; it is recorded, and the slice's retry in the next wave is the engine's doing, read for whether the second attempt merged.
- **A verify outside N2's band that repaired nothing** is the toolchain's, read from its shell lines where the log holds them whole: the compile at eleven crates, or a fetch the integration copy's cache did not hold.
- **A `timeout` under N3** is the finding, not climbed; the turn's shell lines, where readable, say whether the compile under three concurrent builds or the model's part took the 1200 s, and a timeout on a turn whose last line is a running `cargo` is the contention figure this card was run to measure.
- **A completion ending on the budget under N4** is read by what the counted calls were: `write_files` fragments and restores are the second card's finding and no new one; a turn counting past 90 on whole files is the slice's size, and the budget's figure for the recipe.
- **N6 failing with every verdict passed** is read as the second card's O2 was: `unreached` above 0 is the mount check's, whether run and missed or not run; a file outside the layout is the shell's and named; a named figure under 80% is the Rust reader's or the tree's, read per stem.
- **A run that opens no completion** is the backend's and decided before anything above; a run the relay's upstream ends mid-turn is a dead attempt, recorded as the second card's first was, and the card put again.
- **Read against the second card's row and the boundary card's `nestjs-pair` row.** Same prose, component, binary, backend, boundary, and model as the second card: a difference is the plan's scale; same plan as the boundary card's row: a difference is the target's; what both show is the specify's.

**N1 holds.** Both slices of wave 1 laid the root files from a head holding none, the static six landed byte-identical, the two lists merged to one line each after the laid first line, and the seal was the lock alone. **N2 holds**: a verify that repaired nothing ran 95–108 s at two crates and at eight, the shell part 72–91 s, since the integration copy keeps its `target/` and each verify compiles the wave's crates alone; the band's upper half was never reached. **N3 holds, and the band was set too high**: no turn timed out, the three-wide waves ran their builds in 116–293 s and 219–379 s, no slower than the lone slices of waves 3 and 5 at 431 and 287 s, and six turns sat under the band's 180 s floor; a build turn's shell part was 73–96 s in the turns read, one `cargo` running or three, so on fourteen cores three concurrent dependency compiles cost nothing measurable, and the figure the card was run for is that there is no contention figure at this width. **N4 holds**: the busiest turn by the budget's count was `application-bootstrap`'s eighteen, every other under that, and the busiest by every call the last verify's 124, five of them counted. **N5 holds** and its figures are the table's. **N6 holds in every clause but the one it hedged**: the host agrees with every verdict, `unreached` is 0, 91% named against the 80% floor and above the second card's 86%, no file outside the layout, and `covered` is 221/221 where the boundary card's row has 208/221 and the prediction had the thirteen framework-vocabulary requirements uncovered here too. They were covered: `files`' `require-jwt` and `multipart-field` requirements as a bearer check and a multipart field in the Rust handlers, and `auth-core`'s five, the docs' statements of the token header, the payload types, and the rolling refresh window, as routes and probes of its own. Five of those `auth-core` routes are what waves 8 and 9 removed.

**What the card found past its predictions is the second card's finding in its other form, and the second card's `write_files` reading at its worst.** Two sources state the `auth` behaviours, the guide and the code, and the specification holds them as separate requirements under one stem, the guide's under `auth.refresh-hash-rotation`, `auth.logout`, `auth.sign-in-token-response`, the code's under `auth.login.*`, `auth.refresh.*`, `auth.me.*`; the plan put the guide's in `auth-core` at wave 5 and the code's in `auth-login` at wave 8 and `auth-account` at wave 9. `auth-core` covered its eighteen by mounting the login, refresh, logout, and `me` routes itself, every test mounting it alone; when `auth-login` landed, the smoke test met two modules on `/api/v1/auth/email/login` and three more, and wave 8's verify removed `core`'s four and pointed its tests at `login`'s module; when `auth-account` landed, wave 9's did the same for `/refresh` and `/me`, 103 lines of handlers with them. Every repair this run paid for past wave 7's one character was a route built twice because the specification states it twice, which is the specify pass's, as the second card's `/health/ready` was, at a scale where it cost two verifies 895 s and the longest turn of the run. Through the TypeScript target the same plan left those five requirements uncovered and built nothing for them; this target covered them and then unbuilt them, so `covered` 221/221 is read beside the three seals, not alone. The cross-cutting layer the second card found wrapping placeholders did not recur in that form: here `start`'s `application-integrations` exports `verify_hs256_jwt` and every stem that guards a route calls it, `files`, `users`, and `auth` alike, so the guard reached every stem as a function rather than a layer, and the one `.layer(` in the tree wraps its own module's routes. What the layout's one shape did instead is visible in the route table: the label mounts seventy routes, sixteen of them under the application's own prefixes (`/api/v1`, `/files`, `/`) and fifty-four probe routes (`/architecture/layering`, `/bootstrap/validation-pipe`, `/auth/jwt/revocation-policy`, nine under `run-seed`) that answer a JSON description of a behaviour the requirement states and no HTTP surface holds, since the documentation stems, the bootstrap, and the seed command are not routes and every module must `mount` one; forty-two of the 236 scenarios end `then [unknown]`, eleven of `architecture`'s eleven. Wave 9's verify is the `write_files` finding at its limit: it emptied `crates/auth/src/core.rs` with a zero-byte write at 08:21:47, spent the next five minutes looking for the content — `rg` over the worker's scratch for a `core.rs.new`, `find` over `~/.cursor` and the harness tree, `git rev-parse` against this repository, a `curl` to this repository's `main` on GitHub that the egress allowlist refused, and seven queries against a database in the bridge's own scratch, which is where it found the file — and rewrote it 103 lines shorter through a Python script, after which the checks passed. The SDK accepted the empty write as it accepts a fragment. The shell lines of every verify, and 170 of the run's 353 shell calls, sit behind a `cd "<the lend's absolute path>" &&` prefix that fills the eighty characters the backend logs, so which checks a verify ran is not in this run's record at all, and the readings above come from the seals, the durations, and the result sizes.

**Decision — the target holds at scale, and the figures stand beside the second card's.** N1 and N6 hold with every slice merged in the plan's nine waves and no conflict, the root files laid at once by two slices merged as the layout says, and the label is a program every list reaches and every host check passes; this card is the arm's figure for a tree of eleven crates and eighteen slices. The one prediction that failed, N6's `covered` clause, failed upward, and what it exposed is the specify's double statement of one behaviour landing as a route built twice. Three readings go to the recommendations: the specification's duplicate routes, which no target should be asked to reconcile at a merge; the layout's one shape forcing a probe route onto every requirement that states none; and `write_files` taking a zero-byte write of a 33 KB file in a verify, which cost 562 s and was recovered only by the model reading the bridge's own database.

### Recommendations

Compiled from the three omnia cards (`2026-10-12T00-42-13Z`, `2026-10-12T06-05-44Z`, `2026-10-12T07-13-30Z`) and the dead attempt between the last two, grouped by where each points. The target is confirmed and holds at scale; what follows is the cost and the gaps the cards measured, each with the card that found it, so the next card can be written against the one it takes up.

**The adapter's prose and layout.**

- *A cross-cutting rule needs a place.* The second card's `start` stem wrapped placeholder routes in its layers, so the program's `/api` routes answered unguarded at a green label, and every repair the run paid for was a real route meeting a placeholder; Card C's `start` reached every stem with an exported function instead and met no collision of that kind. `layout.md` should say which: a rule every route obeys is a function the owning stem exports and every handler calls (what Card C did unprompted), or a wrapping line the layout gives the `start` stem at the end of the router list; and the smoke test asks a guarded route for its refusal rather than any route for a 404.
- *A requirement that states no route is not a route.* Card C's label mounts seventy routes, fifty-four of them probes (`/architecture/layering`, `/bootstrap/validation-pipe`) answering a description of a behaviour no HTTP surface holds, and forty-two of 236 scenarios end `then [unknown]`, because every module must `mount` and the documentation stems, the bootstrap, and the seed command have nothing to mount. `build.md` should let a slice's `mount` return the router unchanged and hold such a behaviour as functions with tests; the verify's mount check still holds every `pub fn mount` to a line.
- *A stem's manifest wants one shape at creation.* The second card's two `orders` slices created `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` in one wave, one keeping the layout's dev-dependency block and one extending it, and the union duplicated three keys until the verify repaired it. The layout should spell every dev-dependency it allows once, as the fixed block it already calls it, so two creations differ only under `[dependencies]`; a test that needs more names it under a `[dev-dependencies]` line the layout admits by name.
- *Lay `clippy.toml` with the static files.* The dead attempt's host checks failed on this repository's deny-list, found by clippy's walk up from a tree staged beneath it, which the fenced worker never reads; a project a user runs `emery` in may sit beneath another workspace too. An empty `clippy.toml` as a seventh static file makes the tree lint the same wherever it is checked, and the runner's shim becomes unnecessary.
- *Give the model the API it probes for.* The second card's first slice spent 44 shell calls and a probe crate under `/tmp` (refused, then laid in the tree and sealed as `.probe/Cargo.toml`) reading omnia's handler signatures out of the registry sources, and Card C's turns ran `strings` and `nm` over rlibs for the same. A `references/omnia-api.md` holding the signatures a handler uses (`handle_with`, the route fns, `Context::provider`, the capability traits the layout bounds on) is one `read_doc` in place of that, and the build turn's cost is the first slice's 796 s.
- *What the shell leaves is sealed.* The slice's `written` is its copy's `pending`, so a stray the shell lays is committed with the work (`.probe/Cargo.toml`, second card). `build.md`'s loop should end with the tree holding the slice's files alone, and the verify's check 6 already reads the same for a wave.

**The SDK.**

- *`write_files` needs an edit shape or a guard.* The brief and the tool both say "replaced whole"; the model edits by fragment anyway: the second card's first slice sent eight fragments of 233–791 bytes over two 15 KB files and restored each at full length, its two `orders` slices eight more, wave 4's verify replaced a 14930-byte file with 11997 and spent sixty shell calls finding it, and Card C's wave 9 verify emptied a 33 KB file with a zero-byte write and recovered it from the bridge's own scratch database five minutes later. Two changes, either sufficient for the worst case: refuse a write whose `content` is empty, or shorter than half the file it replaces, unless the call says so, answering the refusal as a correction; and offer an `edit_files` call (`path`, `find`, `replace`, the match unique) beside it, so a one-character repair is not a 22 KB rewrite (Card C, wave 7).

**The engine.**

- *One behaviour stated twice is built twice.* Both Rust cards met a route specified under two subjects, `/health/ready` under `start` and `health` in the second card, the `auth` login, refresh, logout, and `me` routes under the guide's `auth.*` and the code's `auth.login.*` / `auth.refresh.*` / `auth.me.*` in Card C, and the verify unbuilt the earlier copy each time, 895 s of Card C's run. The specify pass records these as `behaviour split` and leaves them separate; the slicing brief should keep requirements that name one route in one slice at the least, and the basis should read a docs statement and a code statement of one route as one class where their anchors agree, which is the reconciliation the cross-source cards were written for.
- *A verify should be put again on `bad_gateway` as a build is.* The dead attempt ended on a `CreateAgent` 500 at wave 2's verify, which `RETRIES` does not cover; the integration copy is lent and nothing is sealed until the verdict, so a second attempt in the same copy costs nothing of the history.
- *`repaired` names a wave whose verify left anything pending*, the lock rewrite included: all nine waves the boundary card lists under it sealed `package-lock.json` alone, and three of the six Card C lists sealed `Cargo.lock` alone. `BuildOutput` could carry what each wave's seal holds, which the runner now reads from the chain, or the lock rewrite could be told from a repair by the adapter's `ours` rule, since the engine already knows which paths that rule covers.

**The runner.**

- The two readings this session added, `unreached` and the seal's files per wave, stand; next are the route table (routes per module, those under the application's prefixes against the rest) and the `write_files` sequence per turn (fragments, shrinking writes, empty writes), both read by hand for Card C and both mechanical.
- The verify's checks are unreadable in the record from the second card's wave 3 on (below), so the runner should read a verify by its shell durations and result sizes until the backend's subject is fixed, as this card was read.

**omnia-backends.**

- *The logged subject loses the command.* `observe` strips the `cwd` from a `path` argument and cuts the first line at eighty characters; a command the agent spells as `cd "<the lend's absolute path>" && cargo …` keeps its prefix and is cut before the verb, which is every verify command of Card C and 170 of its 353 shell calls. Strip the `cwd` wherever the first line holds it, or log the line after the last `&&` beside the head.
- *The relay's upstream failures end a run.* Eleven `relay upstream failed` lines in one window and one `did not answer` ended the dead attempt at wave 2; the three cards that completed met none. A retry of `CreateAgent` on a 5xx inside the relay, bounded, would have carried that run through.
- *The workers' caches.* Every slice copy fetched the crates again, 59 tunnels on the second card and 140 on Card C, and compiled the dependency tree cold into its own 2.5 GB `target/`; the fetch is seconds and the compile 30–40 s a turn, so the figure is small beside the model's part, and a per-deployment scratch the workers share for `~/.cargo/registry` is the one knob worth adding, the compile left where it is.
- *The bridge's scratch holds the session.* Card C's wave 9 verify recovered a file it had emptied from a database in the worker's scratch, inside the boundary and the worker's own, so nothing crossed the fence; it is recorded because what the scratch holds is what a turn can read back, which the boundary's reading should name.

**The recipe.** `CURSOR_EGRESS=index.crates.io,static.crates.io` held on all three cards; the three refusals (`crates.io`, `raw.githubusercontent.com`, `/tmp`) were turns' own lookups, each gone on without, so nothing is added. The second card's `RUST_LOG` with `omnia_cursor=debug,omnia_confine=debug` is the one to keep, since the fence's own lines are what the denials were read against.

**Taken up.** Every item but omnia-backends' landed in the working trees after this card, each where its recommendation pointed, and the root files went one step further than the fourth item asked. The adapter's: the cross-cutting rule is the exported-function shape in `layout.md`'s wiring section, `build.md`'s `start` bullet, and `testing.md`'s refusal test under the stem whose route it is; a slice whose requirements state no route returns the router from `mount` unchanged and holds its behaviour as functions with direct tests; a stem's dev-dependency block is the layout's fixed four lines, a test's extra crate a `[dependencies]` line; `references/omnia-api.md` holds the signatures of the route fns, the capability traits, and the test doubles as the 0.36.0 pin spells them, linked from `build.md`, `handlers.md`, and `testing.md`; and `build.md`'s loop ends with the verify's `find -newer`, strays removed through `delete` before the report. `clippy.toml` is not a seventh fence the model copies but one of nine root files the adapter now lays itself: `targets/omnia/seed/` is the adapter's `SEED`, which the SDK lays in a working copy that lacks them before the first turn and names in that slice's brief, so no slice reads the layout's fences back through `write_files` — the first card's folded router list, wave 1's repaired manifest, and the conflict the layout left possible are gone by construction — and the two list files are appended to through `edit_files` at the anchors the layout names. The SDK's: `write_files` refuses an entry that would leave a held file empty or under half its size unless the call sets `shrink`, logs each file's bytes, and `edit_files` stands beside it (emery 0.38.0). The engine's: a verify is put again on `bad_gateway` as a build is; `BuildOutput` carries `sealed` with each wave's paths and `repaired` names a wave whose seal holds a path outside the adapter's `ours` rules, so the lock rewrite alone is no longer a repair; and the double-stated route is two lines of the specify prose — `grouping.md` groups a docs statement and a code statement of one route, `slicing.md` keeps sub-stems stating one route in one slice — written as the arm of a card not yet run, the one the plan's §5 describes. The runner's: the route table per module with its probes, the `write_files` sequence per turn, and the verify's shell part are read mechanically now, and `repairs()` reads the envelope's `sealed`. Open: omnia-backends' three — the relay's bounded `CreateAgent` retry, a shared `~/.cargo/registry` scratch, and naming what the bridge's scratch holds — the logged subject being done (`28391c0`); the two seeding follow-ups, `node`'s three root manifests and a stem's crate at creation; and the card that confirms all of this, `--rebuild 2026-10-11T23-43-20Z --target omnia express-orders` over the amended component against the second card and the seeded-greenfield arm.

## The confirmation card

The second card put again over everything the recommendations changed at once: `--rebuild 2026-10-11T23-43-20Z --target omnia express-orders`, the exact command, knobs, ladder (`1200/400`), and model of the second card, over the component built from `abe7ab1` — the amended prose, the seed, and the two tools' bullets — and a release `emery` built from `c05c7b65` — the SDK's seed, `write_files` guard, and `edit_files`, the engine's verify retry and `sealed`. The plan is the second card's (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, 1/2/1/2/1: `start`'s `bootstrap-api` alone, then `start`'s `bootstrap-lifecycle` with `health`, then `customers`, then the two `orders` slices, then `orders-amend-and-transitions`), and the boundary, the backend, and the model are the second card's, so what differs is the adapter, the SDK, and the engine together and never one at a time; the rule below tells them apart by the shape of what fails. The plan's "measure first" step, a seeded-greenfield arm over the second card's component, was prepared as a worktree and never run: by the time it could have been, the SDK seed it was to decide on was built and merged, so its decision was spent, and the worktree was dropped with this card. The seed's signature is mechanical and unlike the other changes' — the `seeded` line in the log, the brief's laid-files sentence, the first slice's paths, wave 1's seal, the root files at every slice commit — so this card reads it directly and the card is honest about the step it skipped. The specify prose's two lines (`grouping.md`, `slicing.md`) are in the binary and reach nothing here, since `--rebuild` builds the committed revision; their card is the next engine arm's. The runner reads the route table per module with its probes, the `write_files` and `edit_files` sequence per turn, a verify's shell part, and each wave's seal from the envelope's `sealed`, so the readings the second card made by hand are columns and notes here. The backend's logged subject is still cut at eighty characters behind a `cd … &&` prefix — `28391c0` is not in the omnia-backends revision this binary pins (`2c1bbc0`) — so a verify's checks are read by durations and result sizes as before. The predictions and the rule were written before the binary and the component were built.

The second card's figures are the bands: 7/7 in 5 waves, 3117 s; build turns 177–503 s, the first slice 796 s with 26 `write_files` calls (23 of them rewriting a path the turn had written, thirteen files laid, twelve of them the layout's); the other build turns 4–22 calls, 84 calls and 72 rewrites over the seven; verifies 86 and 135 s where nothing was repaired, 153, 233, and 531 s where something was, 15 `write_files` calls between the three; wave 1 and wave 5 sealed nothing, wave 2 `crates/start/Cargo.toml`, `bootstrap_api.rs`, and its test, wave 3 `bootstrap_api.rs`, wave 4 `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` and `bootstrap_api.rs`; one file outside the layout; 86 of 100 scenarios named; `covered` 89/89; 36.3 M input and 440 K output tokens.

### Predictions

- **C1, the seed.** The first slice's turn opens over a tree holding the nine root files: the log's `seeded` line counts eight, the greenfield's `.gitignore` being the seed's bytes already and left as it stands; the brief names the eight; and every `write_files` path of the turn is under `crates/start/`. The nine are byte-identical to `adapters/omnia/seed/` at all seven slice commits and at the label, wave 1's seal is empty or the lock alone, and check 1 has nothing to repair at any wave.
- **C2, the lists through `edit_files`.** Every slice adds its lines with `edit_files` at the anchors the layout names — the router line on all seven turns, the `app/Cargo.toml` stem line on the first slice of each stem, the two `orders` slices of wave 4 each adding `app-orders` at the one anchor and the union keeping one — and no list file is written whole. At the label `unreached` is 0, the router list reads `let router = axum::Router::new();` and one `mount` line per slice in merge order at every commit, `app/Cargo.toml` holds four stem lines, and every `pub mod` list one line per module.
- **C3, the guard and the tool.** No turn writes an empty file, so every writes line reads `0 empty`; a shrinking write is refused or carries `shrink`, 0–3 refusals across the run, each followed by `edit_files` or a rewrite at full length; `edit_files` is called on every build turn and on every repairing verify; over the seven build turns `write_files` is under 50 calls and 25 rewrites against 84 and 72, the first slice's under 12 and 6 against 26 and 23.
- **C4, the wiring.** `start` lays no route under `/api/customers` or `/api/orders` and no placeholder: `authenticate`, the body limit, and the request log are `pub fn`s of `crates/start/` that `customers`' and `orders`' handlers call, and the smoke test asks an `/api` route for its refusal; so no verify meets a real route against a placeholder, and waves 3 and 4 seal nothing or the lock alone. The one collision this plan carries is the specify's: `/health/ready` is stated under `start` (REQ-015, REQ-016) and under `health` (REQ-037–041), both slices mount it, the smoke test panics at `app::router` in wave 2, and wave 2's verify removes one copy as the second card's did, through `edit_files` where the change is within a file, sealing a module of `start`'s or `health`'s and a test. The one risk the second card's M2 left stands: `SLICE-005` and `SLICE-006` both create `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` in wave 4 with the layout's fixed dev-dependency block, so the two creations differ under `[dependencies]` alone, and a duplicate key there, repaired by wave 4's verify, is the stem-crate seeding follow-up's figure and no finding against this card's items.
- **C5, no stray.** 0 files outside the layout at the label, where the second card had `.probe/Cargo.toml`; a turn that laid a stray removed it through `delete` after its `find -newer`; the first slice reads `omnia-api.md` through `read_doc` and runs no `strings`, `nm`, or `find` over the registry sources, where the subject is readable.
- **C6, the seals.** The envelope's `sealed` names each wave's paths, the runner's wave lines read `(lock only)` or the files from it, and `repaired` names wave 2 and no wave whose seal is the lock alone.
- **O1–O5, re-read.** O1: 7/7 in 5 waves, no conflict, the lists merging clean with C2 behind it. O2: the host's three checks agree with every verdict and `cargo test` passes every test. O3: the two crates.io hosts alone, no completion `inactive`. O4 with this card's bands: the first slice 180–600 s, its root files no longer its to write (Card C's first slice laid them and its own crate in 159 s); the other build turns 120–500 s; a verify that repairs nothing 80–140 s with its shell part 70–95 s; wave 2's repairing verify under 300 s; the run under 2700 s. O5: 80–92% of scenarios named with 86 the comparison, `covered` 89/89, `unreached` 0, and 0 probe routes, since `bootstrap-lifecycle` and the body-limit, request-log, and authentication requirements state no route and are functions under the layout now.
- **Observed, never decided on:** the tokens against 36.3 M and 440 K; the `edit_files` count per turn; a verify's writes line; whether the verifies' `find -newer` ran; the kernel's denials against the second card's; disk.

### Decision rule

- **The amendments are confirmed** when C1 and C2 hold with O1 and O2: the first slice writes nothing of the root files and the nine stand at every commit; every list line is appended and `unreached` is 0; every slice merged in the plan's waves with no conflict; the host agrees with every verdict. This card is then the baseline a later omnia card reads against, and its bands replace the second card's.
- **C1 failing** is read by its shape: a `seeded` count other than eight, or a root file among the first slice's `write_files` paths, is the SDK's seed or its brief, emery's; a root file differing at a later slice commit is `build.md`'s root-files bullet, the slice named.
- **C2 failing** is read as M3 was: a list written whole through `write_files` is `build.md`'s list rule, the slice named; `unreached` above 0 is the verify's mount check, run and missed or not run.
- **C3 failing**: an empty write accepted is the SDK's guard, emery's; a refusal answered by a `shrink: true` rewrite of the same size is the model's, read for what it then wrote; a build turn with no `edit_files` whose list lines nonetheless appear is the brief's or the tool description's wording.
- **C4**: a repair at wave 3 or 4 removing a route of `start`'s under another stem's prefix refutes the wiring section as written, the layout's finding; wave 2's readiness repair is the specify's, recorded for the grouping arm and no finding here; a duplicate under `[dependencies]` of `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` is the stem-crate follow-up's; a duplicate under the dev-dependency block is the layout's fixed-block wording, a finding.
- **C5 failing**: a file outside the layout at the label is `build.md`'s loop step, read from the turn's `delete` calls, made or not.
- **C6 failing** is the engine's `sealed` where it disagrees with the chain, emery's, or the runner's reading where the two agree and the notes do not, this tree's.
- **O4 outside its band** is read from the turn's shell part and its writes line, the model's part or the compile; **O5 under 80%** per stem, the Rust reader's or the tree's.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay's upstream ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided before anything above; a verify put again on `bad_gateway` that then lands is the engine's retry doing its work, recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against the second card's row.** Same plan, knobs, ladder, model, and boundary: a difference is the adapter's, the SDK's, or the engine's, told apart by the shape above; what both cards show is the layout's, the specify's, or the toolchain's.

The seeded-greenfield arm the plan put before this card was never run, and its question is answered here by the seed's own signature.

## The `start` translation

The confirmation card's `start` is two slices, `bootstrap-api` and `bootstrap-lifecycle`, 41 of the plan's 100 scenarios, and `SLICE-002` laid `src/lifecycle.rs`; the first live build of a real tree (`r9k`, 2026-10-13, no card) was handed a `start` of 25 requirements — the clients, the secret retriever, APM, the listener, the status routes — and laid 1,655 lines under `crates/start/` against the guardrail as it stood, which said what a module never does and left the slice to work out what to lay for a requirement that states a process. Three things change under this round, none of them the plan. The target's prose (this tree's `RELEASES.md` 0.13.0, "`omnia` translates a process's `start`"): `guardrails.md` gains "What `start` becomes", a table from what the source did at start-up to what the slice lays and how the requirement reports — a setting a catalog constant read at use, a secret retriever and a client nothing under `start`, listening and monitoring nothing laid and covered, signals and a lifecycle type nothing laid and uncovered, a stated health route and a cross-cutting `pub fn` the two things it writes — linked from `build.md`'s `start` bullet; `verify.md` gains check 3, a `grep` over `crates/start/src` for a lifecycle (`fn start|fn stop|fn listen|struct Main|started: bool|tokio::spawn|loop {`) that prints nothing, a hit removed through `delete` with its `pub mod` line and test rather than renamed. The contract (emery `RELEASES.md` 0.38.0, "A target adapter declares the base it builds over"): `omnia` and `node` declare `Base::Fresh`, and a base whose root holds a directory outside `.git` and `.emery` is refused `base-not-fresh` before any slice — the greenfield `target.git` the runner lays passes, and the `r9k` build, run over the `emery-adapters` checkout itself and landing `adapters/`, `evals/`, and `examples/` in its label, is the run it refuses. And the engine's slicing (the [plan widths](plan-widths.md#the-dependency-rule) round): the build-dependency definition and `SLICE_CAP` 12, which reach a fresh `specify` alone.

Two arms, so the prose is read apart from the plan. **Arm A** is the protocol's `--rebuild`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-13T01-23-50Z --target omnia express-orders`, the confirmation card's plan (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, `1/2/1/2/1`), knobs, ladder, and model, over the component built from this tree and a release `emery` built from the engine working tree; what differs from the confirmation card's row is the `start` table, check 3, and the fresh-base check, and the plan's variance is out. **Arm B** is a fresh `specify` and build, `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --build --target omnia express-orders`, where the dependency rule, the cap, and the grouping arm's two lines all reach the plan, read against Arm A for the build and against the confirmation card for the waves. The predictions and the rule were written before either was built; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **S1, the lifecycle, Arm A.** At the label `crates/start/src` holds no match for check 3's pattern: `SLICE-002` (`bootstrap-lifecycle`) lays no `lifecycle.rs`, or lays one and wave 2's verify removes it through `delete` with its `pub mod` line and test, the seal naming the three paths. Lines under `crates/start/src` at the label fall to **≤ 60%** of the confirmation card's count at its label (read from the staged tree, `wc -l` over the directory on both).
- **S2, the coverage, Arm A.** `covered` falls from 89/89 to **78–86 of 89**, every uncovered id under `start`: the requirements that state a listener, a shutdown, a readiness *flag*, a connection kept, or an APM agent, left out as the table's last column says, never reported covered over a stub. `covered` for the other stems stays 48/48. `start`'s scenarios named falls with it, 25–35 of 41, the others as the confirmation card's.
- **S3, the base.** Both arms' builds run — the greenfield's root holds `.gitignore` and `README.md` alone — with no `base-not-fresh` in any log; the refused shape is asserted in the engine's suite and not run here.
- **S4, the settings and the route.** `start` reads its settings through `Config::get` under constants after `ENV` in the catalog, no settings struct and no load at start; `authenticate` stays a `pub fn` called at the top of `customers`' and `orders`' handlers; the `/health/live` route stays under `start` as the plan states it and the one probe (`/health/ready` under `health`) recurs on Arm A, the specify's, as the confirmation card read it.
- **S5, the waves, Arm B.** The fresh plan is 4–7 slices in **2–3 waves**, widest ≥ 3, as the plan widths round predicts for this case; the `start` stem is one slice or two of fewer requirements than 41 scenarios' worth where the grouping arm's lines take `/health/ready` to `health` alone; wave 2's seal is empty and the probes 0 where they do, the grouping arm's two predictions read here as the confirmation card asked. The build then runs **under 1500 s** against 1895, since a wave of three builds at once.
- **O1–O5, both arms.** Every slice merged in the plan's waves with no conflict; the host's three checks agree with every verdict; the two crates.io hosts alone, nothing `inactive`; build turns 120–500 s, the first 180–600, clean verifies 80–140 s with shell parts 70–95 and a repairing verify under 300; `unreached` 0, 0 files outside the layout, `write_files` under 15 calls and 0 rewrites over the build turns.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens against 13.4 M and 176 K; the `grep` of check 3 in each verify's shell part; whether a `start` turn reads `guardrails.md` through `read_doc`; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The translation holds** when S1 and S2 hold on Arm A with O1 and O2: no lifecycle under `start` at the label, lines down, `covered` down by the lifecycle requirements alone and the other stems' whole, every slice merged, the host agreeing. This card's `start` figures are then the band a later omnia card reads against.
- **S1 failing by shape.** A match at the label with no wave-2 repair is check 3 not run or not read — the verify's shell part names the `grep` or does not; a match repaired by rename (`fn start` → `fn initialise`) is the check's wording, a finding against the repair paragraph; a module removed and laid again by a later slice is the build prompt's `start` bullet, the slice named.
- **S2 failing by shape.** `covered` 89/89 with S1 holding is the report rule — the turn covered over nothing laid, which the table's last column forbids — a finding against the table's wording; `covered` under 78 with ids outside `start` is a slice reading the table as its own, the build bullet's placement.
- **S3 failing** is the engine's: a `base-not-fresh` over the greenfield is `Files::list` or the check reading `.gitignore` and `README.md` wrongly, emery's, decided before anything above.
- **S5** is read in the plan widths round first and here for the build's wall time alone; a fresh plan of one wave per slice is that round's "the rule does nothing", and this card's S5 is void.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a verify put again on `bad_gateway` that then lands is the engine's retry, recorded as the attempts.
- **Read Arm A against the confirmation card's row**, Arm B against Arm A's: same plan on A, so a difference is the prose's or the contract's; a difference B shows that A does not is the plan's, the specify's three changes together, told apart by the plan widths round's reading of the plan.

Not yet run. The round is the operator's; each arm's card, the `start` tree at its label, the `sealed` and `covered` of its envelope, and `run-1.fidelity.md` are read into this section when it has.

**Arm B of the business-specification round** ([business-spec.md](business-spec.md#arm-b--the-build-after-the-arm-holds)) reads this round's S1 and S2 bands from the other side: once the specification states no requirement under `start` — the credential, the prefetch, the clients, the status log, and the exit timers the design's — the translation's table reduces to its first row and its last, a catalog constant read at use and a cross-cutting `pub fn`, so `crates/start/src` at the label is predicted under 60 lines and `covered` whole, with nothing under `start` left to leave out. A `start` that still lays a lifecycle there is check 3's finding as here; one that lays the clients is a slice reading the design's `apis` section as its own requirements, the build prompt's `start` bullet.

## The composed layout

Every omnia card so far built the registration layout: a route was a constant in `crates/common/src/routes/http.rs`, a statement in `src/routes.rs` naming the handler through its stem, an entry in the rung's table in `tests/routes.rs`, and a setting a constant in the catalog `crates/common/src/config.rs`, eleven list files in all, and the adapter held the tree to the wiring the compiler could not see through three readers of its own, `unwired_tables`, `unlisted_handlers`, `unread_settings`, each a reader of the eval's own failure modes rather than of Rust. The [confirmation card](#the-confirmation-card)'s findings named the shape: the layout's layer-shaped rule and one row of `handlers.md`. This round replaces the shape rather than tune it (this tree's `RELEASES.md` 0.13.0, "Composition replaces registration", and the exemplar's `templates/guest` at the commit `Cargo.lock` pins): a stem is a crate exposing `pub fn router<P: Capabilities>() -> axum::Router<Client<P>>` and `pub fn topics<P: Capabilities>(router: messaging::Router<P>, env: &str) -> messaging::Router<P>`, each a fold of `common` over one entry per part, `common::routers([..])` and `common::topics(router, env).parts([..])`, laid whichever parts the stem has; a part declares its paths, settings, and state keys as `pub(crate)` constants, its handlers `pub(crate)`, and `pub(crate) fn routes()` / `topics(..)` registering them; the root lists hold one statement per stem, `router.merge(<stem>::router())` and `<stem>::topics(router, &env)`; the two rungs are static, one test each that the composed router builds and refuses an unknown path or topic; the topic table alone stays in `common`, as `crates/common/src/topic.rs`; and a stem's tests drive its `router()` with `oneshot` and its `topics(..)` with a delivery. The merge rules fall from eleven paths to seven (`Cargo.lock` ours; `Cargo.toml`, `src/routes.rs`, `src/messaging.rs`, `crates/common/src/topic.rs`, `crates/*/Cargo.toml`, `crates/*/src/lib.rs` union); the three wiring readers are gone, since a handler no `routes()` names and a part no entry lists are dead code the forbidden `dead_code` lint refuses and a route or topic two stems claim panics as the rung builds; two readers stay for what the compiler never sees, `stems_merged` (a stem the root lists do not name compiles clean and serves nothing) and `unnamed_topics`; and the host boundary moved into the template's `clippy.toml` under the earlier PR, `disallowed-methods`, `-types`, `-macros` at `forbid`, so `simulated_host`'s spelling list is a `process` reader of `crates/start/` and the lifecycle names alone. The eval's reference reader (`evals/src/reference.rs`, `Shape::read`) now reads the topics from `crates/common/src/topic.rs` and the settings from every `const NAME: &str = "NAME";` under `crates/*/src`, beside the catalog an older tree or the reference keeps.

Two arms. **Arm A** is the protocol's `--rebuild`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-13T01-23-50Z --target omnia express-orders`, the confirmation card's plan (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, `1/2/1/2/1`), knobs, ladder, and model, over the component built from this tree and the release `emery` the [`start` translation](#the-start-translation) names, whose S1–S5 are read on this same card, since the command is the same and that round has not run; what this round reads is the layout. **Arm B** is `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --build --target omnia train`, the one case with a `reference`, for the reference comparison under the new reader. The predictions and the rule were written before either was built; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **L1, the merge, Arm A.** Every slice merged in the plan's five waves with no conflict and no `resolved` path, as every omnia card so far; and in a wave holding two slices of one stem, both lay `crates/<stem>/src/lib.rs` whole and the union keeps one text: at the label every stem's entry holds **exactly one** `pub fn router` and **exactly one** `pub fn topics`, each array's comment once, every `pub mod` once. A conflict, or an entry with two of either, is read under the rule below.
- **L2, the wiring is the compiler's, Arm A.** At the label the host's `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` exits 0 over the export with **no `allow(` or `expect(`** under `crates/*/src` (`rg -c 'allow\(|expect\(' crates/*/src` 0, the lints being `forbid`), so every handler is named by a `routes()` or `topics(..)` and every part by its entry; `src/routes.rs` holds one `merge(<stem>::router())` and `src/messaging.rs` one `<stem>::topics(router, &env)` per crate under `crates/` other than `common`, however few routes or topics the stem has; `crates/common/src/config.rs`, `crates/common/src/lib.rs`, `tests/routes.rs`, and `tests/messaging.rs` are byte for byte the template's; `crates/common/src/topic.rs` holds no constant no module names.
- **L3, the turns, Arm A.** Build turns within the confirmation card's band, 120–600 s, with fewer writes: `edit_files` calls per build turn **≤ 2** and edits per slice **≤ 7** (the entry's two or three lists, the root manifest's two, the two root lists, the topic table), against the registration layout's three files per route; `write_files` under 15 calls and 0 rewrites over the build turns, as the earlier bands have it. Correction rounds over the build turns **≤ 2 in total**, and `stems_merged` the finding where one occurs, since it is the rule the model has not met before; `unnamed_topics` 0.
- **L4, the tests, Arm A.** No stem's test calls a handler: `rg -c 'Client::call' crates/*/tests` 0, every stem with a route driving `<stem>::router()` through `oneshot` and every stem with a topic driving `<stem>::topics(..)` through `handle`; `scenarios named` **≥ 80%** of the merged slices' scenarios, the confirmation card's 86 of 100 the figure read against, and the `probes` 1 as that card read it, the specify's.
- **L5, the verdicts, Arm A.** The host's three checks agree with every verdict; every verify's static drift finding 0, since no slice edits a static file and no check rewrites one; a wave's seal, where any, `Cargo.lock` and a repair alone.
- **L6, the reference, Arm B.** `run-1.fidelity.md`'s `## Reference comparison` reads the built tree under the new shape: `config keys` on the built side **≥ 4** and the keys the fixtures seed among them (`GTFS_URL`, `BLOCK_MGT_IDENTITY`, `STATION_FILTER`, the source's names or the slice's own, as `config_seed` reads them), `topics` ≥ 2, and the oracle consuming one inbound topic the built `topics(..)` registered under `dev-`; the fixture outcomes are the slice's and read as a baseline for the shape, not decided on.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens against the confirmation card's 13.4 M and 176 K; the wall time against 1895 s; which of a wave's two slices of one stem laid the entry first and what the second's `edit_files` found; whether a build turn reads `layout.md` or `testing.md` through `read_doc`; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The layout holds** when L1, L2, and L5 hold: every slice merged with no conflict, one entry text per stem at the label, the wiring the compiler's with no attribute lifting it, the static files standing, and the host agreeing. This card's figures are then the band a later omnia card reads against, and the registration layout's cards are history.
- **L1 failing by shape.** A conflict at `crates/<stem>/src/lib.rs` is read from the two sides the merge turn was handed: a doc line or a `use` line differing is the layout's one-text rule, `layout.md`'s "The stem's entry" and `build.md`'s entry bullet, the slice that varied it named; an array comment differing is the anchor's wording in the layout's table. An entry with two `router()` functions and no conflict is the union keeping two forms the model wrote differently, the same rule, read the same way. A conflict at a part's module is a slice writing another's, the wiring rule, as before.
- **L2 failing by shape.** An `allow(` or `expect(` under `crates/*/src` is clippy refusing it at the slice's own check, so its presence at the label is check 1 not run or not read, the build turn's shell part naming it or not; a stem the root lists do not name at the label is `stems_merged` not applied, the adapter's, since the rule holds both turns; a static file differing at the label is `static_drift` not applied at the verify, the adapter's, decided before anything of the prose.
- **L3 failing** is read from the turns' tool-call lines: edits beyond the lists are a slice rewriting a line it found, the build bullet's "change no other"; a correction round beyond two is the finding's wording, the rule named by its text.
- **L4 failing** is `testing.md`: a `Client::call` in a stem's tests is the handler made `pub` to reach it, read beside `handlers.md`'s visibility bullet, a finding against the two documents' wording, never the layout's.
- **L6 failing by shape.** `config keys` of `ENV` alone on the built side while `crates/*/src` holds `const X: &str = "X"` lines is `setting_consts`, the harness's, fixed and the card re-read without a run; no inbound topic consumed is the oracle's candidates against the built `topics(..)`, read from `oracle-none`'s list before any reading of the slice.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a verify put again on `bad_gateway` that then lands is the engine's retry, recorded as the attempts.
- **Read Arm A against the confirmation card's row**, same plan, so a difference is the layout's, the prose's, or the `start` translation's three changes, told apart by the [`start` translation](#decision-rule-4)'s rule first, since its S1–S5 are read on the same card; Arm B stands alone as the first reading of the reference under the new shape.
