## The train reference round

The first `train` build under the composed layout was a manual run, `cargo run -- build --config examples/emery.toml` over the shipped binary, revision `6242de6a`, one `train-update` stem in two slices, landed in `.emery/vcs/repos/omnia` and read against `evals/cases/train/reference` by hand rather than by a card. The reference oracle passes 0 of its 22 fixtures over it and stops at `oracle-none`: the stem's entry holds an empty `topics` array, `crates/common/src/topic.rs` holds the outbound constant alone, and no delivery reaches the handler. Behind that, five defects that every fixture would still fail on: `haEntrado` and `haSalido` typed `i32` where the wire spells `true` and `false`; the delay computed `now − midnight + seconds` where the source and the reference compute `now − (midnight + seconds)`, the sign on the event's seconds inverted; validation refusals answered `Ok(())` at the handler, so the ten refusal fixtures would read as processed; `DATE_FORMAT` and `TIMEZONE` read through `Config::get` and the date parsed as a `NaiveDateTime`; and both the even and the odd train id queried against `/allocations/trips`, the labels joined and sorted, where `evenTrainId || oddTrainId` is one identifier. Friday's card over the same case (`2026-10-15T17-41-37Z`, 12 of 22, consumed under `dev-realtime-train.v1`, the event fixtures failing on `duplicate field pasoTren` and one on the delay's sign) is not read against: it built the registration layout, under the expectation before the business-specification round moved it, under `auto` where this round pins `composer-2.5`; its one bearing is that the trigger was laid then and not now, since the requirements that stated the subscription are `[[absent]]` now and `build.md`'s wiring bullet leaned on them.

Four parts answer it, none of them the plan. The adapter (this tree, Part A): three rules of the tree the turns repair, `unreached_publishers` (a stem that publishes and no route, topic, or other stem reaches, the finding naming the trigger the design's inbound stream bullet spells), `swallowed_rejections` (an `Ok(())` answering a refusal a `let .. else`, an `Err` arm, or an `is_err` branch caught), and `format_settings` (a setting whose name spells `FORMAT`); `build.md` defines when a requirement states a topic — the design's `## APIs and integrations` lists an inbound stream whose bullet cites it or whose deliveries its scenario describes, never a requirement that says `subscribe` — and has the slice that ends the pipeline register the consumer where no part has; `translation.md`'s row 20 names the `let Ok(..) = .. else { return Ok(()) }` as the catch it refuses, its worked example types a `boolean` element `bool`, keeps every field on the element that carries it, parses the date under a `const` of the parser's syntax, groups the delay as the sum subtracted whole, and queries one identifier; its rule reads `configured` for where the documents put the value. The `typescript` extraction (Part B): a setting the bootstrap refuses to run without is one it reads itself from the environment, never a retriever's throw; the guard after a catch is the catch's boundary; a formula keeps its grouping and a field one noun, `a || b` is a precedence, and `configured` is said of a `process.env` value alone. The engine's prose (Part C, emery): the spec-draft keeps a formula's grouping and one noun per field, the design spells an upstream's base URL by its setting key and names on an inbound stream's bullet the requirements its deliveries set off. The eval's measure is unchanged.

**The baseline is Arm B of [the composed layout](#the-composed-layout), at the tree before Parts A–C.** `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --build --target omnia train` under `CURSOR_MODEL=composer-2.5` is that round's Arm B and the business-specification round's, and neither has run; run once before the parts land, it gives both their result and this round a baseline on protocol. The manual run is its pre-protocol reading — same model, same tree, one run, no card — recorded above as such. Landing the parts first puts the composed layout and this round's changes on one card where they cannot be told apart; that trade-off is the operator's, and the results record which was taken. **The arm** is the same command after the parts land, one run as every omnia build arm is, its `run-1.fidelity.md` read against the baseline's. The predictions and the rule were written before either was built; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **T1, the trigger.** At the label `crates/common/src/topic.rs` holds **two** constants, the inbound `realtime-train.v1` and the outbound `realtime-train-positions.v1`, the design's two stream bullets less their `dev-` environment, and the `train-update` entry's `topics` array holds **one** `<part>::topics,` entry; `run-1.oracle.md` reads `consumed under dev-realtime-train.v1` and never `oracle-none`.
- **T2, the refusals.** `static/0006`–`0010`, `replay/0001`, and `replay/0048` land as `pass`, each refusal an `Err` out of the handler; at the label no `Ok(())` stands in a `let .. else` block, an `Err(..) =>` arm, or an `is_err()` branch of the stem's modules (the check's own pattern, `rg` by hand on the staged tree), and `swallowed_rejections` reports 0 at every verify.
- **T3, the allocations.** One `/allocations/trips` request per delivery that reaches it (the oracle's recorded requests), its `externalRefId` the even train id where that is non-empty, else the odd; the `fail` reasons, where any, name no sorted or joined labels.
- **T4, the settings.** No `const` under `crates/*/src` whose name spells `FORMAT`; the `config keys` row's built side holds **≤ 8** keys — `ENV`, the stations, the two upstream URLs, the identity, the two delay bounds the source reads from `process.env`, and at most a zone — with no `DATE_FORMAT` among them, against the manual run's nine.
- **T5, the checks.** Over the build turns `unreached_publishers`, `swallowed_rejections`, and `format_settings` report **≤ 1** finding each, since the prose carries each rule before the check reads for it, and **0** at the verifies; the correction rounds over the build turns ≤ 2 in total, as the composed layout's L3 has them.
- **T6, the wire.** `haEntrado` and `haSalido` land as `bool`; no fixture fails at the parse (`duplicate field`, `invalid type`): `static/0001`, `static/0002`, and the replay event fixtures fail, where they fail, past it, on the events published.
- **Observed, never decided on:** fixtures passed, read as a band between the manual run's 0 of 22 and Friday's 12; the build's wall time and tokens; whether a build turn reads `translation.md` through `read_doc`; the kernel's denials. The specification-side columns — no `start` requirement, `absent hits` 0 — are the business-specification round's [P1 and P2](business-spec.md#predictions), read there and not re-predicted here.

### Decision rule

- **The round holds** when T1, T2, and T4 hold with every slice merged, the host's three checks agreeing with every verdict, and T5 within its figure: the trigger is read from the design, a refusal is an `Err`, and a format is a constant. T3 and T6 are then read as the first band for the fixtures past the trigger, and the fixtures passed become the figure the next omnia card over `train` reads against.
- **`oracle-none`** is A1 and A2, read from the build turns' findings first: no `unreached_publishers` finding on the publishing slice's turn is the check not reading the tree — the `Publish` spelled some other way, or the stem reached by a `<stem>::` the check credits — the adapter's; a finding answered without the registration is the header's wording; a registration under a topic the design's bullet does not spell is `design-format.md`'s stream bullet, the engine's, read from `design.md` by hand.
- **A refusal fixture failing** is `swallowed_rejections` and row 20: an `Ok(())` at the label inside the check's pattern is the check not applied, the adapter's; one outside it — a `match` on a boolean the validation answered, an `Option` the refusal became — is the pattern's reach, the shape named and the check widened; a refusal with the wrong reason code is the specification's, read in the business-specification round.
- **A `FORMAT` constant** at the label is `format_settings` not applied, the adapter's; a `TIMEZONE` setting is within the rule where neither `spec.md` nor `design.md` spells the zone, and the `configured` rule's finding where one does.
- **Two allocation requests** is item 5 and Part B's precedence sentence, told apart by `spec.md`: `the even train id, or the odd where the even is empty` is the build's, item 5; `the even or odd train id` is the extraction's, the sentence, and the build read as given.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a verify put again on `bad_gateway` that then lands is the engine's retry, recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against the baseline's `run-1.fidelity.md`** where the baseline ran, so a difference is the four parts'; where it did not, against the manual run's reading above, and the composed layout's Arm B is recorded as read on this same card.

### Results

Not yet run. The round is the operator's; the baseline's and the arm's cards, `topic.rs` and the stem's entry at the label, the build turns' findings, and `run-1.fidelity.md`'s reference comparison and oracle table are read into this section when it has.

## The scaffolded slice

Every omnia card so far had the model lay each slice's files whole — the stem's manifest, its entry, the part with its constants, `routes()`, handlers, and types, the test file, and the stem's lines in the seeded lists — from the prose's description of the layout, and the adapter read the tree back for what the model got wrong: the [confirmation card](#the-confirmation-card)'s wave 2 seal and probe, the [composed layout](#the-composed-layout)'s L1 and L3, and the three readers `split_stems`, `stem_lints`, and `stems_merged` are the cost of that reading, an entry two slices spelled differently, a manifest without its `[lints]`, a crate laid under a stem's head. This round moves the layout out of the turn (this tree's `RELEASES.md` 0.13.0, "`omnia` lays each slice's skeleton", over emery 0.38.0's typed slice): the engine lowers each requirement typed — id, subject, statement, scenarios — beside the owned design types' signatures and the plan entry's brief, and `Scaffold::plan` / `lay` in `adapters/omnia/src/scaffold.rs` lays, before the turn and with no model, the stem's manifest (the layout's one text), its entry (`pub mod <part>;` and `<part>::routes(),` in its lists, or those two lines added to an entry the tree holds), the part (`//!` from the brief, one `pub(crate) const` per path, `routes()` over them, one `pub struct <Name> {}` per owned type with its signature fenced in the doc, one handler per route over `<Name>Input` / `<Name>Output` with `let _ = (input, context);` over `todo!("REQ-012 orders.pay: the handler")`, a `pub fn` over `todo!("REQ-012 orders.sum: the behaviour")` for a requirement stating no route), the test file (one `#[tokio::test]` per scenario under `#[ignore = "REQ-012 Balance settled: unfilled"]` over `todo!("REQ-012 Balance settled: arrange, act, assert")`), and the stem's lines in the root manifest's two lists, `src/routes.rs`, and `src/messaging.rs` at the layout's anchors; the route is read from the statement, then each scenario's `when`, `:id` as `{id}`. The brief's `What the adapter laid for this slice before this turn:` section names each file as laid or held and the markers in fill order, and the turn fills them through `edit_files`; two checks of the turn hold the fill — `markers_left`, a `todo!("REQ-..")` of a covered requirement standing, and `scenarios_tested`, a covered requirement's scenario whose test is missing, ignored, or holds its marker — and `split_stems` holds every crate the turn lays to the slice's stems. `build.md` lost its file-shape sections for the markers; `layout.md`'s intros, `handlers.md`, and `testing.md` say the adapter lays what they describe. Topics are not derived: a part that consumes one still writes its `topics(..)`, the entry's `<part>::topics,` line, and the table's constant as before.

One arm, the protocol's `--rebuild`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-13T01-23-50Z --target omnia express-orders`, the confirmation card's plan (`9ba1cdba`, seven slices in five waves, `1/2/1/2/1`), knobs, ladder, and model, over the component built from this tree and a release `emery` built from the engine at its typed slice, whose `start` translation S1–S5 and composed layout L1–L5 are read on this same card where those rounds have not run, since the command is theirs; what this round reads is the scaffold. The predictions and the rule were written before the component was built; the run is the operator's.

### Predictions

- **K1, the skeleton is the adapter's.** Every build turn's brief carries the laid section, and at the label every stem's manifest and entry are the scaffold's text less the lines the turns added — every `crates/*/Cargo.toml` holds `[lints]\nworkspace = true` and the fixed dev-dependency block, every entry **exactly one** `pub fn router` and **exactly one** `pub fn topics` with each array's comment once, as L1 has it — and `split_stems`, `stem_lints`, and `stems_merged` report **0** over the build turns, since the adapter laid what they read for.
- **K2, the markers are filled.** At the label `rg -c 'todo!\("REQ-' crates/*/src crates/*/tests` and `rg -c '#\[ignore = "REQ-' crates/*/tests` count **0 over the covered requirements** — a marker standing belongs to a requirement its slice's report left out of `covered`, read against the envelope — and `rg -c 'let _ = \(input, context\);' crates/*/src` is **0**; `markers_left` and `scenarios_tested` report **≤ 1 finding each over the build turns** and 0 at the verifies, since the brief lists every marker before the check reads for it.
- **K3, the turns.** Build turns within the confirmation card's band, 120–600 s, with the writes the skeleton left: `write_files` calls per build turn **≤ 1** and `edit_files` calls **≤ 3**, no rewrite of a file the adapter laid (`shrink` set nowhere), and the files a build turn names in `written` the part, its tests, and the lists the slice's topic touched, never a stem's manifest or entry it found laid; correction rounds over the build turns **≤ 2 in total**.
- **K4, the tests.** Every scenario of a covered requirement is a test at the label, run and named for the scenario: `scenarios named` **≥ 95%** of the merged slices' scenarios, against the confirmation card's 86 of 100 and L4's 80%, since the adapter names each from the scenario and the turn fills rather than invents; `rg -c 'Client::call' crates/*/tests` 0, as L4 has it.
- **K5, the verdicts.** The host's checks agree with every verdict, every verify's static drift finding 0, a wave's seal `Cargo.lock` and a repair alone, as L5 has it; no `#[ignore]` line stands on a covered requirement's test at the label, since `scenarios_tested` holds it at the slice.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens and wall time against the confirmation card's 13.4 M, 176 K, and 1895 s, since the brief grew by the laid section and the turn's writes shrank; whether a handler's `<Name>Input` kept the laid `String` of a path parameter or took the wire's shape; how many requirements the route heuristic routed against the plan's statements, read from the laid constants; whether a turn read `layout.md` through `read_doc` at all, now that the skeleton stands; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The scaffold holds** when K1, K2, and K5 hold: the skeleton the adapter laid stands at the label, every covered requirement's marker filled and its scenarios run, the host agreeing. This card's figures are then the band a later omnia card reads against, and K3's and K4's figures the first band for a turn that fills rather than lays.
- **K1 failing by shape.** A manifest or an entry differing from the scaffold's text at the label is a turn rewriting a laid file, read from the turn's tool-call lines: a `write_files` with `shrink` over `crates/<stem>/src/lib.rs` or `Cargo.toml` is `build.md`'s "change no other" bullet, the prose's; an `edit_files` beyond the lists is the entry's one-text rule in `layout.md`. Two `router()` functions in one entry is the union keeping two forms, which the scaffold makes impossible where both slices find the entry laid, so it is read as the second slice laying the entry whole, `Scaffold::lay` not finding the file, the adapter's.
- **K2 failing by shape.** A marker standing on a covered requirement at the label is `markers_left` not applied, the adapter's, since the rule holds at the slice; a `let _ = (input, context);` line standing in a filled handler is `build.md`'s handler bullet and the brief's marker line, the prose's; an ignored test on a covered requirement is `scenarios_tested` not applied where its name is the laid one, and the naming rule where the turn renamed the test, read from the test file against the brief's marker list.
- **K3 failing** is read from the turns' tool-call lines: a `write_files` over a file the adapter laid is the turn laying what it was told stands, the brief's wording; a correction round beyond two is the finding's text, the rule named by it.
- **K4 failing** is the runner's reader first — a test renamed from the scenario the reader does not match — then `testing.md`'s fill bullet; a `Client::call` is read as L4 has it.
- **A route the heuristic missed** — a `pub fn` marker laid for a requirement whose scenario spells the route in a `then` or a `given` — is observed and recorded with the requirement, not decided on, since the heuristic reads the statement and the `when` alone, the slice carrying no typed route; the turn is expected to give the function the route itself, and the handler it writes is read under K2 as any.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a verify put again on `bad_gateway` that then lands is the engine's retry, recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against the confirmation card's row**, same plan, so a difference is the scaffold's, the composed layout's, or the `start` translation's, told apart by those rounds' rules first where their S1–S5 and L1–L5 are read on this same card.

### Results

**The plan moved, and the arm became three runs.** The round named `--rebuild 2026-10-13T01-23-50Z`, but that card's revision `9ba1cdba` is stamped at the engine's grammar 5 and the engine at its typed slice reads 6 (emery `#356`, `Role` on `Block::Type`), so a rebuild is refused `spec-outdated` before any load, and a revision is content-addressed and cannot be re-stamped. The control is therefore a fresh `--build` under the tree before the scaffold (`9365204`, the last commit before it, compiled against the same SDK through a path patch), card `2026-10-18T22-15-56Z`: revision `20dad6b9`, 63 requirements, 82 scenarios, seven slices in five waves `2/2/1/1/1` (`start-foundation` and `health`; `start-errors-and-listen` and `customers`; `orders-reading`; `orders-placement`; `orders-updates`), built 7/7 and verified 5/5 in 1888 s, `covered` 63/63, the host `ok 88/88`, scenarios named 80/82 (97%) · 6 outside · 0 unreached · 13 probes, 33.4 M input tokens and 239 K output over 12 completions, wave 1's verify repairing `crates/foundation/src/start_foundation.rs`. **The off arm's row.** 7/7 merged in the plan's five waves with no conflict and no `resolved` path, 5/5 verified, `covered` 63/63, the host `ok 86/86` and agreeing with every verdict, scenarios named 80/82 (97%) · 6 outside · 0 unreached · 2 probes; no candidate rejected at any turn, every build completion `ok`, 0 correction rounds; 18.2 M input tokens (17.6 M cached) and 171 K output over 13 completions, against the control's 33.4 M and 239 K and the confirmation card's 13.4 M and 176 K; 2905 s, of which 1203 s is `build-SLICE-006`'s first attempt, timed out with the shell holding it — its `cargo clippy -p orders --all-targets -- -D warnings` never returned, `tool_ms` 1,089,887 of the 1,203,416 — and the engine's retry in a fresh copy landed in 237 s, the toolchain's and the backend's, decided first and carried by the retry; the critical path less that attempt is under the control's 1888 s. **K1 holds in shape.** Every `crates/*/Cargo.toml` at the label holds `[lints]\nworkspace = true` and the fixed dev-dependency block, the three scaffolded manifests with the scaffold's two comment lines and `foundation`'s, the model's for a `start` slice, without them; every entry exactly one `pub fn router` and one `pub fn topics`, each array's comment once, every `pub mod` once; `stem_lints` and `stems_merged` 0 over the build turns, and `split_stems` not read, being off. **K2 holds by its counts and fails by what they count.** At the label `todo!("REQ-` 0, `#[ignore = "REQ-` 0, `let _ = (input, context);` 0 over `crates/*/src` and `crates/*/tests`; `markers_left` and `scenarios_tested` 0 findings over the build turns and 0 at the verifies. The `cargo clippy` that never returned is one attempt of fourteen.

**Decision — the scaffold does not hold as built, and the control card is the band.** K1 and K5 hold, K2 by count and K4 at the control's figure, and K3 fails outright, but the finding that decides the round sits under K2: with `Requirement.route` `None` until specify lowers it and the statement by role under the spec gate, the text heuristic can route nothing of this specification, so the scaffold laid a behaviour function for every requirement and the turn, told to fill the markers, filled functions — the API's routes among what it did not lay, and the tree the gates passed serves none of them. Three findings travel, none to the prose first. The route must be typed: specify lowering `route` and `topic` onto the requirement, or the scaffold reading the surface's spelling from the subject's second segment (`health.get-ready`, `orders.post-id-pay`, `customers.put-id-tier`, which the survey made from `GET /health/ready`, `POST /orders/:id/pay`, `PUT /customers/:id/tier`) and the mount from the design's `## APIs and integrations`, since the statement will not spell it again — the engine's or the adapter's, and this round's figure for the handler marker is not read until it does. `split_stems` over a `start` slice is the adapter's: an empty stem set refuses every crate and prints an empty list, Round A's failure, and what a `start` slice lays under an invented crate name (`foundation` in both arms, `http` and `start` in Round A) is the `start` translation's open question, not the scaffold's. And the `write_files` the canvas deferred — "a restriction only if it fails" — failed: a `write_files` over a path the scaffold laid is a finding of the build turn's `check`, or the tool withheld for those paths, and the brief says so. The reader's hyphen is the runner's. The off arm is not a shipped tree, so its figures are not a band; the next card over revision `20dad6b9` reads against the control, `2026-10-18T22-15-56Z`.

## The typed route

The [scaffolded slice](#the-scaffolded-slice) found that the skeleton stood but its markers were the wrong markers: `route::of` read `METHOD /path` from each requirement's statement and its scenarios' `when`, the specification states both by role under the spec gate, so three of 63 requirements routed, all `start`'s, and the turn filled a `pub fn` for every route of the API; a `start` slice met `split_stems` with an empty stem list and spent its budget laying `crates/http/` then `crates/start/`; and the first act of every scaffolded turn was a `write_files` over the files the brief said stood. This round moves the route out of the text (emery 0.39.0 and this tree's `RELEASES.md` 0.14.0): the survey's recogniser types each surface's route at its registration — `router.post("/:id/pay", ..)` under its `/api/orders` mount is `POST /api/orders/{id}/pay`, by `Route::parse` over the mount and the literal, never the model's answer — the SDK carries it into one `contract` claim per routed surface, the engine binds the claim to every requirement whose subject leads with the surface's id, else whose winning anchor falls within the surface's lines, stores it as `Requirement.route`, renders `Route: POST /api/orders/{id}/pay` after `Status:`, lists each floor's routes in the slicing brief's `## Baseline` and refuses an answer that splits one route's requirements across slices, and lowers it to the target typed; `route::of` reads that field and nothing else, so a routed requirement is one constant, `.route(..)`, and handler whatever its statement spells, and a `start` requirement carrying a route is laid under the crate of the stem its route's resource names, `GET /health/live` under `crates/health/`. `split_stems` over a slice of `start` requirements alone names the slice's standing and where each kind of `start` behaviour lands in place of an empty list. `Scaffold::paths()` is handed to the SDK's `with_scaffolded`, so a `write_files` entry or `delete` naming a scaffolded file is refused with the call and the brief's file lines say `through `edit_files``. The runner's reader matches a scenario and a test as words, every non-alphanumeric run one space, and reads the typed route first for a probe. Before the component was built the field was reshaped to one `trigger` — a closed variant of `http(route)` and `message(name)`, both read by code at the source's registration, the spec-draft's `topic` answer gone with it — so the predictions below read `Requirement.route` as the `http` case of `trigger`, `Route:` keeping its line, and nothing of the rule changes. `layout.md` is not shed further; what is read is observed.

The control's revision `20dad6b9` is stamped at grammar 6 and the typed route makes 7, so the protocol's `--rebuild` is refused `spec-outdated` before any load and the arm is a fresh `--build`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --build --target omnia express-orders`, same knobs as the control (`composer-2.5`, the ladder 1200/400 → 2400/480, the build rung pinned to 1200/400 through `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG`), the component and `emery` built from this tree and the engine at 0.39.0, read against `2026-10-18T22-15-56Z` (63 requirements, 82 scenarios, seven slices in five waves `2/2/1/1/1`, 7/7 merged and 5/5 verified in 1888 s, the host `ok 88/88`, scenarios named 80/82 · 6 outside · 0 unreached · 13 probes, 14 routes under 7 modules, 33.4 M input and 239 K output over 12 completions) and, where the clause is the scaffold's, the off arm `23-02-59Z` (4 routes under 2 modules, `write_files` 2–4 and `edit_files` 4–45 a scaffolded turn). The plan is a new revision, so the slices and waves are the specify's and a difference in them is the slicing gate's or the model's, told apart below. The predictions and the rule were written before the component was built; the run is the operator's.

### Predictions

- **R1, the route is on the requirement.** The fixture registers twelve routes — seven under `/api/orders`, three under `/api/customers`, two under `/health` — and `run-1.spec.json` carries **twelve distinct `route` values**, each read from a registration and spelled as the source mounts it (`GET /api/orders/{id}`, `PUT /api/customers/{id}/tier`, `GET /health/ready`); **every requirement whose subject leads with a routed surface's id carries that surface's route**, and no requirement carries a route whose id its subject does not lead with and whose lines its winning anchor is outside; a `start` requirement carries a route only where it anchors inside a route's handler. The `## APIs and integrations` of `design.md` lists each route once, since a `contract` informs it.
- **R2, the markers are handlers.** For every requirement carrying a route the scaffold laid a `pub(crate) const`, a `.route(..)` in its part's `routes()`, and a handler over `<Name>Input` / `<Name>Output` with `todo!("REQ-.. <subject>: the handler")`, and **no `pub fn … the behaviour` marker stands for a routed requirement**, read from the scaffolded files at each build's first `edit_files`; at the label every routed surface is served under its stem — `run-1.fidelity.md`'s `## Routes` lists **≥ 12 routes under the `orders`, `customers`, and `health` crates**, against the control's 14 under 7 modules and the off arm's 4 under 2 — and `rg -c 'todo!\("REQ-' crates/*/src crates/*/tests` and `let _ = (input, context);` count **0** over the covered requirements, as K2 had it.
- **R3, the writes.** `write_files` calls per scaffolded build turn **0**, and `edit_files` calls **at most the markers the brief lists plus the lines it says were not added plus the loop's fixes, ≤ 3 × markers**; a refused `write_files` — the SDK's `was scaffolded before this turn` correction — **≤ 1 per scaffolded turn** and none on its second attempt, read from the turns' tool-call lines; correction rounds over the build turns **≤ 2 in total**, and none for a refused write, since the tool answers it inside the call.
- **R4, the `start` slice.** Where a slice of `start` requirements alone writes a crate, the `split_stems` finding **names the slice's standing** (`and this slice has no stem of its own: every requirement of it is `start`'s`) **and where a stated route lands** — never an empty list — and the slice's second candidate **removes the crate and reports**, so the run passes wave 1 with **≤ 1 correction round on that slice**; at the label **no `crates/foundation/`, `crates/http/`, or `crates/start/`** stands, and a `start` requirement carrying `GET /health/live` or `GET /health/ready`, where the specification states one under `start`, is laid and served under `crates/health/`, its part named for the slice.
- **R5, the tests.** `scenarios named` **≥ 97%** of the merged slices' scenarios, the control's figure, with the hyphenated scenario (`Per-probe …`) **among the named**, so the unnamed are `then [unknown]` scenarios alone; `rg -c 'Client::call' crates/*/tests` 0; and a test of a routed requirement drives the stem's `router()` through `oneshot` where the off arm's called the function.
- **R6, the host.** The host's three checks agree with every verdict; `unreached` **0**; probes **0**, every route at the label being a typed route of the slice that serves it, against the control's 13 and the off arm's 2; every verify's static drift finding 0; no `#[ignore]` line stands on a covered requirement's test.
- **Observed, never decided on:** the slicing gate's effect where `GET /health/ready` is stated under `start` and `health` both — the slices the plan cuts, whether the gate's finding was raised, and which stem the merged slice took; tokens and wall time against the control's 33.4 M, 239 K, and 1888 s and the off arm's 18.2 M and 171 K; which references each turn read through `read_doc`, `layout.md` and `translation.md` among them; whether a handler's `<Name>Input` kept the scaffolded `String` of a path parameter or took the wire's shape; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The route holds** when R1, R2, and R6 hold: the route is on the requirement from the registration, every routed requirement is a handler at the label, and the host agrees. This card's figures are then the band a later omnia card reads against, and R3's and R4's the first band for a turn that cannot write over the skeleton.
- **R1 failing by count** — fewer than twelve routes in `spec.json` — is the recogniser first: a registration whose verb or literal `typed_route` refused, read from the `contract` claims in `run-1.evidence-*.json` against the fixture's twelve, the adapter's; twelve claims accepted and a route missing from the specification is the engine's binding — a requirement under the surface whose subject leads with another id, or anchored outside the registration's lines — read from the requirement's subject and anchor, and recorded against the engine's `Basis::route_of`. **R1 failing by spelling** — a route without its mount, `POST /orders` for `POST /api/orders` — is the recogniser's `tree.mount(..)`, the adapter's.
- **R2 failing by shape.** A `pub fn … the behaviour` marker standing for a requirement that carries a route is `route::of` not reading the field, the adapter's, and decided first; a handler laid and a route missing from `## Routes` at the label is the turn removing the `.route(..)` or the entry's line, read from its `edit_files`, the prose's; a route served under another stem's crate is the slicing gate's or the relay's, read from the slice's requirements.
- **R3 failing.** A `write_files` over a scaffolded path that landed is the guard not applied — `paths()` missing the file, read from the brief's file lines against the refused and landed calls — the adapter's; one refused and the turn answering the refusal with a second `write_files` over the same path is the correction's wording, the SDK's; a `write_files` over a path the brief does not name is within the clause and counted apart.
- **R4 failing.** An empty list in the finding is `split_stems` not reading the slice, the adapter's; the finding named and the slice spending its budget on the crate anyway — moving it, as Round A's did — is the finding's wording first and `build.md`'s `start` bullet second, the prose's; `crates/health/` lacking the health route stated under `start` is the relay (`route::resource` over the typed path) where `spec.json` carries the route, the adapter's, and the extraction where it does not.
- **R5 failing** is the runner's reader first — a test named with a separator the words rule does not fold — then `testing.md`'s fill bullet, as K4 had it.
- **R6 failing by probes** is the runner's `states_route` not reading the typed route where `spec.json` carries one, the runner's; a route at the label under a slice none of whose requirements carries or states it is the turn adding a route, the prose's "add no route to give it a surface", read under R2.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a `cargo clippy` the shell holds past the rung, as the off arm's `SLICE-006` was, is the toolchain's, carried by the engine's retry and recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against the control**, `2026-10-18T22-15-56Z`, a fresh `--build` over the same fixture: the plan is a new revision, so a difference in slices or waves is told first by the slicing gate's finding where one was raised, then by the stems the specify derived against the control's `start`, `health`, `customers`, `orders`; the off arm is read for the scaffold's clauses alone, since its tree never shipped.

## Round B — the small model

No small-model build has run. The [typed route](#the-typed-route) is the band, built by `composer-2.5` under the Cursor backend, and what it spent is the gap a small model has to cross, read against `omnia-genai`'s budgets: 60–105 tool calls a scaffolded slice (`edit_files` 16–42, `read` 9–21, `shell` 12–32, `read_doc` 3–5, `list_docs` 1, `glob` and `grep` 1–3) against `GENAI_MAX_TOOL_CALLS` 128 and `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS` 128, with the candidate budget a constant of 2 in `omnia-backends/crates/genai/src/conversation.rs`, so one correction is all a turn gets, and the backend offering `read`, `list`, and `shell` alone; the `read` calls mostly over files the adapter had just written, since the brief named the scaffolded files and their markers and not their text; `edit_files` 42/16/21/38/20 over markers 10/13/14/14/20 and a `find` refused 1/1/0/3/3, since a handler's fill was two edits (the body, then the `let _ = (input, context);` line that otherwise moved the values) and a test's two (the `#[ignore]`, then the `todo!`); every test file's imports, provider, request, `oneshot`, and `to_bytes` lines rewritten by the model where `testing.md` describes them as one helper; 4 of the 12 routes restructured as `fn <name>_route()` over `handle_with` to answer a non-200 status; the two `start` slices opening with a `write_files` and spending a correction each, `Scaffold::paths()` being empty for them; and a cold `cargo clippy` of 1,089 s on the scaffolded slice's off arm against `GENAI_SHELL_TIMEOUT_SECS` 600, under a loop of five shell steps an iteration. This round cuts what a slice's turn has to read, decide, and spell, in the adapter and by code: the stub borrows, `let _ = (&input, &context);`, so a one-edit fill never moves a value; the brief cites every marker as the one block the turn replaces whole — the stub and the `todo!` for a handler or a consumer, the `todo!` for a function, the `#[ignore]`, the `async fn` head, the act line, and the `todo!` for a test — and inlines the text the scaffold wrote under each file's line, fenced as `rust`, with a held part of the slice's own stem while the total stays under `INLINE_BYTES`; each test file carries a lint-clean prelude, `provider`, and `send` or `deliver` where a scaffolded test calls one, and each scenario its act line laid from the typed route before its marker; the four root lists stand in `paths()` on a slice that laid no stem, so a `write_files` over one is refused inside the call; a design type whose signature is an enum — Rust `enum`, a TypeScript union of string literals, Python `class X(str, Enum)` or `class X(Enum)` — is scaffolded `pub enum <Name> {}` with `PartialEq, Eq`, every other `pub struct` with `PartialEq`; a `POST` handler is pre-rendered as `fn <name>_route()` over `handle_with` with `StatusCode::OK` explicit, so a `201` is one token; the loop is one check command the brief spells with the stem filled in, and the `find -newer` stray rule is `Tree::check_build`'s over a listing taken at `Scaffold::write`; and the brief carries, selected by code from what the scaffold laid, the signatures the slice's shapes use and the `translation.md` bullet each role the design names, quoted from the references through `emery_sdk::body`, so `read_doc layout.md` is no longer asked of a slice whose files all stand. The runner reads both backends — `omnia_genai`'s targets in `rust_log`, the `completion` and `tool call` lines matched by message, `GENAI_MODEL` and the binary on the card, the rung mapped to `GENAI_TIMEOUT_SECS` and `GENAI_SHELL_TIMEOUT_SECS` — and carries the typed route's three findings: `scenarios named` over the covered requirements' scenarios, the method behind a `<fn>_route()` over `handle_with`, and `emery_engine=debug`. The backend gains `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES`, default 2, so the correction budget is a knob the card records; its `completion` line already carries what the runner reads — `outcome`, `attempts`, `duration_ms`, `model_ms`, `tool_ms`, `check_ms`, and the tokens, under the `complete{n, label}` span — so no line was added. The context length is the server's own setting and never the backend's: genai's Ollama adapter carries `temperature`, `top_p`, `num_predict`, `seed`, and `stop` and no `num_ctx`, so an Ollama server is started with `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=<n>` or the model built from a Modelfile with `PARAMETER num_ctx <n>`, and a vLLM server with `--max-model-len <n>`, the figure recorded on the card.

Two runs make the control, one difference each. **B0, the component under the band's model**: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders`, the typed route's plan (`c2c52172`, seven slices in six waves `1/2/1/1/1/1`), knobs, ladder, and model, over the component built from this tree — a fresh `--build` instead where the engine's grammar has moved past 7, as the two rounds before it fell to — so what differs from the band is the scaffold alone. **B1, the component under the small model**: the same command with `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev` and the backend's variables — `GENAI_MODEL=qwen3-coder` at `GENAI_ENDPOINT` for an Ollama server, `GENAI_MODEL=openai::qwen3-coder` with `OPENAI_API_KEY` for vLLM — the endpoint, the server, the context length (set at the server, since Ollama's default silently truncates a brief that now inlines the files and the design), `GENAI_MAX_TOOL_CALLS`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES`, and `GENAI_SHELL_TIMEOUT_SECS` recorded in the results, so what differs from B0 is the backend and the model alone; `EVAL_JOBS=1`, since a local server answers one completion at a time. B1 runs at the backend's default tool-call cap only where B0's volume reads under ~60 a slice, and at a raised cap recorded on the card otherwise. The shell budget is the build rung's second figure under `emery-dev`, `GENAI_SHELL_TIMEOUT_SECS`, so where B0's cold `cargo clippy` reads past 600 s the run is put at `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=1200/1200` and the header records it; warming the worker through a `~/.cargo/registry` read root is a grant widening the adapter would carry for every deployment, measured on a card of its own if at all, and never `~/.cargo` whole. Three arms follow, each its own card and read against B1: **the `start`-only slice decided in code**, `Scaffold::plan` finding no stem and no relaid route and `run` answering `Built { covered: [], written: [] }` with no turn, the requirements reported `uncovered` where the band's 13 landed anyway, and what it forgoes — a cross-cutting `pub fn` a turn might have placed under `guardrails.md`'s table — read from B1's `start` turns before the arm is built; **the small-model register** for `build.md`, imperative sentences, one literal `edit_files` call shown per marker kind, lines under ~200 characters where the longest run 1,261–1,673, under `tests/prose.rs`'s 800 lines and parsing example either way; and **shrinking the slice's turn load**, through library helpers (`omnia-extensions`), deterministic typed scaffolding, or the engine's `SLICE_CAP`, keeping one turn per slice rather than splitting a slice into per-handler turns that lose part-level context and multiply `cargo` runs. The SDK's follow-ups — a `find` retried with whitespace normalised and the nearest line named, a `fill` tool keyed by the marker id so no `find` is spelled — are designed from B1's refused `find`s, read from the `responded` lines, and not before. The predictions and the rule were written before the component was built; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **B1, the text is in the brief, and the reads go.** Every scaffolded build turn's brief carries, under `What the adapter scaffolded for this slice before this turn:`, the text of each file the scaffold wrote fenced as `rust`, the lines it added to the entry, the topic table, and the root lists, each marker as the one block the turn replaces, the one check command with the stem filled in, and the signatures of the route fns, `handle_with`, `Json`, `Config::get`, and the doubles the slice's shapes use; `read` calls over a file the brief inlined **0** per scaffolded turn against the band's 9–21 a slice, `read_doc` **≤ 1** per scaffolded turn and `list_docs` **0** against 3–5 and 1, read from the tool-call lines; a `read` over a part the brief named and did not inline, the total having passed `INLINE_BYTES`, is within the clause and counted apart.
- **B2, one edit a marker.** `edit_files` calls per scaffolded turn **≤ 1.5 × markers**, each marker filled in one edit whose `find` is the block the brief cited, read from the `responded` lines, against the band's 42/16/21/38/20 over 10/13/14/14/20; a `find` refused **0** per scaffolded turn against 1/1/0/3/3; at the label `todo!("REQ-`, `let _ = (&input, &context);`, `let _ = (&status, &body);`, and `#[ignore = "REQ-` count **0** over the covered requirements, and `markers_left` and `scenarios_tested` report 0 over the build turns.
- **B3, the writes.** `write_files` calls **0** on every scaffolded turn, as the band has it, **and on every `start`-only turn**, against the band's 5 and 3; a `write_files` over a root list refused inside the call **≤ 1** per `start`-only turn and none on its second candidate; correction rounds over the build turns **≤ 2 in total**, none for a write.
- **B4, the shell.** Shell calls per scaffolded turn **≤ 16**, half the band's 12–32, the loop being one command; the command the turn runs is the one the brief spelled, read from the `tool call tool=shell subject=` lines, and no turn composes a `touch` or a `find -newer`; `Tree::check_build`'s `written beside the slice` finding **≤ 1** over the build turns, so the tree outside the layout at the label is **0**, the band's figure.
- **B5, the tests.** `scenarios named` **59/59 over the covered requirements' scenarios** of the scaffolded slices, the band's figure held, each test the name the scaffold gave it; every test file at the label carries the prelude's `provider` and its `send` or `deliver`, so `rg -c 'fn provider' crates/*/tests` counts the test files and `rg -c 'oneshot' crates/*/tests` the prelude's alone — no helper of the model's own beside it — and `Client::call` 0; a routed requirement's test drives the stem's `router()` through `send`.
- **B6, the enum and the status.** Every design type whose signature is an enum is a `pub enum` at the label deriving `PartialEq, Eq`, no `pub struct` standing for one (`CustomerTier`, `OrderStatus`, the fixture's two); a `POST` handler answering a non-200 status does so by one edit of its pre-rendered route fn's encoder, `StatusCode::OK` to `CREATED`, with no route restructured — `rg -c 'fn .*_route\(' crates/*/src` the scaffold's `POST` count exactly — and the runner's `## Routes` spells the method behind each, `?` **0**.
- **B7, the small model, B1 alone.** Built 7/7 and every wave verified with the host agreeing; tool calls per slice **≤ 50**, half the band's 60–105; no completion `exhausted` on its tool calls, its rounds, or its candidates, and none `timeout`; B1–B6 within B0's figures, `edit_files` per marker **≤ 2.0** and a `find` refused **≤ 1** per scaffolded turn, the small model's spelling allowed one slip where B0's is allowed none.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens and wall time against the band's 21.7 M, 188 K, and 1818 s, the brief having grown by the inlined text and the quoted signatures and the turn's reads shrunk; which references each turn still reads through `read_doc`; whether `<Name>Input` kept the scaffolded `String` of a path parameter or took the wire's shape; the slicing gate's `candidate rejected` finding, now in the log; the context length the server held the brief in and whether any turn's prompt was truncated; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The scaffold holds** when B1, B2, and B3 hold on B0: the turn edits text it was shown, one edit a marker with no `find` refused, and writes over nothing the scaffold laid or the seed holds. B0's figures are then the band a later omnia card reads against in place of the typed route's, and B4–B6's its first band. **The small model builds** when B7 holds on B1: B1's figures are the band for `emery-dev`, read beside B0's, and the arms are read against it.
- **B1 failing by a `read` over an inlined file** is the brief's wording — the turn re-reading what it was shown — the prose's; by a `read` over a file the brief did not inline is the cap, counted apart and recorded; by a `read_doc` of a document whose passage the brief quoted is the selection, the adapter's, the passage named.
- **B2 failing by two edits for one marker** is read from the first edit's `find` against the block the brief cited: a `find` shorter than the block, the block standing byte for byte in the file (`tests/target.rs` holds the scaffold to it), is the model's spelling, and the second edit the one the shorter `find` left; a block that does not stand in the file as cited is the citation's, the adapter's, decided first. **A `find` refused** is read the same way, and the SDK's two follow-ups are designed from the refused `find`s' shape — whitespace where the tolerant retry would have matched, a block cited and misspelled where `fill` would have — never from the count alone.
- **B3 failing.** A `write_files` over a root list that landed is `paths()` missing it, the adapter's; one refused and the `start`-only turn answering with a second `write_files` or spending its correction on a crate anyway is the finding's and the brief's wording first, and sends **the `start`-only slice decided in code** to its card; a `write_files` over a path the brief does not name is within the clause and counted apart.
- **B4 failing.** A file beside the slice at the label is the stray rule not applied — the listing missing it, or the path under a stem the rule admits — the adapter's; a `touch` or a `find -newer` in the shell lines is `build.md`'s loop still describing the five steps, the prose's; a shell count over 16 with the one command run is the model re-running it between edits, the band's shape under a smaller spelling, observed.
- **B5 failing** is the runner's reader first, as R5 had it; a test renamed from the scaffold's name is `testing.md`'s fill bullet; a prelude fn removed or a second request helper written beside `send` is the turn rewriting the prelude, the brief's wording, the prose's.
- **B6 failing.** An enum scaffolded as a struct is the detection, the adapter's, the signature's head recorded; a route fn restructured by the turn where the scaffold pre-rendered one is `handlers.md`'s status passage, the prose's; a `?` in `## Routes` behind a `<fn>_route()` is the runner's reader, fixed and the card re-read without a run.
- **B7 failing while B1–B6 held on B0 is the model's, read by its phase.** A completion `exhausted` is read for which budget — the tool calls, the rounds, or the candidates — and the knob raised for a re-run recorded on the card, the cap named; a `timeout` with the shell holding it is the toolchain's, the shell budget raised or the worker warmed and recorded; a candidate refused twice on a finding the brief's text answers sends **the small-model register** to its card; a turn that spends its tool calls editing without converging, the register in place, sends the slice's repeating mechanisms to the library helpers (`omnia-extensions`), typed scaffolding, or the engine's `SLICE_CAP`, keeping one turn per slice. A truncated prompt, read from the server's log or a reply answering what the brief's tail asked, is the deployment's context length, the card's `num_ctx` moved and the run put again before any arm.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the provider refuses (`provider`, `transport`), is the backend's and decided first; a run the engine's budget ends after the adapters' facts is graded on what landed, as the protocol has it.
- **Read B0 against the band**, `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z`, the same plan where the rebuild is admitted, so a difference is the scaffold's; where the grammar moved and the plan is fresh, the slices and waves are told apart by the slicing gate's finding first, now read at `debug`. **Read B1 against B0**, so a difference is the backend's and the model's; the arms against B1.

## Round C — the component fixes

[Round B](#round-b--the-small-model) attributed what failed to code before prose, and this round makes those changes, each in its checkout and none committed. In the adapter: a slice of `start` requirements alone — `Scaffold::plan` finding no stem and no relaid route — is answered `Built { covered: [], written: [] }` by `run` with no turn, no write, and no brief, so the engine merges nothing for it and reports every requirement of it `uncovered`, and the brief's `start` note, the root lists in `paths()`, and `split_stems`' "no stem of its own" branch go with the turn they served; a root-manifest entry whose crate the tree lacks — `start = { path = "crates/start" }` with no `crates/start/Cargo.toml`, the line B1b's turn left behind after its `rm -rf` — is a finding of both turns' `check` beside `manifest_drift`, which keeps the line by shape; the route fn is pre-rendered over `handle_with` for any handler whose scenarios state a status other than 200, whatever the method, and `Shape::Encoded` is read from the same scenarios, so a `GET /health/ready` answering 503 is handed its encoder and the general constructor's passage rather than restructured, and the doubles passage carries `impl ScriptedTables` and `impl Memory` whole where the slice's roles reach a table or a state store; and a requirement lowered with no statement, the `conflict` whose body the engine leaves empty, reads `[conflict]` in its marker and doc line. In the SDK: `write_files` and `edit_files` admit `files` and `edits` passed as one JSON string, parsed and read as the list or map they spell, and a `find` that matches nothing is refused naming the line of the file nearest its first line, so the `//!` the nine refused `find`s misspelled comes back quoted. In the backend: the one nudge for a reply that is not JSON becomes a final-answer round — the lend tools withdrawn and `format: "json"` set, which Ollama honours only with the tools gone — put once before each candidate, the tools and the schema steering returned for the correction turn after it, so a verify whose replies are prose is given one round with nothing else to do before a candidate reaches the check; `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES` stands at `0f55ae8`, and the uncommitted change rides into `emery-dev` through the `path` patch for the C1 build alone.

Two runs, one difference each, as Round B had them. **C0, the component under the band's model**: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders` under `composer-2.5`, the band's plan, knobs, ladder, and rung, so what differs from B0 is the adapter and the SDK. **C1, the component under the small model**: the same under `emery-dev`, `qwen3-coder:30b` at the Ollama server B1b ran against — `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=131072`, `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`, `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, and `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=1` beside them — `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, the tool-call and round caps at their defaults, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES` at its default of 2, so what differs from C0 is the backend and the model, and from B1b the component, the SDK, and the final round. The predictions and the rule were written before any change was made; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **C1, the `start`-only slices open no turn.** `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` open **no completion** on either run — no `build-SLICE-001` or `build-SLICE-002` label in the log — and the run reports each with **`covered` 0, every requirement `uncovered`, and `written` empty**, `nothing to merge` in the text render; wave 1 is verified over the seed alone, since a wave that merged nothing is verified; at the label no `crates/start/`, `crates/http/`, `crates/auth/`, `crates/bootstrap/`, `crates/errors/`, or `crates/start-stem/` stands and the root manifest's lists name the stems alone; the `split_stems` finding over an empty stem list **0**, the branch being gone. The band's `start` figures — the two slices' 5 and 3 `write_files`, 2 corrections, `start.listen-port` and `start.not-found` covered with nothing written — move to 0, 0, and 0 covered.
- **C2, the manifest.** No `<stem> = { path = "crates/<dir>" }` line whose `crates/<dir>/Cargo.toml` the tree lacks stands at any merge or at the label, and no `<stem>.workspace = true` without its path entry; the new finding's count over the build and verify turns is **observed**, 0 expected where no turn writes a crate it removes.
- **C3, the final round, C1 alone.** Every completion whose reply is not JSON is followed by **one round with no tools and `format: json`**, read from the backend's `nudging` line and the request after it, and the reply to it parses as JSON on every such completion; **no completion ends `exhausted` on its candidates with a prose reply** among them, so wave 1's verify — the seed alone, the three checks passing — answers a `Verdict` and the wave is labelled; the rounds spent on the final round **≤ 1 per candidate**.
- **C4, the arguments and the refused `find`.** `write_files` or `edit_files` refused as `invalid arguments: invalid type: string` **0** on both runs against B1b's 2, a string `files` or `edits` landing as the list it spells; every `find` refused for matching nothing **names a line of the file** in its refusal, read from the `responded` lines, and a `find` refused twice over one block **≤ 1 per turn**, the second spelling the line named.
- **C5, the selection and the route fn, C0 first.** `health`'s brief carries the general constructor's passage and `impl ScriptedTables` and `impl Memory` whole, and its turn reads **`read_doc` ≤ 1 and no registry source through `read` or the shell** against B0's 4 `read_doc`, 5 registry reads, and 15 `rg`/`find`/`ls`; `rg -c 'fn .*_route\(' crates/*/src` at the label **equals the scaffold's count** — one per handler whose scenarios state a status other than 200, `health_ready` among them, read from the scaffolded files at each turn's first `edit_files` — and **no route is restructured by a turn**, the pre-rendered fn edited at its encoder; `?` in `## Routes` 0.
- **C6, the conflict marker.** No `// REQ-… <subject>:` line in a scaffolded part is empty after the colon; REQ-017's and REQ-018's read `[conflict]`, and their `todo!` markers spell it; the turn fills them from the scenarios as B0's did, with no finding raised over the marker.
- **C7, the small model builds, C1 alone.** Every scaffolded slice reaches its turn and **no completion ends `exhausted` or `timeout`** at 7200/600; **built 7/7 and every wave verified** with the host agreeing is the figure the band asks, and the floor the round holds C1 to is **wave 2's verify reached** — `health` built and merged, the verify answered — with `edit_files` within 2.0 a marker and a `find` refused ≤ 1 per scaffolded turn, as B7 allowed the small model; tool calls per scaffolded slice **≤ 50**, half the band's 60–105, and a build turn's `model_ms` **≤ 3600 s**, read against B1b's 1879 s for a `start` turn that wrote a crate four times.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens and wall time against B0's 6.81 M and 382 s over two waves and the band's 21.7 M and 1818 s; the server's prompt evaluations per turn and their rates, against B1b's six over two turns at 87–682 tok/s; which references each turn still reads through `read_doc`; the `read`s over seed files the brief does not inline, counted apart under B1's clause; whether a C1 turn reaches the tree through the shell around the guard — an `echo >`, a `git checkout`, an `rm -rf` — and what the check named of it; the enum and status readings B6 left undecided, `CustomerTier` and `OrderStatus` at the label; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The component holds** when C1, C2, C4, and C6 hold on both runs and C5 on C0: no turn for a `start`-only slice, no dangling manifest line, the string arguments admitted, the refusal naming its line, the conflict marker spelled, and `health` handed its passages. **C0 is then the band** in place of the typed route's where its 7/7 are built and 6/6 verified with the host agreeing, the five scaffolded slices' figures read for B1–B6 as Round B's rule has them. **The small model builds** when C7 holds on C1: C1's figures are the band for `emery-dev`, read beside C0's, and Round D is read against it.
- **C1 failing.** A `start`-only slice opening a completion is `Scaffold::plan`'s reading: a requirement of it carrying a route whose resource names a stem is the relay's and within the clause, the turn opening for the relaid part; one carrying no route and a turn opening is the predicate, the adapter's. A `start` requirement the plan put under a `start` slice that the band's `health` carried is the slicing gate's, since the plan is the band's and does not move on a rebuild.
- **C2 failing** by a dangling line at the label is the rule not reading the manifest's shape — the line spelled with a `version`, a `package`, or a path outside `crates/` — the adapter's, the line quoted; the finding raised and the turn leaving the line is the finding's wording first and the register second.
- **C3 failing.** A completion `exhausted` on its candidates where the log shows a prose reply with no final round after it is the backend's — the round not taken, or taken with the tools still advertised — decided first; the final round taken and the reply still prose is the model's under JSON mode, and sends the verdict's instruction to **Round D** with the count; a JSON reply the check refuses twice on a finding the brief states is the register's and sends the same.
- **C4 failing.** A string `files` or `edits` refused is the visitor not re-dispatching the parsed value, the SDK's; a refused `find` carrying no line is the refusal's shape, the SDK's; a `find` refused twice over one block with the line named is the model's spelling, counted and observed.
- **C5 failing.** A `read_doc` of `omnia-api.md` or `testing.md` on `health` with their passages in the brief is the turn re-reading what it was shown, the prose's, as B1's clause had it; a passage absent from the brief where the slice's roles reach the capability is the selection, the adapter's, the role and passage named; a `fn .*_route\(` count over the scaffold's is a turn restructuring a route the scaffold pre-rendered none for, read for the status its scenarios state — a status the scenarios do not state is the model's, one they state and the detection missed is the adapter's.
- **C6 failing** by an empty marker line is the fallback not applied, the adapter's; `[conflict]` spelled and the turn leaving the marker is the fill, counted under B2.
- **C7 failing while C1–C6 hold is the model's, read by its phase**, as B7 had it: a `timeout` with the model holding it is the context, the server's prompt evaluations recorded and the cap raised once more where the model time read under 3600 s; an `exhausted` on the tool calls or the rounds is the knob, raised and recorded; a candidate refused twice on a finding the brief's text answers, or a prose reply surviving the final round, sends **Round D**; a turn that spends its tool calls editing without converging, the register in place, sends the slice's repeating mechanisms to the library helpers (Phase 5), typed scaffolding, or the engine's `SLICE_CAP`, keeping one turn per slice, as Round B had it. A C1 turn reaching the tree through the shell around the guard is observed and, where the check named nothing of what it left, the rule it escaped is named for the adapter.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the provider refuses, is the backend's and decided first; a run the engine's budget ends after the adapters' facts is graded on what landed.
- **Read C0 against B0** for the two slices B0 built, `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-003`, and **against the band**, `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z`, for the rest, the same plan rebuilt; **read C1 against C0**, so a difference is the backend's and the model's, and against B1b for the `start` turns and the verify it exhausted.

## Round D — the small-model register

[Round C](#round-c--the-component-fixes) leaves the prose as Round B found it: `build.md`'s 62 non-blank lines, 19 of them past 400 characters and the longest 1,261–1,673, and `verify.md`'s 46, each a clause-chained paragraph a large model reads whole and a small one reads to its first instruction. This round rewrites the two in the register the small model follows — short imperative sentences, one idea each, no line past about 200 characters, one literal `edit_files` call shown for each marker kind (a handler's stub and `todo!`, a function's `todo!`, a consumer's, a test's `#[ignore]`, head, act line, and `todo!`, and a list's line under its anchor), one literal verdict, and the "Answer with one JSON object" instruction as the last line of each prompt — with nothing of the code changed, under `tests/prose.rs`'s cap of 800 non-blank lines and its parsing worked examples, and every rule the SDK's brief states left out of the prompt as the invariant has it. Two paired `--rebuild` runs, as the protocol holds a small-model prose change to: **D0**, C0's command, model (`composer-2.5`), knobs, and rung over the component built with the register, read against C0 to confirm the register does not regress the large-model band; and **C2**, C1's command, server, knobs, and rung under `emery-dev` and `qwen3-coder` over the same component, read against C1. The predictions and the rule are written before the prompts are rewritten; the runs are the operator's.

### Predictions

- **D1, the shape.** `build.md` and `verify.md` hold under 800 non-blank lines with their worked examples parsing, `tests/prose.rs` passing; no line of either runs past 200 characters, read by `awk 'length > 200'`; every document of `PROSE` is still reached from one of the two.
- **D2, the verdict and the final round.** Every verify completion's first reply is JSON — the backend's `nudging` line **0** over the run against C1's count — and no build completion's reply is nudged either; the final round stands unused.
- **D3, the edits.** `edit_files` per marker **≤ 1.5** on every scaffolded turn against C1's figure and B7's 2.0, each marker filled in one edit whose `find` is the block the brief cited; a `find` refused **0** per scaffolded turn against ≤ 1; `write_files` 0 on every scaffolded turn and none refused by the guard.
- **D4, the build.** Built 7/7 and every wave verified, within C1's figures where C1 built: tool calls per scaffolded slice **≤ C1's**, shell calls **≤ 16** a turn with the one command re-run no more than twice between edits, and a build turn's `model_ms` **≤ C1's**; where C1 did not build, C2 reaching the wave C1 fell short of is the figure.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens, the brief having shrunk by the prose and grown by nothing; which references each turn still reads; the server's prompt evaluations; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The register holds** when D1 and D2 hold, D0 stays within C0's band (7/7 built, 6/6 verified, `edit_files` per marker and tool calls within C0's figures), and D3 and D4 hold on C2 within C1's figures or better; C2 is then the band for `emery-dev` in place of C1, and the register ships for both models.
- **D2 failing** by a prose first reply on a verify whose instruction is the last line is the model's under this register, the final round carrying it and the count recorded; by a prose reply on a build turn is the build prompt's closing instruction, the prose's.
- **D3 failing** by two edits a marker is read as B2's clause reads it: the first `find` against the cited block, the model's spelling where the block stands byte for byte, the citation's where it does not; a `find` refused with its line named and spelled again is the model's, counted.
- **D4 failing** while D1–D3 hold is the model's by its phase, as C7's clause has it — the context, the knob, or the slice's turn load — and a turn that edits without converging under this register sends the slice's repeating mechanisms to the library helpers (Phase 5), typed scaffolding, or the engine's `SLICE_CAP`, keeping one turn per slice.
- **Read D0 against C0** and **C2 against C1**, the same component but the prose, so a difference on either is the register's.

### Results

**D0 and Round E ran together**, card `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, since `#176` (the register) and `#177` (the laid design types) landed on `main` before the `express-orders` rebuild was put: `--rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders` under `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `composer-2.5` under the Cursor backend, the ladder 1200/400 → 2400/480, the build rung 1200/400, over the component built from `fe155ef` with the six JSON example lines of `build.md` that ran past 200 characters (204–273, at lines 97, 126, 149, 170, 209, and 210) shortened and the test marker's `find` aligned to `render::test_block` (`#[ignore = "…"]` without `#[tokio::test]`, `emery_omnia.wasm`, 4,202,213 bytes). **D0 built the plan: 7/7 merged in 6 waves, 6/6 verified, 2931 s (`2913 s` serial, of which `464 s` is `build-SLICE-003`'s first attempt ended by an upstream relay error and retried by the engine in a fresh copy, and `411 s` is wave 1's cold compile), the host's checks `ok 63/63` and 59/59 scenarios named**, the label at `084790f9`. Build turns **11.72 M input (11.18 M cached) and 94.3 K output** over 12 completions, a **32.8% drop in input tokens and 32.1% drop in output tokens** against C0's 17.45 M and 138.8 K. **C2** (`qwen3-coder:30b` under `emery-dev`) has not yet run (the local Ollama server was down during this session).
