**Decision — the register holds on D0 and does not regress the large-model band; C2 remains for the local server.** D1 and D2 hold, D3 holds on `edit_files` per marker (0.60–1.33×) and `write_files` (0), and D0 builds 7/7 and verifies 6/6 with a 32.8% reduction in input tokens against C0.

## Round E — the laid design types

Every omnia card so far scaffolded each design type a slice owns as an empty `pub struct <Name> {}`, or `pub enum <Name> {}` where the signature is an enumeration, the design's signature fenced in its doc and a marker in the brief telling the turn to replace the block with the type the signature spells, every field documented, `Eq` dropped where a field is a float. That fill is a transcription — a TypeScript interface or a Python model read into Rust under the layout's lints — and every scaffolded turn of [Round C](#round-c--the-component-fixes)'s C0 made it before any behaviour was written, one edit a type among its 10–25 markers, reading the signature off the doc and spelling the derives, the `serde(rename)`s, and the docs itself. This round moves the transcription into the adapter: `scaffold/types.rs` reads the signature — a TypeScript `interface`, object alias, class, `enum`, or union of string literals; a Python `BaseModel`, `@dataclass`, `TypedDict`, `NamedTuple`, or `Enum` class; a Rust `struct` or `enum` — into fields and variants, and the scaffold lays the type whole: `string` `String`, `boolean` `bool`, `number` `f64`, or `i64` where the field's last word is a count (`cents`, `count`, `id`, `index`, `page`, `size`, `total`, `quantity`, `seconds`, `millis`), `T[]` `Vec<T>`, `Record<string, T>` `BTreeMap<String, T>`, `x?: T` and `T | null` `Option<T>`, `Date` `String`, `unknown` `serde_json::Value`, the Python spellings beside them; every field under a `///`, `#[serde(rename = "..")]` where the wire's name is not snake_case, a Python default `#[serde(default = "default_<field>")]` over a fn beside the struct; a literal union or an `Enum` class a `pub enum` of PascalCase variants each `#[serde(rename = "<literal>")]`; a field typed by a design type the slice owns by that type's name, and by one another slice owns as `serde_json::Value` with a doc line naming the design type, since the owning part is not in the tree when the field is laid; `Eq` dropped where a field is `f64` or holds a type that dropped it; the type named from the identifier the signature declares rather than the key's last segment. A signature the reader cannot read whole — a `z.infer<..>` alias, a union of object types, an interface of index signatures alone, a table — is laid as before, the empty marker with the brief's entry, so the turn transcribes those alone. The brief names the laid types in one sentence as finished, each `serde_json::Value` field as one to leave, and keeps the marker entry for the fallbacks.

One arm, the protocol's `--rebuild`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders`, the band's plan (`c2c52172`, seven slices in six waves `1/2/1/1/1/1`), knobs, ladder, and model, over the component built from this tree, so what differs from C0 (`2026-10-19T22-15-39Z`) is the laid types alone. The plan owns eighteen data types: `customers` holds `CreateCustomer`, `Customer`, and `CustomerTier`; `orders-reading` `ListQuery`, `Order`, `OrderLine`, and `Page`; `orders-placement` `Address`, `Catalogue`, `DeliveryZone`, `PlaceOrder`, `ShippingQuote`, and `Totals`; `orders-mutations` `AmendLines`, `OrderState`, `TERMINAL_STATES`, `TRANSITIONS`, and `Transition`. Four are `z.infer<typeof ..Schema>` aliases (`CreateCustomer`, `PlaceOrder`, `AmendLines`, `ListQuery`) and two tables (`TRANSITIONS`, `TERMINAL_STATES`), the six the reader leaves as markers; the twelve others are ten interfaces and two literal unions. `Order` types `shipTo: Address`, `state: OrderState`, and `transitions: Transition[]`, which `orders-placement` and `orders-mutations` own and build after it, so those three fields are laid `serde_json::Value`; `Transition.state: OrderState` is laid by name, the two being one slice's. Four interfaces carry a `number` whose last word is outside the count list — `OrderLine.weightGrams`, `Catalogue.weight_grams`, `DeliveryZone.minDays`, `ShippingQuote.businessDays` — so those four drop `Eq`, `Order` drops it too since it holds `OrderLine` (corrected from four before the arm ran: the fixpoint the plan states reaches `Order` through `lines`), and the seven others keep it. A `--build --target omnia train`, where the operator puts one beside the arm, records the first `## Reference comparison` figures over laid types for [the train reference round](#the-train-reference-round) to read, and decides nothing here. The predictions and the rule were written before any code was changed; the run is the operator's.

### Predictions

- **E1, the types are laid.** The scaffolded parts, inlined in each brief and read from the log, carry the twelve readable types whole — ten `pub struct`s and the two `pub enum`s `CustomerTier` and `OrderState`, every field and variant under a `///`, every camelCase wire name under `#[serde(rename = "..")]`, `Order`'s three cross-slice fields `serde_json::Value` with their doc lines, `Page<T>` generic, `Eq` dropped on exactly the five structs named above — and the six fallbacks as empty markers, `pub struct CreateCustomer {}`, `PlaceOrder`, `AmendLines`, `ListQuery`, `Transitions`, and `TerminalStates`, with the signature fenced in each doc; the brief's marker list counts those six among the types and none of the twelve, and names the twelve in its laid-types sentence.
- **E2, the fill leaves the laid types.** `edit_files` edits whose `find` lies inside a laid type's declaration — other than one retyping a `serde_json::Value` field — **≤ 2 in total over the five scaffolded turns**, read from the `responded` lines; `write_files` 0 on every scaffolded turn, as C0 has it; at the label every laid type stands with every field's name and Rust type as laid, the `Value` fields and the docs excepted, read by `rg -A` over the label's parts against the scaffolded text; `edit_files` per marker **≤ 1.5** on every scaffolded turn where the markers are the brief's list, now without the twelve, against C0's 5/10, 15/16, 16/18, 20/20, 23/25 over lists that held them.
- **E3, the mapping stands.** A laid field retyped by a turn — an edit whose `replace` changes the field's Rust type, `f64` to `i64`, `String` to a date type, `Option<T>` to `T` — **≤ 2 over the run and none on a field whose last word is in the count list**; no correction round over the build turns names a design type, and no `cargo clippy` error a turn repaired through `edit_files` sits inside a laid type's lines, read from the edits' `find` blocks.
- **E4, the cross-slice fields.** At the label `Order`'s `ship_to`, `state`, and `transitions` each stand `serde_json::Value` as laid, or are retyped to the owning part's type (`crate::placement::Address`, `crate::mutations::OrderState`) by a later slice or a wave's verify; **no verify fails and no `## Findings` entry names them**, the host's checks agreeing with every verdict as C0's did, and no part declares a second `Address` or `OrderState` of its own, `rg -c 'pub (struct|enum) (Address|OrderState)\b' crates/*/src` **1 each**.
- **E5, the gates.** `markers_left`, `scenarios_tested`, and `response_wrappers` 0 over the build turns, `## Findings` 0 over the run, built 7/7 and verified 6/6 as C0; `rg -c 'pub struct \w+ \{\}' crates/*/src` at the label **0**, every fallback filled by its turn.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens and wall time against C0's 17.45 M input, 138.8 K output, and 1792 s, the brief having grown by the laid fields and the turn's transcription gone; whether a turn re-documents a laid field, `/// The `id` field.` to a sentence; whether a `Date` field stays `String`; which references each turn reads through `read_doc`; the `train` build's `## Reference comparison` where one is put; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The laid types hold** when E1, E2, and E3 hold: twelve laid as the signatures spell and six left as markers, the turn filling the markers and not relaying what stood, the mapping standing but for two retypings. This card's figures are then the band for the type markers' share of a turn, read beside C0's for the rest, and E3's retypings the first reading of the count list.
- **E1 failing by count** — a readable signature left as a marker, or a fallback laid — is the reader, the adapter's, the signature's head recorded; **by shape** — a field dropped, an optional field laid required, a rename missing, a variant misspelled, `Eq` kept over an `f64` or dropped where none is — is the reader or the fixpoint, the adapter's, the field named; a `cargo clippy` error at the loop's first run inside a laid type's lines is the printer's or the mapping's, decided first and apart from E2.
- **E2 failing by an edit inside a laid type** is read for what it changed: a doc line is the model's spelling, counted and not decided on; a field renamed off the laid spelling is `handlers.md`'s wire rule against the laid rename — the adapter's where the laid rename was wrong, the prose's where it was right; a laid type rewritten whole, its `find` the declaration, is the brief's laid-types sentence, the prose's; a type retyped is E3's.
- **E3 failing.** A count-listed field retyped `i64` to `f64` is the list's, the adapter's, the field and the values its tests carry recorded; a field off the list retyped `f64` to an integer is observed, and the list grows by a word only where two cards retype it; a `String` date retyped to a date crate's type is observed, with the dependency line it added.
- **E4 failing.** A verify failing, or a finding naming a `Value` field — two slices typing one design type differently, `verify.md`'s seam — is the `Value` rule meeting the verify's repair, read for which side the repair kept: the rule holds where the owner's type was kept, and the brief's "leave it" sentence is the prose's where the non-owning part was retyped to a struct of the turn's own; a second `Address` or `OrderState` declared in a non-owning part is the same sentence.
- **E5 failing** by an empty `pub struct` at the label is a fallback the turn did not fill, read under B2's clause for the marker's citation; by a `response_wrappers` finding on a laid type is the reader laying a `data` wrapper, the adapter's.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the relay ends mid-turn, is the backend's and decided first; a `cargo clippy` the shell holds past the rung is the toolchain's, carried by the engine's retry and recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against C0**, `2026-10-19T22-15-39Z`, the same plan rebuilt over the same model and knobs, so a difference is the laid types'; the `train` build is read by the train reference round's rule and decides nothing here.

### Results

**The arm ran on `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`** (shared with D0 above), `--rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders`, and **the `train` build ran beside it on `2026-10-20T05-34-32Z`** (`--rebuild 2026-10-20T04-53-00Z --target omnia train`, after `#177` fixed a TypeScript `extends` base whose trailing comment swallowed the opening brace): on `express-orders`, built 7/7 in 6 waves, 6/6 verified, `ok 63/63` and 59/59 scenarios named at `084790f9`, 11.72 M input tokens (down 32.8% from C0's 17.45 M) and 94.3 K output (down 32.1% from 138.8 K); on `train`, built 2/2 in 2 waves, 2/2 verified in 628 s (`5.49 M` input, `47.8 K` output), `ok 23/23` and 19/19 scenarios named at `4ab7f4f6`, **reference oracle 21/22** (9/10 static, 12/12 replay; up from 7/22 on `2026-10-20T04-53-00Z` before the `extends` fix).

- **E1 holds.** All twelve readable types were laid whole into their parts before the turns — `Customer` and `CustomerTier` in `customers.rs`; `Order`, `OrderLine`, and `Page<T>` in `reading.rs`; `Address`, `Catalogue`, `DeliveryZone`, `ShippingQuote`, and `Totals` in `placement.rs`; `OrderState` and `Transition` in `mutations.rs` — every field and variant under a `///`, every camelCase wire name under `#[serde(rename = "..")]`, `Order`'s `ship_to`, `state`, and `transitions` laid `serde_json::Value` and `Vec<serde_json::Value>` under `/// Carries the design's \`<Type>\`, declared by another slice, as a value.`, and `Eq` dropped on `OrderLine`, `Order`, `Catalogue`, `DeliveryZone`, and `ShippingQuote` alone; the six `z.infer<..>` and table types (`CreateCustomer`, `ListQuery`, `PlaceOrder`, `AmendLines`, `Transitions`, `TerminalStates`) were laid as `pub struct <Name> {}` markers and listed alone among the brief's type markers (`10`, `14`, `15`, `15`, and `22` markers across the five scaffolded turns, down from `10`, `16`, `18`, `20`, and `25` in C0).
- **E2 holds.** Across all five scaffolded turns, **only 1 edit** (`≤ 2`) had a `find` inside any laid type's declaration — in `SLICE-007` (`n=11`, call 7), adding `Copy` to `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]` on `OrderState`; **0 edits** touched any field, variant, rename, or doc of the twelve laid types, and at the label `084790f9` all twelve stand byte for byte as the scaffold laid them (save that one `Copy` on `OrderState`). `write_files` **0** on every turn; `edit_files` per marker **6/10 (0.60×), 17/14 (1.21×), 11/15 (0.73×), 20/15 (1.33×), and 18/22 (0.82×)**, every turn `≤ 1.33× ≤ 1.5×`.
- **E3 holds.** **0 laid fields** were retyped over the run (`0 ≤ 2`, and `0` on the count list): `weight_grams`, `min_days`, and `business_days` stayed `f64`, every `*Cents`, `quantity`, `total`, `page`, and `page_size` field stayed `i64`, and `created_at`, `updated_at`, and `at` stayed `String`. No correction round named a design type (the one rejection on `SLICE-004` was `unused_dependencies` for `anyhow` and `bytes`), and no `cargo clippy` error sat inside a laid type's lines.
- **E4 holds.** At `084790f9`, `Order`'s `ship_to`, `state`, and `transitions` stand `serde_json::Value` and `Vec<serde_json::Value>` as laid; no verify failed and no finding named them; `rg -c 'pub (struct|enum) (Address|OrderState)\b' crates/*/src` matches **1** in `crates/orders/src/placement.rs` (`Address`) and **1** in `crates/orders/src/mutations.rs` (`OrderState`), with no duplicate in `reading.rs`.
- **E5 holds.** `markers_left`, `untested_scenarios`, and `response_wrappers` reported **0** over the build turns; all six fallback markers were filled by their turns (`CreateCustomer`, `ListQuery`, `PlaceOrder` with `PlaceOrderLine`, `AmendLines` with `AmendLine`, `TerminalStates` as a unit struct with `VALUES`, and `Transitions` as a unit struct with `allowed`), leaving **0** empty design-type structs at the label (the only `pub struct \w+ \{\}` in `crates/*/src` are the two parameterless `GET` route inputs `HealthLiveInput {}` and `HealthReadyStoreOkInput {}` in `health.rs`).
- **Observed.** No turn re-documented a laid field (`/// The \`id\` field.` stood untouched on all twelve types), and every `Date` field stayed `String`. On `train` (`2026-10-20T05-34-32Z`), the thirteen design types of the specification were laid across `train-update-ingestion` and `train-update-events`, and the reference oracle jumped from 7/22 (where `ExtendedVehiculo` had fallen back to `pub struct ExtendedVehiculo {}` before the `extends` comment fix) to **21/22**, the one remaining failure being `static/0005` (`stops upstream returned no stops`, where an empty `/gtfs/stops` array is refused as `bad_gateway` rather than answered as 0 events).

**Decision — the laid types hold, and `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z` is the new band.** E1, E2, E3, E4, and E5 all hold on `express-orders` with 0 fields retyped, 1 edit across all twelve laid types (`Copy` on `OrderState`), `edit_files` 0.60–1.33× markers, and a 32.8% drop in input tokens against C0.

## Round F — the cut brief

[Round E](#round-e--the-laid-design-types)'s band, `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, handed every scaffolded slice a brief that said more than the slice could use: the whole design, its eighteen data `Type:` blocks and eleven mechanism blocks alike, so a slice read the signatures of types other slices own and the scaffold had already laid into its own module; the passages of every role the design's `## APIs and integrations` names anywhere, since `reference::roles` read that section whole — `Inbound HTTP` selected the consume rows, `url` and `fetch` the upstream capability, `DATABASE_URL` among the words that did it — so `health` was handed `HttpRequest`, `MatchedHttp`, the broker's rows, and the delay row beside the two stores it probes; and no passage for the two shapes the band's turns went looking for, a probe and a cache-aside read, which is where `health`'s turn spent 33 shell calls and 13 registry reads hunting a deadline API the template's lints forbid and wrote a `within<F>` timer and a `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` setting anyway. This round is the three items of the roadmap's second phase, each in its checkout and none committed. In the SDK, the brief's design is cut to the slice under `The design, cut to this slice:` (`crates/sdk/src/target/design.rs`): every paragraph and every `(mechanism)` block stand whole; a data block whose key the adapter's `target::Scaffold.types` names — the keys of the types its scaffold laid, markers among them — is one line pointing at the scaffold section; a data block the slice neither owns nor spells in its text — the plan entry, the specification cut, its owned signatures, and the scaffold's brief, read as `words` splits them, the key's identifier matched word for word with a plural as its singular — is left out and listed once at the end by key. In the adapter, `reference::roles` reads the slice — its requirements, their scenarios, its brief — and of the design's APIs paragraphs only those that cite one of its requirement ids, a `REQ-a through REQ-b` range expanded, or cite none, each text as its plain tokens and as `words` splits it; `api`, `http`, `rest`, `url`, and `fetch` select no upstream and `inbound` no consumer; `handlers.md` gains `## A probe` and `## A cache-aside read`, which `reference::idioms` selects from the slice's own words alone — `probe`, `readiness`, `timeout`, `deadline`; `cache`, `ttl`, `invalidate` — and the brief quotes whole after the capabilities; and `lib.rs` hands `Scaffold::types()` to the SDK beside `paths()`. The `fetch` words were dropped before the arm was built and after the band's revision was read: its uncited store paragraph spells `order fetch` for a `SELECT`, which would have selected the upstream capability on every slice as `DATABASE_URL`'s `url` did, and the one slice that reaches an upstream, `orders-placement`, spells `upstream` itself; the amendment is in `omnia_scaffold_roles_scoped`.

One arm, the protocol's `--rebuild`: `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders` under `composer-2.5`, the band's plan (`c2c52172`, seven slices in six waves `1/2/1/1/1/1`), ladder, rung, and knobs, over the component built from this tree with the round's changes uncommitted and the SDK of the engine's working tree through the `[patch.crates-io]` path entries, the release `emery` 0.39.0 as built, since nothing of the engine's own changed. What differs from the band is the three items and the six example lines of `build.md` that rode into the band uncommitted and landed as `808aa9f`. The backend logs no brief, so the brief is read offline: each slice's brief rendered natively through `Adapter::run` over the band's committed revision — the plan entry, the specification cut, the design, and the typed requirements and owned signatures lowered from `emery show` of the band's project as the engine lowers them — once over the control, `808aa9f` over the released SDK in a detached worktree, and once over this tree, the slices built in plan order into one seeded tree so a held list reads as it does in a wave. The predictions and the rule were written before the component was built; the run is the operator's.

### Predictions

- **F1, the design cut.** Every scaffolded brief (`health`, `customers`, `orders-reading`, `orders-placement`, `orders-mutations`) opens its design under `The design, cut to this slice:`; the eleven `(mechanism)` blocks stand whole in every one; the slice's owned types stand as pointer lines — `customers` 3, `orders-reading` 4, `orders-placement` 6, `orders-mutations` 5, the six fallback markers among them, `health` none; a data block another slice owns stands only where the slice's text spells its name — `Customer` and `Order` on every brief, since every specification cut carries the preamble's "customers and orders" — and the closing line lists the rest: **16 keys on `health`**, whose cut carries the eleven mechanism blocks, `Customer`, and `Order` alone, and **≥ 4 keys on every other scaffolded brief**; `health`'s design section is **≥ 2,500 bytes** below the control's whole design, and no brief's design carries a signature of a type the scaffold laid.
- **F2, the passages.** `health` carries `pub trait TableStore` with `impl ScriptedTables`, `pub trait StateStore` with `impl Memory`, `pub trait Identity` with `impl FixedIdentity` (the inbound paragraph cites its ids and says `bearer authentication on /api`, the paragraph's grain), the store and vault translation rows, and both idioms, `A probe` and `A cache-aside read` (the latter by `cache` in "cache probe outcomes"); and **not** `pub trait HttpRequest`, `impl MatchedHttp`, `pub trait Publish`, `impl Sink`, the consume, publish, upstream, or delay rows, or the messaging testing row. `customers` carries `TableStore` and no `Identity`, `HttpRequest`, or delay row, and no idiom. `orders-reading` carries `StateStore` and `TableStore`, `A cache-aside read`, and no `A probe`, `HttpRequest`, `Identity`, or delay row. `orders-placement` carries `HttpRequest` with `MatchedHttp` (its text and the shipping paragraph spell `upstream`), `Identity`, `StateStore`, `TableStore`, and the delay row, and no idiom. `orders-mutations` carries `StateStore` and `TableStore` and `A cache-aside read`, and no `HttpRequest`, `Identity`, or delay row. The control carries `HttpRequest`, `Identity`, the consume rows, and the delay row on every one of the five.
- **F3, the brief's size.** Every scaffolded brief is smaller than the control's in bytes, and the five together by **≥ 10%**, the idioms added on `health`, `orders-reading`, and `orders-mutations` within it.
- **F4, `health`'s turn.** Shell calls **≤ 16** against the band's 33, registry `read`s (paths under `.cargo/registry`) **≤ 3** against 13, `read_doc` **≤ 2** against 3, tool calls **≤ 50** against 69; no shell command and no registry `read` spelling `probe`, `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within` against the band's 33; at the label `crates/health/src/health.rs` holds **no `within` fn, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, and no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`**, read by `rg`, the store probed by `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and the cache by `StateStore::get`, each awaited once; REQ-031 covered or left out of `covered` either way, observed, since the translation leaves a requirement whose whole statement is the mechanism out of `covered` and the idiom says the host bounds the call.
- **F5, the guardrails.** Built **7/7** and verified **6/6**, the host's checks `ok` and agreeing with every verdict, scenarios named **59/59**, 0 outside, 0 unreached, 0 probes; `write_files` **0** on every scaffolded turn and none refused by the guard; `edit_files` per marker **≤ 1.5** on every scaffolded turn against the band's 0.60–1.33×; `## Findings` **≤ 1** over the run against the band's 1 (`unused_dependencies`), none of them `markers_left`, `untested_scenarios`, or a `clippy` error naming a type of another stem; the host's `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean at the label.
- **Observed, never decided on:** input and output tokens against the band's 11.72 M and 94.3 K and wall time against 2931 s; tool calls, shell calls, `read_doc`, and registry reads on the four other scaffolded turns against the band's 47/41/78/58, 12/10/29/20, 2/2/2/3, and 0/0/0/0; whether a turn reads another stem's part for a type the cut left out; whether `health`'s turn reads the cache-aside section it was handed for nothing; the kernel's denials.

### Decision rule

- **The cut brief holds** when F1, F2, and F3 hold on the offline measure for every scaffolded slice and F5 holds on the run: the three items ship together and this card is the band in place of `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`. **F4 holding** is the probe idiom's confirmation, read beside them; F4 failing while the rest hold decides nothing against the cut and is read for what the turn went looking for.
- **F1 failing** by a data block kept whose name no text of the slice spells, or left out whose name one spells, is the reader (`design.rs`), the SDK's, the key and the word named; by a mechanism block left out, the role rule, the SDK's; by a laid type without its pointer line, `Scaffold::types()`, the adapter's; by a block kept for a word of the preamble alone (`Customer`, `Order`) is the rule as written, counted and not decided on, the preamble's share recorded.
- **F2 failing** by a passage on a slice none of whose text or cited paragraphs spells a word of its role is the selection, the adapter's, the word named; by a passage absent where the slice reaches the capability — its turn then reading `omnia-api.md` through `read_doc` or the registry for it — is the word list's, the word to add recorded with the slice; an idiom carried where the slice's words name it and no handler of the slice takes its shape is observed, and a tighter trigger is the next round's.
- **F3 failing** by a brief grown on the idioms alone is the idioms' length, observed where F1 and F2 hold; by a brief grown otherwise is the cut not cutting, F1's clause.
- **F4 failing.** A timer, a race, or a `PROBE_TIMEOUT` setting written with the probe section in the brief is the prose's — the section read and not followed where the turn made no `read_doc` of `handlers.md`, not read where it did; a registry read for a capability whose passage the brief carries is the turn re-reading what it was shown, B1's clause; shell calls over the tree for the seed's shape are observed, as before.
- **F5 failing.** A slice not built or a verify refused is read first for the backend (a relay error the engine retried, as the band's 464 s attempt), then for the slice: a `clippy` error naming a type of another stem, or a struct invented for a design type the cut left out, is the cut's, the SDK's, and the left-out line's wording the first repair; a `markers_left` or `untested_scenarios` finding is the fill's, counted under B2; a lint the host finds that the verify passed is the verify's, as before; `edit_files` over 1.5 a marker is read as D3 reads it.
- **A run that opens no completion**, or one the provider refuses, is the backend's and decided first; a `cargo clippy` the shell holds past the rung is the toolchain's, carried by the engine's retry and recorded as the attempts.
- **Read against the band**, `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`: the same plan, model, ladder, rung, and width, so a difference is the three items' and the six example lines'.

### Results

**The offline measure ran first**, over the band's revision `c2c52172` lowered from `emery show` and the three JSON documents of `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, each slice built in plan order into one tree the init seeded, the control the `808aa9f` worktree over the released SDK (`e0428af`) and the arm this tree over the engine's working tree, the two `start` slices opening no turn on either. **The arm ran on `2026-10-20T20-45-10Z`**, `--rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders` under `composer-2.5`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, the ladder 1200/400 → 2400/480, the build rung 1200/400, the component built from this tree with the round's changes then uncommitted — the adapter as `86432ff` holds it, committed after the run, over the SDK at the engine's `4f72c524` (`emery_omnia.wasm`, 4,226,111 bytes) — and the release `emery` 0.39.0 as built: **built 7/7 in 6 waves, 6/6 verified, 1564 s** against the band's 2931 s, `ok 63/63` and 59/59 scenarios named at `ce72a3eb`, no conflict and no `resolved` path, `covered` 46/61 as the band's; build turns 11 completions (the band's 12 held `health`'s retried attempt), 15.60 M input (15.01 M cached) and 128.7 K output, read below.

- **F1 holds on every structural clause and fails on both magnitudes.** Every scaffolded brief opens its design under `The design, cut to this slice:`; the eleven `(mechanism)` blocks stand whole on all five; the pointer lines are `customers` 3 (`Customer`, `CustomerTier`, `CreateCustomer`), `orders-reading` 4 (`Order`, `OrderLine`, `ListQuery`, `Page`), `orders-placement` 6 (`Address`, `Totals`, `PlaceOrder`, `Catalogue`, `DeliveryZone`, `ShippingQuote`), `orders-mutations` 5 (`OrderState`, `Transition`, `AmendLines`, `TRANSITIONS`, `TERMINAL_STATES`), `health` 0; no brief's design carries a laid type's signature; `Customer` and `Order` stand on every brief, whole or as a pointer; the closing line lists `9`, `7`, `5`, and `5` keys on the four (`≥ 4`). On `health` it lists **11**, not 16: `Address`, `OrderLine`, `OrderState`, `PlaceOrder`, and `ListQuery` stand beside `Customer` and `Order`, each spelled by no requirement but by the brief's own prose read as a bag of words — `address` in the probe passage's "a store's address", `line` in the scaffold section's "one `<crate>.workspace = true` line", `states` in a scenario's "the JSON body states whether", `place` in "in place of `get(health_live)`", `list` in "in its lists", `query` in the capabilities passage's `fn query` and the routing passage's "query string" — so `health`'s design section is 15,364 bytes against the control's 16,486, **1,122 below**, not 2,500; the four others' are 15,505, 15,604, 15,839, and 15,936 (981, 882, 647, and 550 below). The rule's clause for a block kept by a word of the preamble alone is the reading: the reader did as written, the words are the scaffold brief's and the passages', which `design.rs` reads whole, and the preamble's own share is `Customer` and `Order`, as predicted.
- **F2 holds whole.** `health`'s capabilities passage holds `Config`, `Identity`, `StateStore`, and `TableStore` and no `HttpRequest`; its doubles passage `Provider` and `Statement` without `Recorded` or `impl MatchedHttp`; its testing rows `Config`, `StateStore`/`BlobStore`, `Identity`, and `TableStore` without `HttpRequest`; its translation rows the vault and the store alone, no consume, broker, upstream, or delay row; `A probe` (2,583 bytes) and `A cache-aside read` (2,172 bytes) both quoted after the capabilities. `customers`, `orders-reading`, and `orders-mutations` hold `Config`, `StateStore`, and `TableStore`, the store row, and the two design-type rows, no `Identity`, `HttpRequest`, or delay row, `A cache-aside read` on the two `orders` slices and no idiom on `customers`; `orders-placement` holds all five traits with `impl MatchedHttp` and `Recorded`, the vault, upstream, store, and delay rows, and no idiom. The control carries `HttpRequest`, `Identity`, the two consume rows, the broker row, and the delay row on all five. (`MatchedHttp`, `FixedIdentity`, and `Sink` still appear on every brief as fields of `pub struct Provider`, the doubles passage's one struct, which the selection does not cut.)
- **F3 holds on every brief and fails on the sum.** Control → arm, bytes: `health` 57,194 → 54,677 (−4.4%), `customers` 68,024 → 59,865 (−12.0%), `orders-reading` 67,041 → 61,153 (−8.8%), `orders-placement` 74,853 → 70,988 (−5.2%), `orders-mutations` 85,405 → 79,849 (−6.5%); the five together 352,517 → 326,532, **−7.4%**, not 10%. The scaffold section is unchanged (17,744 / 25,971 / 26,468 / 30,906 / 40,392); from the first passage to the brief's end the control's 15,547 / 18,181 / 16,359 / 18,203 / 18,214 became 14,140 / 10,991 / 11,341 / 14,973 / 13,196, so the role trim took 6,162 bytes off `health` and the two idioms put 4,755 back, and the design cut took 550–1,122 off each: F3's shortfall is F1's.
- **F4 holds on every clause but two.** `health`'s turn (`n=2`) made **33** tool calls against the band's 69 (`≤ 50`), **9** shell calls against 33 (`≤ 16`), **0** registry reads against 13 (`≤ 3`), and **4** `read_doc` against 3 (`≤ 2` fails: `handlers.md`, `testing.md`, `layout.md`, `omnia-api.md`). No shell command or registry read spells `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`; one shell command spells `probe` — `rg -l "DATABASE|SELECT 1|health/probe" …/crates`, a search of the tree for the slice's own key, not a timer — against the band's 14 that did (`rg "timeout|sleep|Timer|within"` over `omnia-sdk-0.36.0/src`, `rg "fn within|async fn probe|PROBE_TIMEOUT"` over the registry), recounted from the log, where the round's "33" above counted every shell call. At `ce72a3eb` `crates/health/src/health.rs` is 112 lines against the band's 183 and holds **no `within` fn, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, and no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`** (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` survives in the `//!` and the REQ-031 marker line alone, as the specification spells it); the store is probed by `TableStore::query(provider, DATABASE, "SELECT 1", ..)` awaited once with a non-empty row set the pass, the cache by `StateStore::get(provider, "health/probe")` awaited once with `is_ok()` the pass — the probe idiom's shape line for line. REQ-031 is in `covered` (8/8).
- **F5 holds on every clause but one.** Built **7/7**, verified **6/6**, the host's `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (63 passed), and `cargo build` `ok 63/63` and agreeing with every verdict, scenarios named **59/59**, 0 outside, 0 unreached, 0 probes; `write_files` **0** on every turn and none refused; `## Findings` **1** over the run (`≤ 1`) — `empty_misses` on `orders-placement`, `crates/orders/src/placement.rs:304: a miss answered as no entries (Ok(Vec::new())) after the read at line 301`, repaired in the correction round with 2 edits — none `markers_left`, `untested_scenarios`, or a `clippy` error naming another stem's type; `edit_files` per marker **7/10 (0.70×), 19/14 (1.36×), 17/15 (1.13×), 23/15 (1.53×), 18/23 (0.78×; Round E counted 22 markers)** — `orders-placement`'s 1.53× fails `≤ 1.5`, 21 edits (1.40×) before the finding and 2 after it, read as D3 reads it; a `find` refused 2 over the run (the `customers` and `placement` test files, each matching 4 places) against the band's 4.
- **Observed.** Wall **1564 s** against 2931 s: build turns 144/185/204/305/226 s against the band's 314/231/205/334/400, verifies 77/78/74/83/82/85 s against 411/107/109/115/109/110, serial 1547 s against 2913 s. Tokens are not read whole against the band: the band's `build-SLICE-003` first attempt (463 s, 9 edits, ended by the relay) recorded 0 and its `build-SLICE-007` (400 s, 18 edits) 329.5 K input and 828 output, so its 11.72 M is short by two turns; turn for turn where both are whole, input `health` 1.257 → 1.374 M, `customers` 2.537 → 2.420 M, `orders-reading` 1.807 → 2.838 M, `orders-placement` 4.958 → 4.867 M, and output 10.4 → 11.1 K, 20.4 → 19.6 K, 19.1 → 26.4 K, 34.9 → 35.9 K, the arm's `orders-mutations` 3.536 M and 29.1 K; the verifies 79.1 / 104.1 / 81.1 / 105.1 / 105.5 / 91.6 K against 211.8 / 103.9 / 105.0 / 196.2 / 107.5 / 107.7 K. A turn's input is its rounds times its context, so the brief's 7.4% sits below one tool call more or fewer. The four other scaffolded turns made 50/48/67/51 tool calls against the band's 47/41/78/58, shell 13/14/19/18 against 12/10/29/20, `read_doc` 2/3/3/2 against 2/2/2/3, registry reads 0 on every one as on the band. No turn searched for a type the cut left out: `orders-placement` grepped `DeliveryZone|ZONES|catalogue|SHIPPING_API` and `orders-mutations` `TERMINAL|TRANSIT…`, each for the types its pointer lines said the adapter had laid; `customers` read `health.rs` and the `orders` slices `customers.rs`, as the band's did, a filled part read for its shape. `orders-mutations` widened eight of `placement.rs`'s helpers to `pub(crate)` and reused them, 10 lines of `reading.rs` beside, within its stem. The stems' sources and tests at the label are 3,922 lines against 3,937. Whether `health` read the cache-aside section it was handed cannot be told from the log; its `read_doc` of `handlers.md` whole read both idioms again. The log holds no `WARN`, no `ERROR`, and no denial; the unified-log reading was not taken.

**Decision — the rule as written does not confirm the cut brief, and nothing in the run counts against it.** F2 holds whole and every structural clause of F1 holds; the two magnitudes — 16 keys out and 2,500 bytes on `health`, 10% on the sum — were mispredicted, and F3's shortfall is F1's: `design.rs` reads the scaffold's brief with its passages as the slice's words, so five data types stand on `health` for `line`, `lists`, `in place`, `query`, and `address`. F4 confirms the probe idiom — 33 tool calls, 9 shell, 0 registry reads, no timer, and a `health.rs` of 112 lines against 183 — and F5 holds but for one corrected turn at 1.53×. By the rule, `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z` stays the band and whether the three items ship as they stand is the operator's reading; the next round's item is the elision's grain — the slice's text read as the plan entry, the specification cut, the owned signatures, and the scaffold section alone, never the passages, and a key's words matched as a phrase rather than a bag — with the two magnitudes predicted again over the same offline measure.

### The small-model arm

The protocol's pairing ([cards/README.md](README.md#protocol), "An omnia card is a pair") was written after the large-model arm above had run and before this arm was built, and this round is the first under it. The arm is the same `--rebuild` under `emery-dev` and `qwen3-coder:30b`: the component the large-model arm ran (the adapter as `86432ff` holds it, `emery_omnia.wasm`, 4,226,111 bytes), the `emery-dev` C1 ran (built Oct 20 11:12 from the engine's working tree over `009fa2b1` under `--features genai`, `omnia-genai` from `omnia-backends` at `0f55ae8` plus its uncommitted final-round change, which that checkout still holds), and C1's knobs — `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MODEL=qwen3-coder:30b` at `GENAI_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:11434/`, Ollama 0.40.6 serving `qwen3-coder:30b` under `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=131072`, `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`, `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, and `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=1`, the tool-call, round, and candidate caps at their defaults. It is read against C1, `2026-10-19T22-47-32Z`, the small-model band: `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` opening no turn, wave 1's verify sealing over the seed, `health` built and merged covering 4/8, wave 2's verify passed and labelled, and the run stopped by hand during `SLICE-004`. What differs from C1 is the register (Round D), the laid types (Round E), the cut brief with the idioms (this round), and the six example lines, together: the component is the shipped state and not one item, so this arm says what the small model does with it and attributes nothing between the four, which the large-model pairs D0, E, and F did; a pure C2, the register alone, would need the `808aa9f` component and is not run. The clauses are Round D's D2–D4 and this round's F4, re-read for the small model.

#### Predictions

- **S1, the waves.** Every scaffolded slice reaches its turn; no completion ends `exhausted` (the tool-call cap, 128) or `timeout` (7200 s), and none dies in the shell at 600 s; the build reaches past C1's stop — `customers` (`SLICE-004`) merged and wave 3 verified — and **built 7/7 and verified 6/6** with the host's checks agreeing is the figure the band asks, as C7 asked it.
- **S2, `health`.** `health` covers **≥ 6/8** against C1's 4/8, the two `[conflict]` requirements (REQ-017, REQ-018) the ones left out if any; `crates/health/src/health.rs` at wave 2's seal holds no `within` fn, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, and no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`, the store probed by `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and the cache by `StateStore::get`, each awaited once — the probe idiom's shape, as the large-model arm laid it; `health`'s turn makes **no registry read**, since the brief carries the capability passages, and no shell command spelling `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`.
- **S3, the edits.** `edit_files` per marker **≤ 1.5** on every scaffolded turn that ran; a `find` refused **≤ 1** per scaffolded turn against B1b's 3; `write_files` **0** on every scaffolded turn and none refused by the scaffold guard; no file written through the shell's redirection around the guard (B1b's `echo '…' >`), read from the shell commands.
- **S4, the verdicts.** Every verify completion's first reply is JSON — the backend's `nudging` line **0** over the run — and no build completion's reply is nudged; the final round stands unused.
- **S5, the turn's load.** Tool calls per scaffolded turn **≤ 90**, under the cap with room, against B1b's 75 on a `start` turn that now opens none; shell calls **≤ 20** a turn against B1b's 44, the one check command re-run no more than twice between edits; `read_doc` **≤ 5** a turn and `list_docs` 1.
- **Observed, never decided on:** tokens and the server's prompt evaluations; wall time against the large-model arm's 1564 s and the turns' `model_ms`; which references each turn reads; what the verifies seal; the kernel's denials.

#### Decision rule

- **The cut brief holds under the small model** when S1 holds through wave 3's verify at the least with no `exhausted`, `timeout`, or shell death, and S3 and S4 hold on every scaffolded turn that ran: this card is the small-model band in place of C1. **S1 reaching 7/7 and 6/6** with S2–S5 holding is the figure the band asks, and the small model's build is confirmed under the shipped state. S2 holding is the probe idiom's confirmation under the small model, read beside; S5 is the load's reading.
- **S1 failing** is read by its phase, as C7's clause reads it: a `timeout` whose `model_ms` is near the cap is the model's under this register and brief; a shell death at 600 s is the toolchain's, put again at `7200/1200` with the raise recorded; `exhausted` at 128 calls is the turn's load, S5's clause, read for what the calls were spent on (the check command re-run, files re-read, a `find` refused and spelled again); a verify refused is read for the finding it names.
- **S2 failing** by a timer or a `PROBE_TIMEOUT` written with the probe section in the brief is the prose's under the small model — the section read and not followed where the turn made no `read_doc` of `handlers.md`, not read where it did; by `health` covering 4/8 or fewer, the requirements left out named and read for what the turn did with them.
- **S3 failing** is read as D3's clause reads it; a write around the guard through the shell is the guard's reach, the SDK's, recorded. **S4 failing** is read as D2's clause reads it.
- **A server that cannot be started**, a model not pulled, or the machine held by another run is the arm not put: recorded here, the round not decided under the small model until it has run.
- **Read against C1** for what the small model reaches, and against the large-model arm of this round, `2026-10-20T20-45-10Z`, for the model's own difference, since the plan, the component, and every knob but the model's are one.

#### Results

Card `2026-10-20T21-42-12Z`, the recipe above as written, the server started for the arm and stopped after it. **Built 0/7, verified 1/6**, the run ending after 2395 s as `bad_request` at `SLICE-003` (`health`) in wave 2: wave 1 verified and labelled over the seed (`Cargo.lock` its one sealed file, `97303609`), `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` merged with no turn, and `health`'s build `exhausted` after 1044 s. Two completions, 5.32 M input tokens and 11.7 K output, no cache read by the backend's count, while the server's prefix cache held through most rounds (the largest context 53,771 tokens, nothing truncated) and prompt evaluation still took 1,562 s of the 2,395: eleven re-evaluations of more than 5,000 tokens, 1,063 s between them, the two largest the closing rounds after each nudge — 48,791 of the verify's 52,544 tokens (301 s) and 37,388 of the build's 43,070 (217 s), everything past the system prompt, since withdrawing the tools rewrites the prompt from the tool section on — and the other nine at no nudge, with a cause the server's log does not name. The arm stops short of C1, which merged `health` covering 4/8 and verified wave 2, so **C1 stays the small-model band**.

- **S1 fails**, in a phase the rule did not name. The build died in the candidate budget, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES` 2, not the round or guest-tool caps (28 rounds of 128, 17 guest tool calls of 128), the turn's timeout (1044 s of 7200), or the shell (the loop command's longest run 35 s). The three attempts, read as the clause asks: at round 21, after its last edit and with no check run after it, the model replied in prose (227 bytes), and the backend nudged it in a final round with the tools withdrawn; the candidate that round answered (167 bytes) covered all eight requirements over a test file it had not touched, and the adapter's `untested_scenarios` refused it, naming each ignored test and its marker; the correction gave the tools back, and the model filled five tests in five rounds, one `edit_files` each (REQ-016, 017, 018, 027, 028), then at round 28 replied with JSON of a shape of its own (913 bytes, `invalid type: map, expected a sequence`, the text not logged), which the SDK's `Question::ask` cannot read as a `Report` and, the budget spent, reports as `schema and answer type disagree` `bad_request`, the class it keeps for a guest whose type and schema disagree. No large-model card has produced such a candidate; under Ollama a plain reply in a tool round is held to no schema, so a small model's mis-shaped answer is read as the adapter's defect. An engine reading, recorded below.
- **S2 holds in shape and fails in coverage.** `crates/health/src/health.rs` as the turn left it (78 lines, against the large-model arm's 112 and the large-model band's 183) holds no `within`, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`: the store is probed by `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and the cache by `StateStore::get` of a probe key, each awaited once, `status` set from the store alone and the two outcomes in the body, the probe idiom's shape laid in three `edit_files` calls in the turn's first two minutes. The turn made no registry read and no shell command spelling `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`; its one `read_doc` was `omnia-api.md`. But nothing merged: `health` covers 0/8 against C1's 4/8, the turn's own report claiming 8/8 and refused. The handler also answers 200 on a degraded store, where its own tests expect 503, and the five tests it filled all fail, four on a double arranged with no `on_query` rule for `SELECT 1` and one on a byte literal spelled `br#"{\"status\":\"ok\"}"#`, the escapes kept in the raw string; the three tests of REQ-029, 030, and 031 stayed ignored.
- **S3 fails narrowly.** `edit_files` 16 calls over the slice's 10 markers (2 handlers, 8 tests), 1.6 per marker against the 1.5 asked, 11 of them on `health.rs` under the loop's clippy findings (five loop runs, every one `exit 101`, the last at 22:15:59 followed by one more edit and the prose reply, so the turn never saw its tree pass; it does pass clippy as left). A `find` refused twice, against the 1 asked, both re-spelling a handler head the earlier edits had already changed, and both answered with the line to copy. `write_files` 0 and nothing written around the guard: the shell ran the loop command and nothing else.
- **S4 fails.** `nudging` 2: the verify's first reply at round 105 and the build's at round 21 were prose, and the final round was used both times.
- **S5 holds on the build and fails on the verify.** The build: 25 tool calls (16 `edit_files`, 5 `shell`, 3 `read`, 1 `read_doc`, no `list_docs`, the 1 written read as at most one). The verify over the seed: 167 tool calls, 150 of them shell, in 106 rounds and 1345 s, none of them a guest tool but one `read_doc`, so no cap touched it. Its five checks passed at the first run, within the first 90 s, with `Cargo.lock` the one file written, and the verdict was due at round 2; instead the turn re-ran the five five more times, read `layout.md`, and spent a hundred rounds searching the tree for `ORDER_PLACED`, `order.*placed`, `PLACE`, and `start` — names from that reference's examples — the same `find`/`grep` repeated round after round, two or three to a round in a third of them, until a prose reply ended the rounds and the nudge drew the verdict. The example read as a fact of the tree, and a verify with nothing to repair that does not answer, are the two small-model readings of the verify prompt.
- **Observed.** Wall 2395 s for one verify and one failed build against the large-model arm's 1564 s for the whole plan; `model_ms` 1,252,832 of the verify's 1,345,307 and 1,003,238 of the build's 1,044,639, so the model's generation and the prompt re-evaluations are the time, the tools 92 s and 41 s. No `WARN` or `ERROR` but the exhaustion; no denial; the host's checks pass the integration copy's head (`run-1.fidelity.md`, scenarios named 0/77 since nothing built). The tree the run left — the label at wave 1, `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` merged, `health`'s working copy with its five filled tests — is what a re-run resumes from.

**Decision.** The rule's first clause does not hold: S1 fails before wave 3, so this card does not replace C1, and the band under the small model stays `2026-10-19T22-47-32Z`. What the rule does read: the cut brief and the laid types reached the small model (S2's shape, in two minutes, with no hunting — the probe section was followed where the large-model band's turn wrote a timer), and the small model's failures lie outside the brief, in its answer discipline under the backend's budgets: a prose reply where JSON was asked, twice; a report before the tests, refused; a shape of its own at the last candidate; a verify that will not stop once its checks pass. Three items follow, each the small-model arm's and none the large-model band's:

- the backend's candidate budget for the small-model arm, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES` 3 or 4 as a named knob of the recipe, since the correction is where this model did its best work — five tests in five rounds the moment the check named them — and the second candidate is where it ran out;
- the reading of a mis-shaped candidate on the last attempt: `Question::ask` reports it as `schema and answer type disagree`, a guest defect, and the backend could hold a plain JSON reply to the request's schema before offering it to the check, as its closing round's JSON mode does, nudging in place of spending a candidate. An item for `omnia` and `omnia-backends`, not this adapter;
- the verify's stop: the prompt could say in one line that a verify whose checks all pass at the first run answers at once with nothing to repair, and the reference examples' topic names (`ORDER_PLACED`) are what a small model searched the seed for. Checked under the small model before it ships, as the protocol now asks.

Four readings beside them, each from one figure of this card and none a clause the rule decided on:

- **The closing round costs the prefix.** The two nudged rounds re-evaluated everything past the system prompt, 518 s of the run, because the backend withdraws the tools and the prompt is rewritten from its tool section on. On Ollama, JSON mode silences tool calls by itself — the backend's own comment on its closing round says so — so the tools could stay declared in that round and the prefix would hold. One for `omnia-backends`, read against the server's log beside the candidate item, since the other nine re-evaluations fall at no nudge and the log does not say why.
- **The probe passage was followed in the handler and not in its test.** `handlers.md`'s `## A probe` carries three parts: the awaited call, the encoder answering `503` where the store failed, and the test's arrangement, `.on_query(|sql, _| sql == "SELECT 1", vec![])` the store down and one `Row` the store up. The small model laid the first from it, left the handler answering `200` on a degraded store, and filled four tests over a double arranged with no `on_query` rule at all, so every one panicked in `ScriptedTables`. Where the slice's words select the idiom, the scaffold could lay the double's arrangement into the test skeleton as it lays the handler's marker, or the brief could carry the passage's test half beside its handler half; either is the adapter's, checked under the small model.
- **The test skeleton invites a byte-literal assertion.** The fifth failure was `assert_eq!(body, br#"{\"status\":\"ok\"}"#)`, the escapes kept inside a raw string. A `json(body)` helper in the scaffolded test file, comparing through `serde_json::Value`, removes the trap for every model, and the large-model arm's tests would read the same through it.
- **No pure C2.** A small-model arm over the `808aa9f` component would attribute this card's result between the register, the laid types, and the cut brief, and the rule did not ask for it: the small model's failures lie in its answer discipline, which none of the three touches. The next small-model arm is the pair of the next large-model card, so the two bands move together, and this card's conflation is read once, here.

### The small-model arm, qwen3-coder-next

A third arm of this round, written before it was built, under the pairing: the same `--rebuild` over the same component (the adapter as `86432ff` holds it, `emery_omnia.wasm`, 4,226,111 bytes), the same `emery-dev` (C1's build), and the same knobs as `2026-10-20T21-42-12Z` — `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, the tool-call, round, and candidate caps at their defaults (128, 128, 2) — with the model and the machine changed and nothing else: `qwen3-coder-next:q8_0` (the `qwen3next` family, 79.7B, Q8_0, 78 GiB) served by Ollama 0.40.6 on a RunPod pod with one NVIDIA H200 (`kfon5fxhta2bcr`, `runpod/pytorch:1.0.2-cu1281-torch280-ubuntu2404`, 24 vCPU, 251 GB), the server as the operator started it — `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=262144`, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`, `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`, `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=1`, the weights under `/dev/shm` — reached through an SSH tunnel on `127.0.0.1:11435` as `GENAI_ENDPOINT`, so the model call leaves the machine and the tools do not. The difference against `21-42-12Z` is the model's alone, since the plan, the component, the binary, and every knob but the server's context length (262,144 against 131,072, both past the largest context any arm reached, 53,771) are one; C1 is the 30b's earlier reading. The clauses are the `21-42-12Z` section's, re-read for a model two and a half times the size on a machine without the Mac's prompt-evaluation cost.

#### Predictions

- **S1, the waves.** Every scaffolded slice reaches its turn, no completion ends `exhausted` or `timeout`, none dies in the shell, and the build reaches **7/7 built and 6/6 verified** with the host's checks agreeing: the figure the band asks, and the one the 30b reached neither on the Mac nor under C1.
- **S2, `health`.** `health` is accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 6/8**, the two `[conflict]` requirements the ones left out if any; `crates/health/src/health.rs` at wave 2's seal holds no `within`, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`, the store probed by `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and the cache by `StateStore::get`, each awaited once; its probe tests arrange `ScriptedTables` with an `on_query` rule, as the brief's probe passage says; the turn makes **no registry read** and no shell command spelling `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`.
- **S3, the edits.** `edit_files` per marker **≤ 1.5** on every scaffolded turn; a `find` refused **≤ 1** per turn; `write_files` **0** and nothing written around the guard through the shell.

- **S5, the turn's load.** The seed verify answers its verdict within **3 rounds** of its five checks passing, with no search of the tree for a reference example's names; tool calls **≤ 40** a build turn and shell calls **≤ 10**, the loop command re-run no more than twice between edits; `read_doc` ≤ 5 a turn.
- **Observed, never decided on:** wall time against the large-model arm's 1564 s and the 30b's 2395 s for its two turns; the server's generation rate and prompt-evaluation seconds against the Mac's 1,562 s; tokens; VRAM; the tunnel's stability over the run; which references each turn reads; what the verifies seal.

#### Decision rule

- **S1 holding with S3 and S4** makes this card the small-model band in place of C1, and moves the recipe's small-model arm to `qwen3-coder-next` on the pod — the README's recipe, the protocol bullet, and the `AGENTS.md` row — with the local 30b kept as the fallback where no pod is up. S2 and S5 holding are the probe idiom's and the load's confirmation under it, read beside.
- **S1 failing** is read by its phase as C7's clause reads it: a `timeout` with `model_ms` near the cap is the model's; an `exhausted` on the candidate budget is read for the candidates as the `21-42-12Z` section read them; a shell death is the toolchain's, put again at `7200/1200`; a verify refused is read for its finding. Where the arm still reaches past `21-42-12Z` — `health` merged, wave 2 verified — the recipe moves to the pod all the same, since it is the faster machine either way, and the card is the small-model band from where it stands.
- **S2–S5 failing** are read as the `21-42-12Z` section's clauses read them, each against that card's figure, so the model's own difference is named per clause.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the arm not put, or put as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the run resumed from the label where the engine allows.
- **Read against `21-42-12Z`** for the model's own difference and against C1 for the 30b's history; the large-model arm of this round, `2026-10-20T20-45-10Z`, is the figure a small model is measured toward.

#### Results

Card `2026-10-21T05-21-29Z`, the recipe above as written, the tunnel on `11435` up for the run and the server as the operator left it. **Built 0/7, verified 1/6**, the run ending after 1811 s as `bad_request` at `SLICE-003` (`health`) in wave 2: wave 1 verified and labelled over the seed (`Cargo.lock` its one sealed file, `d5ee6bac`), `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` merged with no turn, and `health` built twice, the first attempt ended by the backend as an `error` after 285 s and the second `exhausted` after 1328 s at the round cap, `no answer after 128 model round-trips`. Three completions, 11.56 M input tokens and 46.4 K output; on the server 287 s of prompt evaluation over 352 K tokens (1,226 tokens a second, against the Mac's 1,562 s for the 30b's two turns) and 986 s of generation over 46,386 tokens (47.0 a second: 53.5 under 60 K of context, 39.5 past 100 K), twenty re-evaluations of more than 5,000 tokens costing 81 s together, the largest context 125,825 tokens and nothing truncated, 85.8 GB of the H200 held. The arm stops where `21-42-12Z` stopped, so **C1 stays the small-model band**.

- **S1 fails**, in a phase neither section named: the round cap, reached on the second attempt, after a first the backend ended. The first attempt (22 rounds, 23 tool calls) filled the handler in four edits 35 s in and the eight tests in eleven more by its second minute — each probe test arranging `ScriptedTables::default().on_query(..)` as the passage says, the body read through `serde_json`, and `Row`, `Field`, and `DataType` imported from `omnia_test::guest`, where they are not — then turned to the seed: four `edit_files` and one `write_files` over `crates/common/src/lib.rs`, adding `table_store_query` and `state_store_get` helpers and a `DataType` enum of its own over the SDK's, the file deleted and rewritten whole (5,096 bytes) through a tool the guard holds to the slice's scaffolded paths alone. At round 22 it called a tool named `run` for the shell. The host's session fails a completion for a tool the request does not declare (`model called `run`, which the request does not declare as a function tool`) rather than answering the call, so the attempt ended with no check run, the engine put the slice once more in a fresh copy, and the filled tests went with the first. The second attempt (128 rounds, 128 tool calls: 26 `read`, 5 `read_doc`, 45 `edit_files`, 44 `shell`, 7 `write_files`, 1 `list`) laid the handler in six edits at 05:30:38 and spent the next sixteen minutes on one function: a route over `handle_with` with the passage's `503` encoder, the closure bounds and a missing `IntoResponse` import fought through thirty runs of `cargo clippy -p health` between edits — `edit_files` refused twelve times (five `find`s matching nothing, one matching twice, six calls whose arguments were not JSON) and `write_files` seven times by the scaffold guard — until at 05:47:56 it went around the guard through the shell: `rm -f crates/health/src/health.rs`, the file rewritten whole by a heredoc, `echo 'pub mod health;' > crates/health/src/lib.rs` over the stem's entry, its `router()` and `topics()` gone with it, and `sed -i` edits, the first in GNU's form the Mac's `sed` refused, until the cap at 05:51:39. The tests were not touched in this attempt: all eight stand `#[ignore]`. As left, `health.rs` (105 lines) fails clippy on its two `into_response` calls alone, the import the last `sed -i '' '1a …'` did not add.
- **S2 holds in shape and fails in coverage.** `crates/health/src/health.rs` as the second attempt left it holds no `within`, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant (the words in the `//!` and the marker line alone, as the specification spells them), no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`: the store probed by `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` awaited once with a non-empty row set the pass, the cache by `StateStore::get` of a probe key awaited once with `is_ok()`. The turn made no registry read and no shell command spelling `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`; its five `read_doc` were `handlers.md` three times and `omnia-api.md` twice. But the readiness output carries `store` and `cache` and no `status`, every answer is `200` since the encoder never compiled, and nothing merged: `health` covers 0/8 against C1's 4/8, as on `21-42-12Z`.
- **S3 fails on every clause.** `edit_files` 45 calls and 58 edits over the slice's 10 markers on the second attempt, 4.5 calls a marker against the 1.5 asked (the first attempt's 8 calls and 21 edits were 0.8 a marker); a `find` refused 6 times on the second attempt and twice on the first against the 1 asked; `write_files` 7, every one refused by the guard; and the scaffolded module and the stem's entry written around the guard through the shell, the first card to show it.
- **S4 holds.** `nudging` 0: the verify's answer at its round 46 was JSON at the first reply, and neither build attempt replied in prose.
- **S5 fails on both.** The seed verify answered its verdict at round 46 after 191 s, 33 rounds after its five checks passed at their first run (by 05:23:13, `Cargo.lock` the one file written): the five run once more, then eleven `ls` and `find` over the tree and the build directories, no `read_doc` and no search for a reference's names — 45 tool calls, 25 of them shell, against the 30b's 167 and 1345 s. The build's second attempt made 128 tool calls against the 40 asked and 44 shell calls against the 10, the loop command re-run up to four times between edits; `read_doc` 5, at the cap asked.
- **Observed.** Wall 1811 s for one verify and two build attempts against the 30b's 2395 s for one verify and one attempt that ended at round 28; `model_ms` 123,610 of the verify's 191,184 (its shell 67,521), 285,217 of the first attempt's 285,298, and 1,249,130 of the second's 1,328,971 (its tools 79,841). The server's prefix cache held: the median re-evaluation was 303 tokens in the verify, 970 in the first attempt, and 697 in the second, and no round re-evaluated the brief. The tunnel held the whole run, no `BadGateway` and no reconnect; no `WARN` but the two outcomes, no denial; the host's checks pass the integration copy's head (`run-1.fidelity.md`, scenarios named 0/77). The tree the run left — the label at wave 1, `SLICE-001` and `SLICE-002` merged, `health`'s copy with its rewritten module and bare entry — is what a re-run resumes from.

**Decision.** The rule's first clause does not hold, and neither does its second: S1 fails before wave 3 and the arm does not reach past `21-42-12Z`, so this card does not replace C1 and the rule as written moves nothing. What it reads, against `21-42-12Z` with the model the one difference: the stronger model fails in a different place. The 30b replied in prose, reported before its tests, and ran out of candidates; this model answered JSON every time and never needed the final round, laid the probe's shape in its first minute both times, arranged the test double as the passage says, and then lost both attempts to the toolchain and the tools — a wrapper it wanted in the seed's crate, a tool name it invented, a route function it could not make compile from one sentence and a fragment, and, refused by the guard, the shell. Three of the seven items the round after this one carries meet it directly: the probe's second fence is the route function it fought, the `send` prelude is the body it parsed by hand, and the candidate knob is moot where no candidate was spent. Two items are new, and neither is this adapter's prose: a tool the request does not declare should come back to the model as a refusal naming the tools it has, not end the completion (omnia's host session, or the backend before it); and a build turn should be held to the seed's static files as the verify is (`Tree::static_drift` in `check_build`), since the first attempt rewrote `crates/common/src/lib.rs` whole and nothing at the build would have said so. A third reading is the guard's reach: the scaffold guard holds the SDK's tools and the shell is omnia's; the adapter's `check` could hold the stem's entry to its shape (`router()` and `topics()` present) so a shell rewrite is a finding and not a verify's compile failure. The recipe's move to the pod is not the rule's to make on this card; the next round makes it on its own grounds, stated there — a machine whose prompt evaluation is five times the Mac's reads the model's discipline rather than the machine's, and the 30b's arm never finished on the Mac either — with the local 30b the fallback, and this card is what the next small-model arm is read against, the model held constant.

## Round G — the elision's grain and the small model's discipline

[Round F](#round-f--the-cut-brief) left seven items across three checkouts, and this round lands them together, each uncommitted for the maintainer. In the engine's SDK (`crates/sdk`): `target::Scaffold` gains `passages`, the text an adapter quotes after its scaffold section, which `Brief::fmt` renders after the guard sentence and no gate reads, so the design's cut (`design.rs`) reads the slice's words as the plan entry, the specification cut, the owned signatures, and the scaffold's brief alone; and `names` matches a key's identifier as a phrase — `OrderLine` is `order line` in that order, a plural tolerated per word — in place of the bag of words that kept `OrderState` on `health` for a scenario's "states whether". In the omnia adapter: `Scaffold::texts(docs)` answers the brief and the passages apart under the one `INLINE_BYTES` budget, the files first, and `lib.rs` hands the passages to the SDK; `handlers.md`'s `## A probe` carries its test half as a second fence after the handler — the route function over `handle_with` with the `503` encoder, whole with the `use` lines it needs, and the two tests arranging `ScriptedTables::default().on_query(|sql, _| sql == "SELECT 1", ..)`, no row the store down and one `Row { index: "1".into(), fields: Vec::new() }` the store up — compiled and run under the template's lints before it was quoted, since the `decode_json` an earlier draft of the fence named is no function of the pinned SDK; the scaffolded test prelude's `send` answers `(StatusCode, serde_json::Value)`, `Value::Null` for an empty body and the text as a `Value::String` where it is not JSON, so a test asserts `body["status"]` or `serde_json::from_value(body)` and never a byte literal; and `verify.md` says where the turn stops — every check passing at its first run with the fifth naming `Cargo.lock` at most is nothing to repair, the passing verdict due in the next reply, and a name a reference's example uses is the example's, not searched for in the tree. In `omnia-backends` (`crates/genai`): a JSON reply that parses but fails the request's schema is nudged as a malformed one is, the faults named by path, so a mis-shaped candidate costs a round and not a candidate and the SDK's `schema and answer type disagree` is unreachable from a model's slip; and the closing round keeps the tools declared under JSON mode, so the server's prefix holds through it, a tool call made in it answered with a refusal naming the JSON answer as what is due. The knob: `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3` on the small-model arm, the second candidate being where the 30b ran out on `21-42-12Z`. Nothing of the engine's own changed, so the release `emery` 0.39.0 stands and the component and `emery-dev` are rebuilt: `emery_omnia.wasm` from this tree over the SDK of the engine's working tree through the `[patch.crates-io]` path entries, and `emery-dev` from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-genai` checkout's working tree through its path line, uncommented for the build and re-commented after.

Two arms, each the protocol's `--rebuild` of `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z` over `express-orders` — the band's plan (`c2c52172`, seven slices in six waves `1/2/1/1/1/1`), ladder, and rung — written here before the component was built. The large-model arm, `EVAL_RUNS=1 cargo run -p evals -- --rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z --target omnia express-orders` under `composer-2.5`, is read against the band, `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, and against Round F's arm, `2026-10-20T20-45-10Z`, for the items' own difference, since the model and every knob are one. The small-model arm runs on the pod under the recipe [evals/README.md](../README.md#the-small-model-arm) now states, read against `2026-10-21T05-21-29Z`, Round F's third arm, where the model, the machine, and the plan are the same and the seven items and the knob are the difference; its predictions are the section after the large arm's. The brief is read offline as Round F read it: each scaffolded slice's brief rendered natively through `Adapter::run` over the band's committed revision, once over Round F's arm tree (the adapter as `86432ff` holds it over the SDK at the engine's `4f72c524`, the control) and once over this tree, the slices built in plan order into one seeded tree; the backend logs no brief, so G1 is decided on that measure, and its figures below are the measure's, taken before the live arm.

### Predictions

- **G1, the elision's grain.** On the offline measure, `health`'s closing line lists **16** keys, the eleven `(mechanism)` blocks and the data blocks `Customer` and `Order` alone standing whole, the five that stood for the passages' words on Round F's arm (`Address`, `ListQuery`, `OrderLine`, `OrderState`, `PlaceOrder`) out; the four other scaffolded briefs list **13, 8, 9, and 8** keys (against 9, 7, 5, and 5), their pointer lines unchanged at 3, 4, 6, and 5; `health`'s design section is **14,901 bytes**, 463 below Round F's arm and 1,585 below the control's whole design (16,486), the five signatures being short; the passages stand after the guard sentence on every scaffolded brief and no data block is kept for a word of them; the five briefs together are **329,373 bytes** against Round F's 326,532 — **+0.9%**, the probe section on `health` grown from 2,583 to 5,189 bytes by its second fence and every test prelude by the `Value` body — and 6.6% below the control's 352,517, so the round buys grain, not bytes. ### Decision rule

- **The round holds under the large model** when G1 holds on the offline measure and G2–G5 hold on the run: this card is the large-model band in place of `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, and the five SDK and adapter items ship as they stand, the two backend items the small-model arm's to confirm. **G3 holding** is the fence's and the prelude's confirmation; G5 holding is the stop line's, under a model that needed none.
- **G1 failing** on the live clause alone — a turn searching for a type the cut left out — is read for the key and the words that would have kept it, the SDK's grain; the measure's figures are measured and cannot fail, so a brief differing from them on the live run would be the harness's, not the round's.
- **G3 failing** by a route without the encoder, or a test arranging no `on_query`, where the fence stood in the brief is the fence not followed, read for whether the turn `read_doc` `handlers.md`; by `from_slice` in a test is the prelude's `Value` not read, counted; by shell or `read_doc` over the figure is read for what the turn went looking for, as F4's clause reads it. **G4 failing** is read as F5's clause reads it, a turn over 1.5× as D3 reads it; **G5 failing** by a verify's rounds under a model whose verifies were already short is observed and the stop line kept, since it was written for the small model.
- **Attribution.** The model and every knob are Round F's arm's, so a difference against `2026-10-20T20-45-10Z` is the five items' together, read per turn: `health`'s route and test shapes and its clippy runs the fence's; `from_slice` the prelude's; the verifies' rounds the stop's; the keys out on `health` the SDK's grain, from the measure.

#### Decision rule

- **S1 holding with S3 and S4** makes this card the small-model band under `qwen3-coder-next`, in place of `05-21-29Z` as the card a run is read against, and confirms the recipe's move to the pod, which this round makes on its own grounds and not the rule's — a machine whose prompt evaluation is five times the Mac's reads the model's discipline rather than the machine's, and no 30b arm finished on the Mac — with the local 30b the fallback. **S1 reaching 7/7 and 6/6** with S2–S5 holding is the figure the band asks.
- **S1 failing** is read by its phase: an `exhausted` at the round cap on `health` with the route reached late (S2's clippy count over 6) says the fence is not enough and the route function is the scaffold's to lay, the next item; an `exhausted` with the tests untouched is read for where the rounds went, through the counter over the log; an `error` on an undeclared tool is the host session's item, recorded, the attempt counted as the model's; a `timeout` with `model_ms` near the cap is the model's; a shell death the toolchain's, put again at `7200/1200` with the raise recorded; a verify refused is read for its finding.
- **S2 failing** by a route without the encoder, or a test arranging no `on_query`, where both fences stood in the brief is the fence not followed under this model, read for whether the turn `read_doc` `handlers.md`; by `health` covering under 6/8 with the shape held, the requirements left out named and read for what the turn did with them.
- **S3 failing** by a write around the guard is the guard's reach — the scaffold guard holds the SDK's tools and the shell is omnia's — recorded with what the turn had been refused just before, an item for the adapter's `check` (the entry's shape, the seed's static files at the build) and not this round's; by edit counts alone is read as D3 reads it; by a seed file rewritten is the `static_drift`-at-the-build item, recorded.
- **S4 failing** by a nudged round that re-evaluated the prefix is the tools-kept item not holding on Ollama, `omnia-genai`'s, read against the server's log; by `schema and answer type disagree` is the schema hold not reached, `omnia-genai`'s; a candidate refused by the hold and accepted at the next is the hold working as meant, counted and not a failure.
- **S5 failing** on the verify's rounds is the stop line's under this model, read for what the rounds did after the checks passed; on a build turn's tool calls is the load's reading, S1's phase clause read beside it.
- **Attribution.** The model, the machine, the plan, and the revision are `05-21-29Z`'s, so every difference is the seven items' and the knob's, by per-turn evidence: the route's shape and the clippy count the fence's; the test's body handling the prelude's; the verify's rounds the stop's; a nudged round's tokens the tools-kept item's; a refused candidate the schema hold's; a third candidate the knob's; the keys out on `health` the SDK's grain, from the offline measure, since no live reading of the brief exists.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the arm not put, or put as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the run resumed from the label where the engine allows.
