**Decision, the large model — the round holds under the large model by the rule, read with its two misdrawn clauses.** G1 holds on the measure and live, G2 whole, G5 whole; G3 holds on every clause that reads the turn — 22 tool calls, 7 shell, 3 clippy runs, the fence's route and tests line for line — and fails only the `from_slice` figure, which counted the prelude's own decode and so could not be met, the test bodies holding what the clause meant (`health` 0 against 7, three elsewhere for headers `send` does not answer); G4 holds on the build, the verdicts, the scenarios, the guard, every per-marker figure, and the findings, and fails the `find` count on three calls that tried a rename through a `find` matching eleven places. By the rule this card is the large-model band in place of `2026-10-20T07-39-39Z`, and the five SDK and adapter items ship as they stand, the two backend items the small-model arm's to confirm. The two misdrawn figures are recorded against the next round: a `from_slice` clause reads the test bodies after the prelude, and `send` answering the response's headers beside its status and body would take the three remaining decodes with it; a `find` matching several places is the tool refusing a rename it was not built for, counted and not decided on.

#### The small-model arm

**The arm ran on `2026-10-21T07-04-07Z`**, the same `--rebuild` under `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev` — the engine's working tree built `--release --features genai` over the `omnia-genai` working tree and copied to that name, since the engine's `009fa2b1` folded the `emery-dev` bin into the `genai` feature — `GENAI_MODEL=qwen3-coder-next:q8_0` through the tunnel on `127.0.0.1:11435`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`, the component the large-model arm's: **built 0/7, `bad_gateway` after 5220 s** against `05-21-29Z`'s 0/7 in 1811 s. Wave 1 verified at `db2cc98` (`Cargo.lock` sealed), `health` failed in wave 2 after two attempts — `error` at 1323 s, `transport` at 3784 s — the host's checks `ok 4/4` over the integration head; build turns 3 completions, 18.09 M input (nothing cached in the backend's reading) and 163.6 K output.

- **S1 fails**, in two phases, neither the `exhausted` the rule named. The first attempt (103 rounds, 102 tool calls, 1323 s) read the skeleton, `common`, and `handlers.md` (23 s in), filled the handler in twelve edits by its fourth minute, laid the route function over `handle_with` at 07:18:28 and ran the loop command whole from 07:20:57, every call after its last edit (07:20:27) spent on the test file: ten `write_files` of it whole, each refused by the guard, three written through the shell as `rm -f` and a heredoc (a fourth to `/tmp` refused by the sandbox) and formatted with `cargo +nightly fmt --all`, and at round 103 the report — `covered` 8/8, `written` 3 — handed as the `content` of a tool named `write`, which the host's session fails the completion for. The second attempt (127 rounds, 126 tool calls, 3784 s) edited from 20 s in — the handler by 07:30:54, the manifest's `omnia-wasi-sql` line, the eight tests arranged — ran `cargo test -p health` at 07:31:41 (`exit 101`, after 8 clippy runs), read `omnia-api.md` and `handlers.md`, laid the route function from the fence at 07:33:06 over the two `[conflict]` marker lines, and spent eleven edits in three minutes on its bounds — `StateStore + TableStore` unimported, then qualified `omnia_sdk::`, a `use axum::Json;` moved five times, the body's brace lost — over three clippy runs; at 07:36:30 it put the module whole through `write_files`, refused by the guard, tried `git checkout` (`exit 128`, no repository under the lend), removed the module through the shell at 07:37:49, was refused again (the guard holds the path, not the file), removed the crate with `rm -rf crates/health` at 07:42:33, and from 07:47:45 repeated one round 21 times for 41 minutes: `rm -rf crates/health && mkdir -p …`, then `write_files` of the crate's four files (`Cargo.toml` 696 bytes, `src/lib.rs` 471, `src/health.rs` 4,309, `tests/health.rs` 7,677; two byte-identical payloads alternating, 12 and 9 times), ~4,150 tokens in ~110 s each, refused each time as `scaffolded before this turn; change it through edit_files` over paths the tree no longer held, the context 183 K tokens at the last round. At round 127 of 128 the server answered (4,165 tokens, 1m56s, `200` at 08:30:22Z) and the client never received it: the tunnel's `ssh` closed at 08:31:06Z on its keepalive, the backend reported `transport`, and the engine's second failure ended the run. The machine then slept until 17:02Z, the tunnel loop reopening the tunnel on wake. Not resumed: S1 had failed at the first attempt's `error`, the attempt the drop ended stood one round from the cap inside that loop, and a resume would be a third `health` attempt under a rule it cannot change — the operator's call, the pod standing.
- **S2 holds in shape on both attempts and fails in coverage and on the clippy count.** No `within` fn, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant (the words in the `//!`, the marker lines, and the quoted scenario steps alone), no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race` in any edit or write of either attempt; `TableStore::query(.., "SELECT 1", ..)` awaited once with a non-empty row set the pass and `StateStore::get` of a probe key awaited once with `is_ok()`; the route function over `handle_with(MethodFilter::GET, ..)` with the `503` encoder, named in `routes()` as `health_ready_route()` — the fence's shape under the turn's names, in the first attempt's module from 07:18:28 and the second's from 07:33:06; every probe test arranging `ScriptedTables::default().on_query(|sql, _| sql == "SELECT 1", ..)` with `Row` from `omnia_wasi_sql` and `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` in the manifest (7 `on_query` and 4 `Row {` in the first attempt's final test file, 7 `on_query` in the second's at 07:33); the test bodies reading `body["status"]` (6) and `body["store"]` (5) over the prelude's `send`, no `br#`, `from_slice` the prelude's own alone; no registry read and no shell command spelling `deadline`, `timeout`, or `within`. But `health` was accepted at no candidate — no reply reached the check on either attempt (`check_ms` 0) — so it covers **0/8** against C1's 4/8 and `05-21-29Z`'s 0/8, and the route was reached late: **11** `cargo clippy -p health` runs before the first attempt's module stood (≤ 6 asked; 30 there), 8 before the second attempt's first test run and 3 more before it gave the route up for a rewrite.
- **S3 fails on the edits, the `find`s, the guard, and the shell, and holds on the arguments and the seed.** `edit_files` **24 calls a turn** over 10 markers (19 and 18 accepted, 45 and 46 edits) — 2.4 a marker against the 1.5 asked and 4.5 there; a `find` refused **4 and 5** times (≤ 1 asked; 8 there), every one over a line the turn's own earlier edits had changed, answered with the line to copy; one call with `path` missing and one whose arguments were not JSON (≤ 1 asked; 5 there); `write_files` **10 and 40, every one refused by the guard** (against 7): the test file whole ten times on the first attempt, the module whole and then the crate's four files on the second; around the guard through the shell, the first attempt's `rm -f` and three heredocs over the scaffolded test file, the second's `rm -f` of the module, `touch` of three empty files, and `rm -rf crates/health` **23** times (against 4 commands); the seed held — `crates/common` read on the first attempt and edited on neither, the root lists carrying the scaffold's anchored lines alone at the end of both, the four pending paths beside `crates/health/` being `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `src/routes.rs`, and `src/messaging.rs`.
- **S4 holds on the nudge and the hold, and fails on the undeclared tool.** `nudging` **0**; `schema and answer type disagree` **0**; a candidate refused by the hold **0**, since none reached it: the verify's one reply was JSON at the first round without tools and accepted (`check_ms` 44), and neither build attempt replied without a tool call, so the two backend items were never exercised and the knob's third candidate never reached. One completion ended on a tool the request does not declare (against 1): `write`, carrying the report as its `content`, after `run` on `05-21-29Z`.
- **S5 holds on the verify's calls and fails on its rounds and on the build's load.** The seed verify: 14 rounds, **13** tool calls (≤ 20; 45 there), 104 s (191 there) — the five checks run once and passing by 07:05:33 (`find -newer` naming `Cargo.lock`), run once more over five rounds, and the verdict at round 14, **6 rounds** after the pass against the 3 asked and 33 there, with no `read_doc` and no search of the tree. The build turns: **102 and 126** tool calls (≤ 40 asked; 128 there), shell **43 and 44** (≤ 10; 44 there), `read_doc` 1 and 2 (≤ 5), the check command run up to three times between accepted edits on the first attempt; a third candidate on no turn, since no candidate was judged.
- **Observed.** Wall 5220 s against 1811, the second attempt's 3784 s holding 2,400 of the 21 refused writes; model time 98% of both attempts (1,237 of 1,324 s, 3,728 of 3,785 s). The server evaluated 695 K prompt tokens in 461 s (1,508 a second), its prefix cache taking the rest of the 18.09 M the backend counted, and generated 167.7 K tokens in 3,961 s (42.3 a second: 56 at 22 K of context, 40 at 183 K) against `05-21-29Z`'s 287 s, 1,226, and 47.0; the largest context 187,593 tokens, no truncation, every server reply `200`. The first attempt read `handlers.md` alone, the second `omnia-api.md` and `handlers.md` and a `read` of `references/layout.md` as a tree path; the route fn was named `health_ready_route`; the first attempt's final test file carried the prelude verbatim with `Value::Null`; the tunnel stood from 05:20:46Z through both of the pod's arms until the drop.

**Decision, the small model — the rule's first clause does not hold, and the failure moved to the guard.** S1 fails, so this card does not stand in place of `05-21-29Z`, C1 stays the small-model band, and the recipe's move to the pod stands on its own grounds. What the arm reads against `05-21-29Z`, with the seven items and the knob the one difference: the fence was followed on both attempts within minutes — the route over `handle_with` with its `503` encoder, the double arranged with `on_query`, the `Row` crate named in the manifest — where that card's turn fought thirty clippy runs; the prelude's `Value` was read (`body["status"]`, no byte literal); the verify answered in 104 s and 6 rounds after its checks against 191 s and 33, the five run once more before the verdict; the backend's two items and the knob were never reached, since no build reply came without a tool call, so they stand confirmed by nothing and refuted by nothing. The failure sits where no clause looked: the guard — **fifty** refusals where `05-21-29Z` had seven and the large-model arm none — the model preferring a whole file to eight edits on the first attempt and, on the second, a whole file to a mis-edit it could not undo, and, refused, deleting the file through the shell and looping for 41 minutes over a refusal naming a way out (`edit_files`) the deletion had closed. Five items follow, the first three the next round's: the SDK's scaffold guard holds the content rather than the path — a `write_files` of a scaffolded path admitted where it keeps every marker id and laid type name the skeleton holds, or where the tree no longer holds the file, and a deleted file named in the refusal; the undeclared tool answered with a refusal naming the declared tools, one round lost, in place of a failed completion — `run` then `write`, once in each of the pod's arms, the second time with the report inside it — omnia's and `omnia-backends`'; and `use omnia_sdk::{StateStore, TableStore};` among the fence's `use` lines, the bounds' traits being what the second attempt could not import. Beside them: the route function the scaffold's to lay for a handler whose requirement states a status by outcome, as the rule's clause names it; and the recipe's tunnel loop run under `caffeinate` with its exit code read before the `date`, since the machine slept on the drop.

**The pod and the knobs for the next round.** Across `05-21-29Z` and `07-04-07Z`, 80–90% of the small-model arm's wall time was token generation (`47.0` and `42.3` tokens a second under Ollama 0.40.6's `llama.cpp` GGUF engine, `986 s` and `3,961 s`) multiplied by 103–128 round-trips, while `omnia-genai`'s `Ollama` adapter dropped `Format::Schema` (`response_format` sent only in a closing round's `json_object` mode). After `07-04-07Z`, the pod `kfon5fxhta2bcr` (`1× H200`, 143 GB VRAM, 120 GB network volume `cxsks2la1u` at `/workspace`) was reconfigured in place from Ollama (`qwen3-coder-next:q8_0` GGUF) to **vLLM 0.32.0** (`/opt/vllm-venv`, archived at `/workspace/vllm-venv.tar.zst`) serving **`Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`** (74.9 GiB fine-grained 128×128 block-scaled FP8 safetensors, staged in `/dev/shm/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` from `/workspace/models/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`), started by `/workspace/start-vllm.sh` with its log at `/workspace/vllm.log`:

- **Server environment and flags (`/workspace/start-vllm.sh`):** `CUDA_HOME=/opt/vllm-venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/nvidia/cu13` (`nvidia-cuda-nvcc==13.0.88`, `nvidia-cuda-crt==13.0.88`, and `nvidia-nvvm==13.0.88` pinned to match `nvidia-cuda-runtime==13.0.96`, since the container's `/usr/local/cuda` is 12.8 and DeepGEMM 2.8.0 requires `nvcc >= 12.9` for Hopper `sm_90a` FP8 kernels), `VLLM_CACHE_ROOT=/workspace/.cache/vllm` (holding the warmed `torch.compile` and 1,253 DeepGEMM cubins across resets), and `vllm serve /dev/shm/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8 --served-model-name Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8 --host 0.0.0.0 --port 11434 --max-model-len 262144 --gpu-memory-utilization 0.92 --enable-prefix-caching --enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser qwen3_coder --tool-strict-level function`.
- **Why `--tool-strict-level function`:** `omnia-genai`'s `OpenAI` adapter sends `response_format: {"type": "json_schema", ...}` on every round while `request.tools` carry no `"strict": true`. Under vLLM's default `--tool-strict-level auto`, `resolve_tool_strictness` returns `None` for non-strict tools and `xgrammar` constrains every round to the final JSON schema alone, blocking `<tool_call>` tags; under `function`, vLLM builds an `xgrammar` `OrFormat([tools_structural_tag, output_format])` over its builtin `qwen_3_coder` tag so each round constrains tool calls to `<tool_call>\n<function=…>` (leaving tool arguments flexible for `write_files` and `edit_files`) and constrains any non-tool reply to the request's JSON schema.
- **Measured on the pod:** 74.9 GiB model weights in VRAM, 50.2 GiB available for the attention/Mamba (`align`) hybrid prefix cache (`2,165,911` tokens, `8.26×` concurrency at `262,144` max context), **~18,200 tokens/s** cold prefill (23.8 K tokens in 1.49 s over the tunnel, 0.92 s warm) against Ollama's 1,226–1,508, and **177 tokens/s** single-stream decode (500 tokens in 2.82 s) against Ollama's 42.3–47.0 (**4.0×**).
- **Client knobs for the next round:** `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=local`, `GENAI_MODEL=openai::Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`, `GENAI_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:11435/v1/`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS=64` (halved from the backend's 128 default while `GENAI_MAX_TOOL_CALLS` stays 128, so a productive slice's 20–45 rounds fit with room and a stuck turn fails fast into the engine's retry instead of burning 64 tail rounds at peak context), and `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`.

## Round H — the small model's discipline, per slice

[Round G](#round-g--the-elisions-grain-and-the-small-models-discipline) ended with three small-model arms since C1 at 0/7, each lost to the finish or the tools and none to the behaviour: prose replies and a mis-shaped candidate under the 30b, the report handed to an invented `write` tool, a whole-file rewrite refused by the guard and the file deleted through the shell, twenty-one identical refused writes over forty-one minutes, a route function's bounds fought for want of one `use` line. This round changes the harness and not the model, and reads each slice over the large-model arm's tree rather than over a tree the small model must first build, so one round yields five slices of evidence in place of one. The items, each left in its working tree for the maintainer, the `omnia-backends` ones committed as `5149646` while the probes ran. In `omnia-backends` (`crates/genai`): `Tools` knows the names the turn declares and answers a call to any other with a repairable refusal naming them — `tool \`write\` is not declared; the tools are …` — one round lost in place of a failed completion, the host's contract untouched; and the loop breaker, identical failed calls (the tool and a hash of its arguments) counted in `Conversation::tool_round`, the result carrying the count from the third and the completion ending `BudgetExhausted` naming the call at `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS` (a `ConnectOptions` knob, default 5), which the engine's retry reads as any exhaustion. In the engine's SDK (`crates/sdk`): the scaffold guard holds a file's content rather than its path — `Scaffold.files: &[Guarded { path, keeps }]`, a `write_files` entry over a scaffolded file admitted where the tree no longer holds it or the content carries every token the adapter named, refused otherwise naming what it dropped and the three ways out, a `delete` of one still refused — and `restore_files`, a third tool declared when any file is guarded, writing a file back from the snapshot `build` took at entry. In the omnia adapter: `Scaffold::guarded()` in place of `paths()`, a module keeping its requirement ids and each item the scaffold named as its declaration spells it (`fn ready`, `fn ready_route`, `struct ReadyInput`, `const HEALTH_READY`, `struct Order`, `fn routes`), a test file its ids and `fn <test>` names, a manifest, an entry, or a seed list every line it holds, a held module nothing; the route function laid for a `GET` handler of a slice whose words select the probe idiom, beside the `POST` and stated-status cases, so a probe's `503` is the encoder's one expression to change, the brief's hint saying the status is the slice's to decide there; and `use omnia_sdk::{StateStore, TableStore};` among the probe fence's `use` lines, the bounds' traits being what `07-04-07Z`'s second attempt could not import. In the eval runner: `--resume <card> --at <wave>`, the probe's instrument. The first draft's first item — the report answered as a declared `answer` tool, no `response_format` beside the tools — is withdrawn: under `--tool-strict-level function` vLLM builds `OrFormat([tools_structural_tag, output_format])` over the request, so a tool call is constrained to `<tool_call>\n<function=…>` over the declared names and a non-tool reply to the `json_schema` `response_format` `omnia-genai` already sends every round; the finish is the server's, Round G's nudge and closing round stand for the Ollama fallback, and the pre-flight and the control below test the grammar's two claims before any item is credited with them. Nothing of the engine's own changed, so the release `emery` 0.39.0 stands; `emery_omnia.wasm` is built from this tree over the SDK of the engine's working tree and `emery-dev` from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-genai` working tree through its path line, uncommented for the build and re-commented after, Round G's two artifacts kept at `/tmp/roundG/` for the control.

**The method.** The arm is five probes over `2026-10-21T06-28-53Z`, the large-model arm and band (`c2c52172`, seven slices in six waves `1/2/1/1/1/1`, its chain `Initialise` → `Wave 1 verified` → `SLICE-003 health` (wave 2) → `SLICE-004 customers` (3) → `SLICE-005 orders-reading` (4) → `SLICE-006 orders-placement` (5) → `SLICE-007 orders-mutations` (6), no later wave sealing anything): `cargo run -p evals -- --resume 2026-10-21T06-28-53Z --at k --target omnia express-orders` for k = 2..6, one at a time, each copying the staged project with the repository its build created and moving the label to the last commit of wave k−1, so the engine resumes the slices the chain names and runs the plan again from there: `start-bootstrap` in its wave 1 and `start-http-errors` in its wave 2, each built as nothing and its wave verified over the seed, and the first build turn put to the slice the probe is named for over the large model's tree — `health` beside `start-http-errors` in wave 2 at k = 2, the later slices alone in the probe's wave 3, since every slice before them is resumed; a probe runs on to the label or its failure, so every later slice gets an attempt over that tree too and a probe that builds on yields whole-build evidence. The knobs: `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=local`, `GENAI_MODEL=openai::Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`, `GENAI_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:11435/v1/`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS=64`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`, `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS=5`, the server as the pod paragraph above has it. **The control** runs first on the same command at k = 2 under Round G's artifacts — `EMERY_BIN=/tmp/roundG/emery-dev EMERY_OMNIA_WASM=/tmp/roundG/emery_omnia.wasm`, the component and backend `07-04-07Z` ran — so against that card the same brief and tools meet the new server, its quantisation (FP8 for Q8_0), its sampling defaults, its grammar, and the two knobs, and what moves there is the server's; a clause the control already meets is credited to the server, never to an item. **The readings** replace Ollama's `print_timing`, which vLLM has none of: the client's `round answered` lines (`input_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `output_tokens` a round — `cached` may stop reading 0, since vLLM reports `cached_tokens` under prefix caching and Ollama did not, a reading change named as such), `/tmp/g_tools.py` over each `run-1.build.stderr` (tool calls by kind, the guard's refusals by kind, writes around the guard, the breaker's notes, the verify's calls after its last check), the pod's `Engine 000:` lines over each probe's window (prompt and generation throughput, KV cache usage, prefix cache hit rate), and a `/metrics` snapshot through the tunnel before and after each probe (the prompt and generation token counters, the time-to-first-token and per-output-token histograms, the prefix-cache query and hit counters). **The walls** are rebased: `07-04-07Z`'s model time was 98% of its wall and 80–90% of that generation, so at the pod's measured rates (decode 177 against 42.3 tokens a second, 4.0×; prefill ~18,200 against 1,508, ~12×) a turn of its first attempt's counts (6.97 M input, 41.1 K output, 103 rounds, 1323 s) runs in roughly **440 s**, and of its second's (10.95 M, 122.0 K, 127 rounds, 3784 s) in roughly **1,260 s**; capped at 64 rounds, roughly **275** and **640 s**. A built slice's turn is read against the large-model arm's — `health` 140 s, 22 tool calls, 7 shell, 3 clippy runs, 5 `edit_files` calls over 10 markers (two handlers, eight tests); `customers` 187 s, 33 tool calls, 8 shell, 6 clippy runs, 11 calls over 13 markers; `orders-reading` 212 s, 48, 11, 5, 14 over 14; `orders-placement` 346 s, 63, 20, 10, 20 over 14; `orders-mutations` 227 s, 54, 12, 5, 19 over 20 — the markers each slice's handlers and scenario tests from the staged plan, its marker types aside — and the verifies 7–8 tool calls and 0 after the last check; and the pod arms' failure modes: `07-04-07Z`'s **50** guard refusals, **30** shell writes around it, `edit_files` **2.4** a marker, a `find` refused **4 and 5** times, **11** clippy runs before the module stood, the verify **6** rounds after its checks.

### Predictions

Each probe is read at its first built slice — the one whose brief this arm is the first to read over the large model's tree — and then on to wherever it stops. The clauses shared by every probe, each with the item that meets it: the slice **accepted at its first candidate** (the finish, the server's grammar); **no completion ends on a call to a tool the request does not declare** (the grammar on the pod; item 2 is the Ollama fallback's and its evidence is genai's suite) and **0** `undeclared` refusals in the log; **no completion ends `exhausted`** at 64 rounds or 128 tool calls, the breaker's `failed N times with the same arguments` **not reached**, since the finish is structural and a slice's rounds are its tool calls plus the report (Round G's first attempt reported at round 103 and the large model's `health` made 22 tool calls); **the breaker's note** (`This exact call has now failed`) **≤ 1** a turn, so no identical call fails three times; **guard refusals ≤ 2** a turn and none repeated with the same arguments, a whole write of a scaffolded file that keeps its tokens **admitted** (item 4), and **0 writes around the guard through the shell** — no `rm`, heredoc, `echo >`, `sed -i`, or `cat >` over a scaffolded file, no `rm -rf crates/<stem>`; `restore_files` used **≤ 1** a turn, observed; `edit_files` **≤ 1.5 a marker**, a whole write admitted counting as every marker it filled; a `find` refused **≤ 2** a turn; the seed untouched but for the anchored lines; `nudging` **0**, the closing round **unreached**, `schema and answer type disagree` **0**, since the grammar admits no off-shape text, and a **premature report** — a candidate the SDK's report gate refuses for a `written` path the tree lacks or a `covered` id over nothing written, the grammar forcing `{` where the model would have written a sentence between tool calls — **≤ 1** a probe, the one way the withdrawn item comes back; each verify's verdict **within 3 rounds** of its last check passing in **≤ 20** tool calls; `read_doc` **≤ 5** and registry reads **0** a turn.

- **K, the control.** Under Round G's artifacts the path guard stands, so `health` is **not built**: guard refusals **≥ 5** on at least one attempt and a write around the guard through the shell on the attempt that follows them, as on `07-04-07Z`; each attempt ends **`exhausted` at 64 rounds** and neither `error`, since the grammar admits no `write` tool (an attempt ending `error` on an undeclared tool fails the grammar's claim and sends item 1 back as Round I's); the two attempts take **≤ 275 and ≤ 640 s** (the rebased figures, ±50%), the probe **under 1,800 s** with its seed verify; the seed verify's verdict **≤ 6 rounds** after its checks in **≤ 20** tool calls, as there; `nudging` 0 and no prose reply; the fence followed — the route over `handle_with` with its `503` encoder, `on_query` arranged — within the attempt's first ten minutes, as on both of `07-04-07Z`'s; the server's `Engine 000:` lines showing a prefix cache hit rate **above 50%** over the probe and `cache_read_tokens` **> 0** on the client's lines.
- **H1, `health` at wave 2.** The scaffold lays `ready_route` (or the handler's name over `_route`) for the probe's `GET` handlers with `StatusCode::OK` in its encoder, named in `routes()`, and the brief's hint names the encoder; the turn changes the encoder's status to `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` where the store failed in **≤ 2** edits and writes **no** `handle_with` of its own, the import of `StateStore` and `TableStore` standing from the fence or the scaffold; `cargo clippy -p health` **≤ 6** runs (against 11 before the module stood, and the large model's 3); `health` accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 6/8**, the two `[conflict]` requirements the ones left out if any; the module holds no `within`, no `PROBE_TIMEOUT` constant, no `tokio::time`, `select!`, or `race`, `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and `StateStore::get` each awaited once; every probe test arranging `on_query`, `Row` from `omnia_wasi_sql` with the crate in the stem's manifest, `body["status"]` over the prelude's `send`, no `br#`; the turn **≤ 600 s** (the rebased ceiling; 140 s under the large model), **≤ 64** tool calls; its wave **verified**, and the probe running on past it with `customers` observed under H2's clauses.
- **H2, `customers` at wave 3.** Accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 7/8**; the shared clauses; the turn **≤ 700 s**, **≤ 64** tool calls, `cargo clippy -p customers` **≤ 8** runs; its tests driving `customers::router()` through the prelude's `send` with no body decoded by hand; its wave verified.
- **H3, `orders-reading` at wave 4.** Accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 8/9**; the shared clauses, with the cache-aside idiom read — the cache read first, a miss read from the store and written under its TTL, a store miss `not_found`, never an empty answer on a miss, so `empty_misses` finds **0** at its check; the turn **≤ 800 s**, **≤ 64** tool calls; its wave verified.
- **H4, `orders-placement` at wave 5.** Accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 9/10**, the turn **≤ 1,200 s** (the large model's longest at 346 s, its most edits, and the most laid types) and **≤ 64** tool calls, the one slice the round expects to press the round cap — a turn `exhausted` here with its edits productive to the end is read for the cap and not the model; the upstream role faked through `MatchedHttp` in its tests and reached through `HttpRequest::fetch`; its wave verified.
- **H5, `orders-mutations` at wave 6.** Accepted at its first candidate covering **≥ 10/11**; the shared clauses, its edits to `placement.rs` and `reading.rs` within its own stem as the large model's were and no file of another stem touched; the turn **≤ 900 s**, **≤ 64** tool calls; its wave verified and the label at the tip, the probe's chain holding the large model's four merges and this arm's one.
- **Observed, never decided on:** each probe's wall against the control's and the large model's; tokens turn for turn and the `cached` reading; the server's prompt and generation throughput over each window and the prefix cache hit rate; the largest context; which references each turn reads; whether a turn names the route function the scaffold's name; what the verifies seal; how far each probe runs on and what the later slices do over a tree partly this arm's; the tunnel.

### Decision rule

- **The round holds** when H1 holds and at least **three** of H2–H5 hold at their first built slice: the SDK's content guard, the adapter's probe route function and fence line, and the backend's breaker ship as they stand; the undeclared-tool refusal ships on genai's suite alone, since the pod's grammar never reaches it. **A probe that builds from wave 2 to the label** (7/7, 6/6, the host's checks agreeing) stands as the small-model band under `Qwen3-Coder-Next` in place of `05-21-29Z` and `07-04-07Z`, since its chain names no slice of the large model's and every slice was built by this arm over the seeded tree; a probe at k ≥ 3 that builds to the label is whole-build evidence over a mixed tree and no band. Until one does, C1 stays the small-model band.
- **The control** is read first and subtracted: a shared clause K already meets — the finish at the first candidate, no undeclared tool, no nudge, the verify's rounds — is the server's and no item is credited with it; a clause K fails and the probes hold is the items' by the difference. **K failing its own clauses** is read by phase: an attempt ending `error` on an undeclared tool says the grammar does not hold the finish and item 1 returns as Round I's; one ending at the round cap with the guard refusals and the shell writes as predicted says the server changed nothing of the failure, which is what the items are for; `health` **built** under Round G's artifacts says the server alone moved the arm, and every item's clause is then read against that reading, the round's items confirmed only where a probe's figure beats the control's.
- **H1 failing** is read by its clause: guard refusals over 2, or a whole write keeping every token refused, is the guard's token set too wide — the tokens named in the refusal are read against the file the turn sent — the adapter's `guarded()` item; a write around the guard through the shell after a refusal is the guard's reach again, recorded, an item for the adapter's `check` and not this round's; the breaker ending the completion is the breaker working as meant and the slice failing all the same, read for what the call was and what the refusal had said; an `exhausted` at 64 rounds with no loop is the cap's, read for whether the rounds were productive (edits accepted, checks run) — raised to 96 for Round I where they were — or spent (reads, lists, repeated checks); a premature report refused by the gate is the finish's failure mode, recorded against item 1's return; `health` accepted covering under 6/8 with the shape held is read for what the turn did with the requirements left out; the route's status changed in more than 2 edits, or a `handle_with` written over the scaffold's, is the scaffolded route function not read, the hint's wording the item.
- **H2–H5 failing** are read the same way, each at its own slice, and a clause failing on one slice alone is that slice's and not the harness's; failing on three or more is the harness's, the clause named across them. **H4** `exhausted` at 64 rounds with productive edits to the end is the cap's as its clause says.
- **Attribution.** The plan, the revision, the model, the server, and the knobs are one across the control and the five probes, so a difference between the control and a probe at k = 2 is the items' together, read per clause: the guard's refusals and the shell writes the SDK's and the adapter's guard; the route's edits and the clippy count the scaffolded route function's and the fence line's; a loop ended the breaker's; an undeclared tool the grammar's or, where it reaches the backend, item 2's. A difference between the probes at k ≥ 3 and the large model's turns is the model's, the tree being the large model's own up to that wave.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the probe not put, or put as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the probe put again from its start, since a probe is one slice's evidence and a resume of a resume would read a tree partly this arm's.

### Pre-flight

Through the tunnel at 20:30Z on the 21st, the pod up 16 hours, vLLM 0.32.0 serving `Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` (`/v1/models`: `max_model_len` **262144**, `root` `/dev/shm/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`), before any component of this round was built:

1. One tool (`read`, `path` required) and a strict `json_schema` `response_format` (`covered`, `written`) on one request, the system prompt saying to read a file first: `finish_reason` **`tool_calls`**, `content` `null`, one call `read {"path": "crates/health/src/ready.rs"}`, 27 completion tokens in 0.8 s. The grammar admits a tool call beside the schema.
2. The same tools and schema under a prompt inviting prose — the system prompt saying to think out loud in plain sentences, the user asking for a paragraph of prose before any tool: **`tool_calls`** again, `content` `null`, the same `read` call, 27 tokens; and the same prompt with the tools withdrawn, as the closing round sends it: `finish_reason` **`stop`** and `content` `{ "covered": [ "crates/health/src/ready.rs" ] , "written": [ "crates/health/src/ready.rs" ] }` — schema JSON where the prompt asked for prose, the fields filled with the one path the prompt held. A reply under this server is a declared tool call or the schema's JSON and never prose, so the nudge is unreachable here and the finish's one failure mode is the premature report the predictions name.
3. A system prompt describing a `write` tool the request does not declare and a user turn asking to hand the report to it: **`tool_calls`** naming **`read`**, the declared tool, with the file's path — the undeclared name cannot be spelled under `--tool-strict-level function`, so `07-04-07Z`'s `write` ending is unreachable here and item 2's evidence stays genai's suite.

`/metrics` before the control, over the 14 requests the server has answered since it came up (the rate measurements and these four): prompt tokens 45,971, of them 32,096 cached (hit rate 0.698), generation tokens 437, mean time to first token 0.24 s, mean inter-token latency 4.8 ms (209 tokens a second), no preemption. The snapshots are taken with `/tmp/metrics-snap.py` and diffed per probe.

### Results

**The control, `2026-10-21T20-33-14Z`** (`--resume 2026-10-21T06-28-53Z --at 2` under Round G's `emery-dev` and `emery_omnia.wasm` from `/tmp/roundG/`, the label moved to `563744f9` `Wave 1 verified`, the chain naming no slice): **built 0/7, verified 0/6**, the run ending after 203 s as `bad_request` at **wave 1's verify**, so `health` was never put and K's `health` clauses are unread. The seed verify, the one turn before any slice: the first attempt ran the four checks in 80 s, each passing at its first run (clippy, test, the wasm build, the formatter), then put `find -newer` five ways — and ended **`provider`** at its thirteenth call, `find "$WORKSPACE" -type f -not -path './target/*' …` over the absolute root, whose `-not -path './target/*'` excludes nothing under an absolute path, listing `target/` whole: a tool result of **1,048,608 bytes**, the tool's cap, which the next round carried into a request the server refused `400 Bad Request` (its 131,151 input tokens had been 12,421 a round; the result alone is some 300 K tokens against the 262,144 the model holds). The engine put the verify again; the second attempt ran the checks in 75 s, each passing, and then spent **56 rounds** on the tree — `find -newer` eleven ways, `stat -f %m` of `Cargo.lock`, `before`, `crates/common/Cargo.toml`, `examples/runtime.rs`, `ls -la .git`, six `read`s of the seed's config files, two `list`s of `.github` — and ended **`exhausted` at 64 rounds** with no verdict offered, 116 s, 1,008,576 input tokens, 2,513 output. K's verify clause (**≤ 6 rounds after its checks**, as `07-04-07Z` had it under Ollama) **fails** on both attempts; `nudging` 0 and no prose reply, as predicted; no `write` tool and no undeclared call, as the grammar promised. `cache_read_tokens` read **0** on every round: vLLM reports `prompt_tokens_details` as `null` unless started with `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, so the client's `cached` is a dead reading on this server, and the server's own `Engine 000:` lines put the prefix cache hit rate at **82.9–94.7%** over the probe. `/metrics` over the control (and the four pre-flight requests before it): 86 requests, 1,144,740 prompt tokens, 1,094,530 of them cached (hit rate **0.956**), 3,494 generation tokens, mean TTFT 0.25 s, 207 tokens a second decoded, every request `stop`.

**Probe k = 2 under the round's artifacts, `2026-10-21T20-44-29Z`** (`emery_omnia.wasm` 4,262,939 bytes from this tree over the engine's working-tree SDK, `emery-dev` 37,709,760 bytes from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-genai` working tree): **built 0/7, verified 0/6**, ending after 126 s at **wave 1's verify** the same way. The first attempt ended **`transport`** at its second round, 7 s in, the request after the clippy result never reaching the server (nothing in its log at that second; the tunnel's log shows no drop) — a client-side failure the engine's retry absorbed; the second ran the four checks in 73 s, each passing, and then ran **the whole check loop three more times** — `touch before` and the four checks again at 20:45:56, 20:46:19, 20:46:28 — between `find -newer` eight ways, `stat` of `Cargo.lock`, `ls` of the crates, and two `read_doc`s (`references/layout.md`, `references/guardrails.md`), ending **`exhausted` at 64 rounds**, 120 s, 1,149,709 input tokens, with no verdict offered. The round's items touch nothing of the verify's finish, so the probe reads as the control does: **under this server the model does not finish a verify while tools are offered**.

**What the finish is, read at the server.** Four more requests through the tunnel, each a two-call history of a passing clippy and a passing test under a system prompt saying to answer the verdict as JSON once every check has passed, the `shell` and `read` tools declared: with the verdict's `json_schema` and `tool_choice` `auto`, the model answers **a third `shell` call, `cargo clippy …` again**, three of three at temperature 0; the same with **no `response_format`** at all, so prose was open to it, **the same call**; the same under Ollama's sampling (temperature 0.8, `top_p` 0.9, `top_k` 40, `repetition_penalty` 1.1) and under a presence penalty, the same call every time; and the same request with **`tool_choice` `none`**, the tools still declared: `finish_reason` **`stop`**, `{"passed": true, "failures": []}`, 12 tokens. Asked instead to say what the two commands printed, it recounts both outputs and exit codes correctly, so the history is read. The model's chat template, applied on the pod to the same history, renders the calls and results as `<tool_call>`/`<tool_response>` blocks in the shape its card documents. So the grammar holds what the pre-flight showed — a reply is a declared call or the schema's JSON — and the model takes the call whenever one is offered: the finish is not the server's to force by shape but the request's to force by `tool_choice`, which `omnia-genai` never sent. `07-04-07Z`'s verify answered after 14 rounds under Ollama's `llama.cpp` and the Q8_0 GGUF; whether the template, the quantisation, or the server's tool-call parsing moved the model from "answers eventually" to "never answers" is not decided here, since the sampling is ruled out and the template reads right.

**Item 8, added after the control and probe k = 2 and before any further probe, in `omnia-backends` (`crates/genai`, the working tree the probes' `emery-dev` was built over, committed as `5149646` with items 2 and 3 while the probes ran): the finishing round.** The last round of the round budget is put with `tool_choice` `none` and the tools still declared, so the completion ends with the model's answer where the provider honours it, and a call made there all the same is refused as a closing round's is (`Conversation::finishing`, `round_request`, the `//!`, the README's budgets paragraph, a RELEASES bullet, `model_rounds_finish` beside `model_rounds_exhausted`, which now asserts the last request's `tool_choice`; 69 tests, clippy and the nightly formatter clean). This is the withdrawn item 1 in the shape the control decided: not a tool to answer through, but the one round the request forbids acting in. It is a deviation from the protocol — an item after the control — recorded as such; the five probes run under it, and the control and `20-44-29Z` stand as the reading without it. **Its predictions, before `emery-dev` is rebuilt:** every verify answers at **round 64** with **`passed: true`** and no failure, since its checks pass and it repairs nothing (the loop re-runs them), so each probe's seed verify costs some 120 s and reaches its slice; a build turn that does not finish on its own is asked for its report at round 64, and that report is judged by the gate as any is — a report refused there is `exhausted` carrying the correction, since the round budget is spent, so **the premature report** the predictions name is now the forced one at the cap: **H1–H5's "accepted at its first candidate" is read as "accepted at the forced round or before it"**, and a slice whose forced report the gate refuses fails its clause. The shared clauses otherwise stand. **The verify's rounds after its checks** are read as 56 for every probe, the clause failing as the control's does, and credited to nothing: the finishing round answers it, it does not cure it. A provider that ignores `tool_choice` keeps `exhausted` as before, so nothing of the Ollama fallback changes but one refused round.

The turn: eight `read_doc` (every reference, `handlers.md` and `translation.md` twice), six reads, then seven `edit_files` of one edit each over the design type `ListQuery`, the inputs, the outputs, and the two handler markers, one refused as not JSON; then **seven `write_files` whole, every one admitted by the content guard** — the module at 16,974, 16,736, 16,896, 16,816, 11,226, and 11,167 bytes and the test at 8,764 — the tokens kept each time, item 4's admission clause holding where Round G's path guard refused every one of them; **`clippy -p orders` run 13 times**, never clean, seventeen more `edit_files` between the runs over the `not_found!` spelling, the imports (`use omnia_wasi_sql::{Row, DataType};` added and removed), the stem's manifest (`omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` added at the end of `[dependencies]`, the right manifest this time, then dropped with the import), and the cache-miss branch twice; and at round 64 the report. The module merged: 353 lines, the cache-aside read as the idiom spells it — `StateStore::get` under `orders/<id>` first, a miss read from the store and the hit written back with `StateStore::set` under a TTL, the store's miss `not_found!` — so `empty_misses` has nothing to find; but the TTL the literal `60` in place of the `ORDER_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` setting REQ-039 names, the store behind a `pub(crate) mod service` **whose `get_order` returns `Ok(None)` under the comment `The TableStore API does not support SQL queries in this environment`**, and the handlers calling it as `crate::service::` where it sits at `reading::service`, two compile errors; 5 of the test's 12 scenario tests filled, 7 `#[ignore]` over their `todo!`. **Wave 3's verify, 207 s, `passed: false` at round 64**: `cargo clippy --workspace` failing on `crates/orders/src/lib.rs` with `derive_partial_eq_without_eq`, `missing_const_for_fn`, `needless_pass_by_value`, the one failure, honest and gated `verify-failed`. What it did on the way is the round's worst reading of the model with no guard over it, since the verify lends the tree with no scaffold and the SDK's guards read a build's `Scaffold` alone: a `mv crates/orders/src/reading.rs crates/orders/src/service.rs` **through the shell**, the entry's `pub mod reading;` edited to `service`, then **18 `write_files`, 16 of them `crates/orders/src/lib.rs`** between 665 and 11,271 bytes with `delete` naming `reading.rs`, `service.rs`, and `lib.rs` itself in turn, the module folded into the entry, deleted, and rewritten, `cargo update -p orders`, the manifest written twice, 20 `clippy --workspace` runs, the last three edits over a `#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Se…` line — and the integration copy left with `reading.rs` deleted and `lib.rs` and the manifest modified, uncommitted, the label standing at `192a6175`. Read against the clauses: no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, the breaker unreached, `restore_files` 0; guard refusals 0 and seven whole writes admitted; `edit_files` 24 edits over 14 markers, 1.7 a marker — the one probe near the clause's 1.5, the whole writes carrying the rest; `find`s refused 3 where ≤ 2; `read_doc` 8 where ≤ 5; the seed untouched; 284 s where ≤ 800 s; clippy 13; the wave not verified. `/metrics` over the probe: 256 requests, 10,772,400 prompt tokens, 10,468,200 cached (hit rate **0.972**), 59,858 generated, mean TTFT 0.37 s, 195 tokens a second, every request `stop`.

**Probe k = 5, first put, `2026-10-21T21-42-20Z`: not put.** `bad_request` after 1,570 s at wave 1's verify, `exhausted` — `no answer after 64 model round-trips` — on item 8's own round: the checks passed by call 31, 32 more calls over `Cargo.lock`'s timestamp, and at round 64, under `tool_choice` `none` and the verdict's schema, the model generated **243,791 tokens in 23 minutes** until the context was full — `finish_reason` **`length`**, the one such request in the server's counters, 18,353 prompt tokens and 262,144 in all — of which 162 bytes reached the client as content, `EOF while parsing an object at line 5 column 1`, nudged with no round left. The seven finishing rounds before it (three probes' verifies and one build) each answered within a few dozen tokens; this one ran away, and nothing bounded it: the client sets `max_tokens` only where the guest's request carries one, and the engine's and the SDK's carry none, so a degenerate round runs to the context at the server's 175 tokens a second. Recorded as the harness's — a Round I item for `omnia-genai`, a default `max_tokens` on every round sized to the answer a schema can need — and the probe **put again from its start**.

**Probe k = 5, second put, `2026-10-21T22-11-16Z`: not put, and H4 unread.** `bad_request` after 376 s at **wave 2's verify**, the slice never reached — at k = 5 two verifies stand before it, wave 1's over `start`'s first slice and wave 2's over its second, each merging nothing and each verified. Wave 1's first attempt **`provider`** on the same `find "$WORKSPACE" … -not -path './target/*'` (the fourth `400` of the round), its second `ok` at round 64; wave 2's **`exhausted` at round 64, its forced verdict refused**: `{"failures": ["The fifth check (no other files written) found no files newer than the marker except Cargo.lock, so no issues found."], "passed": true}` — the `failures` list used for a note — and the verdict gate's finding, `passed is true, yet 1 failure is listed`, with no round left to correct it in; the 17 rounds before it `find . -type f -not -path './target/*' -newer "$TMPDIR/before"` over and over, each answering six bytes, a loop the breaker does not see since the call succeeds. Two puts, neither reaching `orders-placement`, so the probe stands as not put under the rule and the slice's clauses are unread. What the two puts show is the cost of the verifies a resume pays before its slice: twelve verify attempts have been put under item 8 across the probes: six answering `passed: true` at round 64 in some 120 s, one telling its failing lints there, three ended before the round by the `find` over the absolute root, one by the runaway round, one by a note in `failures`.

**Probe k = 6, `2026-10-21T22-19-53Z`** (the label moved to `e541f0e3`, `SLICE-006 orders-placement`, the chain naming the four slices before it): **built 0/7**, `bad_request` after 593 s at `orders-mutations`' build turn, the two verifies before it `passed: true` at round 64 in 134 and 132 s. **`orders-mutations`, 319 s, `exhausted` at round 64, its forced report refused by `untested_scenarios`**: `covered` named REQ-051, REQ-057, REQ-059, and REQ-060 while every one of the test file's **16 scenario tests stood `#[ignore]` over its marker**, the file never read and never edited — the check's finding naming each test by line, with no round left to fill them in or to drop the ids. H5 fails on its first clause, 0 of 11. The turn, 63 calls: no `read_doc`, sixteen reads (the module eleven times, `lib.rs`, `reading.rs` twice — read, not edited), **30 `edit_files`, 59 edits, every one over `crates/orders/src/mutations.rs`**, 6 `find`ing nothing, the three design types (`AmendLines`, `TERMINAL_STATES`, `TRANSITIONS`) and the inputs and outputs filled in the first five minutes, the handlers' markers all filled (0 `todo!` left in the module), a `use order_repository::{find as f…` line edited four times over an `order_repository` module the slice imagined, `OrderState::Paid` / `Shipped` / `Cancelled` assignments rewritten as `serde_json::Value`s; **three `write_files` of the module whole — the first refused by the delete guard for naming the path in `delete` beside `files`**, the replacement shape again, **the two after admitted** at 15,201 and 15,475 bytes with the tokens kept; and the shell for `cargo check -p orders` nine times and `clippy -p orders` five, every run answering errors to the end — the module left at 383 lines with five, three of them syntax (`expected ':'`). Read against the clauses: no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, the breaker unreached, `restore_files` 0; guard refusals 1; **its edits within its own stem and no file of another stem touched, as the clause said**, though `placement.rs` and `reading.rs` were read and not edited where the large model edited both; `edit_files` 59 edits over 20 markers, 3.0 a marker; 6 `find`s refused where ≤ 2; `read_doc` 0; 319 s where ≤ 900; the seed untouched; the wave not verified. `/metrics` over the probe: 192 requests, 7,980,670 prompt tokens, 7,747,650 cached (hit rate **0.971**), 46,792 generated, mean TTFT 0.35 s, 198 tokens a second, every request `stop`.

**The server over the round**, between the snapshot before the control and the one after the last probe, the diagnosis requests among them: 1,127 requests, 38.0 M prompt tokens of which 36.8 M cached (hit rate **0.969**), 438 K generated, 183 tokens a second decoded, mean time to first token 0.33 s, 1,126 requests `stop` and the one runaway `length`; the four `400`s outside the counters. The control and the eight puts ran in 1 h 57 min of wall, some $10 of the pod's time at its rate.

**Decision — the round does not hold: H1 fails, H2, H3, and H5 fail, H4 is unread, and no probe builds to the label, so C1 stays the small-model band.** The control is read first and subtracted. K fails its own clauses in a phase the rule did not draw: not `error` on an undeclared tool, not the guard refusals and the shell writes of `07-04-07Z`, not `health` built — `health` was never put, since the server's grammar, which holds the reply to a declared call or the schema's JSON exactly as the pre-flight said, has the model take the call every time one is offered, and the seed verify that answered after 14 rounds under Ollama answers never under vLLM. So the server moved the arm before any item could be read, and the finish, withdrawn as item 1, came back as item 8 in the backend — recorded as the protocol's deviation, after the control and the first probe and before the rest, with its predictions written before `emery-dev` was rebuilt. Those predictions hold where they were made: of the fourteen verify attempts put under the item, eleven reached round 64, and eight of those answered `passed: true` in some 120 s while the one with a failure to tell told it (`verify-failed` at k = 4's wave 3, the clippy lints named). The other five were lost to what the item did not predict and the harness does not bound: a `find` over the absolute root whose 1 MiB result the backend carried whole into a request the server refused (three attempts, four `400`s over the round with the control's, two probes' first puts lost to it), a finishing round that ran to the context in 23 minutes with nothing setting `max_tokens`, and a verdict whose `failures` carried a note. The shared clauses, probe by probe, read the model's discipline the round set out to read, and it is poor in the same way at every slice: the grammar holds — no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, `schema and answer type disagree` 0, as predicted — and inside it the model does not finish, so **every build turn ran to round 64** (`customers` to the breaker at 45) and the forced report was what the slice had when the clock stopped: refused for the root manifest (`health`), accepted covering nothing (`orders-reading`), refused for ignored tests claimed covered (`orders-mutations`). The items are read by the difference from the control and from `07-04-07Z`: **item 4 holds** — the content guard admitted ten whole writes that kept the skeleton's tokens (`health`'s test, seven on `orders-reading`, two on `orders-mutations`) where Round G's path guard refused every one, and refused five that dropped them — four renaming `health`'s probe items, one dropping the root manifest's host table — which is what it is for; the model's answer to a refusal is the item's finding — `restore_files` taken three times as the next step on the refusal's own sentence, each discarding the edits since, and a `write_files` naming its paths in `delete` beside `files` three times at one slice and once at another, refused for the `delete` with the content unread. **Item 5 holds on its shape and is moot on its clause**: the scaffold's route function and the fence's `use` line stood and the status edit was two edits, but the slice never reported, so `clippy ≤ 6` and `≥ 6/8` read nothing; the model wrote a `handle_with` of its own for `live` all the same. **Item 3 fired once as built** (`customers`, five identical `find`s with the file re-read three times between them, the note at the third and fourth unheeded) and ended a turn 19 rounds early that would have ended at 64 refused; it does not see a loop of succeeding calls (the verify's thirteen `stat`s, seventeen `find`s). **Item 2 was never reached**, as predicted. **Item 6's resume** did what it was for — every probe cut at its wave over the large model's tree, the chain read, the slices before it resumed — and showed its cost: at k ≥ 3 two verifies of `start`'s empty waves stand before the slice, each a 64-round forced loop, so a probe pays four to five minutes and the harness's odds twice before its first brief. What the probes read beyond the clauses, read against the large model's turns on the same briefs: the small model does not keep a copy — `omnia` spelled `omega`, `omena`, `omea`, `oma` in long `find`s, a `find` sent five times stale over a file read three times, an `edits` argument that was a Windows command line — so `edit_files` at 3–6 edits a marker against the large model's 2 and `find`s refused at 3–13 a turn against ≤ 2; it edits the root manifest where the reference says the stem's, imagines a module (`order_repository`) and crates (`uuid`, `chrono`), stubs the store with a comment saying the API cannot (`orders-reading`), and claims coverage over tests it never opened (`orders-mutations`); and under no guard, at a verify, it `mv`s a module through the shell, folds it into the entry, and writes `lib.rs` sixteen times. None of this is the fence's, the guard's, or the breaker's to fix, and the eleven minutes a 64-round turn takes are not the cap's: the rounds were spent, not short.

Round I's items follow from the three places the harness lost a probe and the two readings the guard's items left: the backend caps a tool result against the model's context before it is carried into a request (`omnia-genai`, a `find` over `target/` is 300 K tokens the model never needed); a default `max_tokens` on every round sized to what a schema can need, so a finishing round that runs away ends in seconds and not at the context (`omnia-genai`); a `write_files` whose `delete` names a path the same call writes is a replacement — the write wins and the `delete` is dropped, or the refusal says so (the SDK); the content guard's refusal names the renamed items and asks for the skeleton's names kept, with `restore_files` out of the sentence, since the model takes the last option offered (the SDK); and `edit_files`' refusal names the first line where the `find` diverges from the file, not the line the first line matched (the SDK). Beside them, two readings for the adapter and one for the recipe: the verify is the one turn with no scaffold under it, so the SDK's guards do not read it and a `mv` through the shell or a `delete` of a stem's module is a verify's to make — the adapter's `check_verify` could hold the stems' modules to the entries' `pub mod` lines as `unmerged_stems` holds the entries to the lists; the probe's hint names the stem's manifest for `omnia-wasi-sql` and the model edited the root's twice over, so the slice's manifest line is the scaffold's to lay where the idiom is selected, as the route function now is; and the small-model arm's recipe should set `--enable-prompt-tokens-details` at the server so the client's `cached` reads what the server's counters do, and should put the seed's verify once under the resume rather than once per empty wave, which is the runner's to arrange. Whether `Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` under vLLM finishes a turn on its own anywhere is the open question item 8 answered around rather than through: the pre-flight showed the finish forced by `tool_choice` alone, and the probes showed the forced round answering well nine times in ten, so the next arm's question is not whether the model can be made to answer but whether a turn of 64 rounds can be made productive — the per-marker and per-`find` figures say the rounds go to copying, and the item that would read that is a tool that edits by line number or by anchor rather than by a copied span.

## Round I — the harness debt and the sampling

[Round H](#round-h--the-small-models-discipline-per-slice) read the small model's discipline per slice and did not hold: every build turn ran to the forced round and five of fourteen verify attempts were lost to the harness, not the model. On 2026-10-22 the small-model arm was decided a goal, pushed through the untrained levers as a ladder — one rung a round, every rung read at `k = 2` — before any model is trained ([the protocol](README.md#protocol), "The small-model arm is a ladder, and `k = 2` is the gate"). This round is the ladder's first two rungs. **Rung 0** is the harness debt, ten items from Round H's decision, each a place the harness lost a probe or taught the model a wrong step, each left in its working tree for the maintainer. In `omnia-backends` (`crates/genai`): the loop breaker counts a succeeding call that returns the same result as it counts a failing one — the same tool, the same arguments, the same result — the result carrying the count from the third (`This exact call has now returned the same result 3 times, and nothing has changed between them`) and, at `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS` (5), saying the answer is due and making the next round a finishing one under `tool_choice` `none`, so a verify that has nothing left to do answers at its loop and not at round 64 (k = 3's thirteen `stat`s, k = 5's seventeen `find`s), the count cleared where a candidate is refused so a correction may use its tools; a tool result is cut to 64 KiB before it enters the conversation, head and tail kept with `[… N bytes omitted …]` between, so a `find` over `target/` cannot carry a mebibyte into a request the server refuses (`400 Bad Request`, four times over Round H); and every round carries a `max_tokens`, the request's own or `GENAI_MAX_TOKENS` (default 32768), so a finishing round that runs away ends in minutes and not at the context (k = 5's 243,791 tokens in 23 minutes). In the engine's SDK (`crates/sdk`): a `write_files` whose `delete` names a path the same call writes is the entry's — the file written whole, held to the content guard and the shrink rule, nothing removed, the answer listing it as written — where the delete guard had refused the shape four times with the content unread; the content guard's refusal names what the write drops and the two ways on, `change the file through edit_files, or send the whole file with those kept`, with `restore_files` out of the sentence, since the model took it as the next step three times and lost every edit since; and `edit_files`' refusal of a `find` that matches nothing names the occurrence the most lines of the `find` follow, the lines after it that match, and the line where the file parts from it (`line 2 matches, and line 3 is …`), where it had named the matched line and the one after, so a `find` gone stale over a field an earlier edit added is repaired on the next call rather than sent five times to the breaker. In the omnia adapter: `verify.md`'s fifth check is `find . -type f -not -path '*/target/*' -newer "$TMPDIR/before"`, the exclusion holding under `"$WORKSPACE"` as under `.`; the scaffold lays `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` in the manifest of a stem whose slice selects the probe idiom, after the six fixed lines or at the end of a held manifest's `[dependencies]`, the brief saying so and `unused_dependencies` holding it to no use, since `health` spent its last fourteen rounds on the root manifest for the crate; and `undeclared_parts` joins `check_common`, so both turns hold every `crates/<stem>/src/<part>.rs` to a `mod <part>;` line of its entry and an entry exposing `router` or `topics` to nothing of its own beyond its `mod` lines, its `use` lines, and the two folds, since k = 4's verify `mv`ed a module through the shell, folded it into `lib.rs`, and wrote the entry sixteen times under no guard. In the eval runner: `--resume` seals the earlier waves' empty slices but the first over the moved label, each an empty commit under the engine's own message, so one seed verify stands before the probe's slice at any wave where k ≥ 3 paid two; the pod's `vllm serve` line gains `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, so the client's `cached` reads the server's counters (0 on every line of Round H against a hit rate of 0.969); and the header's knobs gain `GENAI_MAX_TOKENS`. **Rung 1** is the sampling: nothing in the engine's or the SDK's requests sets a temperature and `omnia-genai` forwards one only where the guest sets it, so every Round H turn copied spans under the model's own `generation_config.json` — temperature 1.0, `top_k` 40, `top_p` 0.95 — which is where `omnia` spelled `omega`, `omena`, `omea`, and `oma` most plausibly comes from; Round H's temperature-0 diagnosis tested the finish and not the copying. The rung sets `--override-generation-config '{"temperature":0}'` at the server and runs one `k = 2` probe, and one more at `0.2` where `0` loops harder; a value that moves `health` becomes a `GENAI_TEMPERATURE` default in `omnia-genai`, so the Ollama fallback gets it too. Nothing of the engine's own changed, so the release `emery` 0.39.0 stands for the large-model arm; `emery_omnia.wasm` is built from this tree over the engine's working-tree SDK through the root manifest's path patches, and `emery-dev` from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-genai` working tree through its path line, uncommented for the build and re-commented after; Round H's two artifacts are copied to `/tmp/roundH/` before either is built over, for the control.

**The method.** Every probe is `--resume 2026-10-21T06-28-53Z --at 2 --target omnia express-orders` — `health` beside `start-http-errors` in wave 2 over the large model's seeded tree, the one seed verify before it, the plan, the revision, and the brief the same as `2026-10-21T21-00-02Z`'s, so the rung's `health` turn reads against that card's: **202 s to the cap, `exhausted` at round 64, the forced report refused by `manifest_drift`; 25 `edit_files` calls and 75 edits, 64 of them over the skeleton and the test, 6.4 an edit per marker; 12 `find`s refused; 5 whole writes refused and 1 admitted; 3 `restore_files`; 5 `clippy -p health` runs; the last 14 rounds on the root `Cargo.toml`** — and against the large model's on `06-28-53Z`: 140 s, 22 tool calls, 7 shell, 3 clippy runs, 5 `edit_files` calls over 10 markers (two handlers, eight tests), its wave verified. The knobs are Round H's: `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=local`, `GENAI_MODEL=openai::Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`, `GENAI_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:11435/v1/`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS=64`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`, `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS=5`, `GENAI_MAX_TOKENS` at its default and recorded, the server restarted under the recipe with `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`. The puts, in order: **L0**, the large-model arm — the same command under the release `emery` and `composer-2.5` with the round's component, the pair's other half and the read of the three adapter items under the model they must not slow; **K**, the control — the same command under Round H's artifacts from `/tmp/roundH/` on the restarted server, so what the flag and the server's state move is read before the items; **I0**, rung 0 — the command under the round's artifacts; **I1**, rung 1 — the server restarted with the temperature override at `0` and the command again under the round's artifacts, and **I1′** at `0.2` where I1 loops harder than I0; then **the gate**: a `k = 2` probe of this round that builds `health` to its label opens the ladder under its configuration — `k = 3..6` over `06-28-53Z` one at a time, then the `--rebuild` of `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z`'s plan — and one that does not closes the round at the figures, the failure mode that remains naming Round J's rung. The readings are Round H's: the client's `round answered` lines, `/tmp/g_tools.py` over each `run-1.build.stderr`, the pod's `Engine 000:` lines over each window, a `/metrics` snapshot before and after each put; the runner's `--resume` line now names each slice it sealed. Each put is a quarter of an hour of pod time or less, the five together an hour and some $5 before the gate, against Round H's 1 h 57.

### Predictions

The shared clauses of Round H stand for every small-model put — no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, `schema and answer type disagree` 0, the grammar holding — and are credited to the server. The round's clauses:

- **L0, the large model under the items.** `health` built at its first candidate covering **8/8** and its wave verified, as on `06-28-53Z`, in **≤ 200 s** and **≤ 25** tool calls; the turn edits **no manifest**, the stem's `omnia-wasi-sql` line laid and the brief naming it; `undeclared_parts` finds **0** at the build and at every verify; every verify's fifth check lists `Cargo.lock` at most, under `.` or `"$WORKSPACE"`; `edit_files` **≤ 5 calls over 10 markers**, one fewer than the band's where the band's turn added the manifest line; the probe runs on to the label, **7/7 built, 6/6 verified**, in **≤ 1,800 s**, since every later brief is the large model's own over its own tree.
- **K, the control.** Repeats `21-00-02Z` within its spread: the seed verify answers `passed: true` at **round 64** in **110–140 s**; `health` `exhausted` at round 64 with its forced report refused, **≥ 3** whole writes refused by the guard, **≥ 1** `restore_files`, `find`s refused **≥ 8**, the root manifest edited; and the one move the server makes — `cache_read_tokens` **> 0** on the client's lines, the card's `cached` within **10%** of the server's hit rate over the put (above 0.95). A control that builds `health` says the server alone moved the arm, and every clause below is read against that.
- **I0, rung 0: the harness.** The seed verify's four checks pass at their first run and the verdict is `passed: true` **at or before round 64** in **≤ 130 s**; where any one call repeats identically, the fifth repeat makes the next round the finishing one and the verdict comes **within 2 rounds** of it, so a verify whose walk is one `stat` or one `find` answers in **≤ 40 s** after its checks; a verdict the gate refuses at a breaker-forced finish — a note in `failures` under `passed: true` — is corrected within the candidates, since rounds are left where the cap-forced one had none. **No put of the round is lost to the harness**: **0** `400`s in the server's log (the result cap and the `find`'s exclusion), **0** requests finishing `length` and no round past 32,768 tokens (the default `max_tokens`), **0** verify attempts `exhausted` at the cap with no verdict offered. `cache_read_tokens` **> 0** every round, as K's.
- **I0, rung 0: `health`'s turn.** The items read by the difference from K and `21-00-02Z`, the model and its sampling unchanged, so the copying stands and the harness's share goes: `restore_files` **0** (the refusal offers it no more); a whole write naming its path in `delete` beside `files` **admitted where it keeps the tokens** and no refusal for the `delete` alone; a `find` refused **≤ 2 times with the same arguments**, the failed-repeat breaker **unreached**, since the refusal names the diverging line; the stem's manifest holding `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` **before the turn**, **no `unresolved import omnia_wasi_sql`** in any clippy run, and **the root `Cargo.toml` untouched** — so the forced report, where the turn is forced, is refused by **nothing of the layout's lists** (`manifest_drift`, `dangling_entries`, `undeclared_parts`, `split_stems` each 0) and judged on the behaviour alone (`markers_left`, `untested_scenarios`, the gate); `find`s refused **≤ 12** and `edit_files` **≤ 6.4 edits a marker**, no better, since no item addresses the copy; `clippy -p health` **≤ 6** runs; the turn **≤ 300 s**. The gate's clause — `health` accepted at or before the forced round covering **≥ 6/8**, its wave verified — is predicted **not met** at rung 0 (none of the ten moves 0/7); a report accepted covering **≥ 2/8** is the harness's share returned to the slice and read as such.
- **I1, rung 1: the sampling at 0.** Against I0 and `21-00-02Z`: `find`s refused **≤ 4** a turn (against 12), **0** `find`s spelling `omnia` as anything else and **0** `edits` arguments that are not JSON (against one Windows command line), `edit_files` **≤ 3 edits a marker** (against 6.4), the design type and the inputs filled in the first five minutes as `orders-mutations`' were; the finish no worse, the report **at or before round 64**; the loop no worse — a succeeding call repeated identically **≤ 4** times on the build turn, the check loop run **≤ 2** times whole on the verify. The gate's clause met with **p ≈ 0.4**: `health` accepted covering **≥ 6/8** at or before the forced round, its wave verified, the module holding `SELECT 1` over `TableStore::query` and `StateStore::get` each awaited once with the `503` from the store alone, the probe tests arranging `on_query` and `Row`. **I1′ at 0.2** is put only where I1 loops harder than I0 — the breaker firing on `health`'s turn, a `find` sent identically five times, or the verify's check loop run three times whole — and is read under the same clauses.
- **Observed, never decided on:** each put's wall and tokens against K's and `21-00-02Z`'s; the server's prompt and generation throughput and the prefix cache hit rate against the client's `cached`; what each verify's walk is once a loop is cut (a different call each round, or the verdict); the breaker's notes and whether the model heeds the third; which references each turn reads; what the verifies seal; the sealed slices' `resumed` line at `k ≥ 3` once the ladder runs; how far a probe that builds `health` runs on, and what the later slices do over a tree partly this arm's.

### Decision rule

- **Rung 0 holds** when I0's harness clauses hold whole — no put lost to the harness, the seed verify answering, `restore_files` 0, the replacement shape admitted, the root manifest untouched, the forced report refused by none of the lists, `cached` reading — and L0 builds `health` to its label with `undeclared_parts` 0 and no manifest edited: the ten items ship as they stand, the `omnia-backends` ones in a release the next `emery-dev` pins. **A clause failing names its item**: a `400` is the result cap or the `find`'s exclusion, read from the server's log for which; a `length` finish or a round past 32,768 tokens is the default `max_tokens` not applied, read from the request; a verify `exhausted` at the cap with no verdict is the finishing round, item 8's, not this round's; a verify looping an identical call past five results is the succeeding breaker; a `restore_files` taken is the refusal's wording read again; a refusal for the `delete` alone is the replacement rule; a stale `find` sent three times identical is the diverging-line refusal, the refusals read for whether they named the line; an `unresolved import omnia_wasi_sql` or a root-manifest edit is the laid line, the scaffold's brief read for whether it named it; a module moved through the shell at a verify and no finding is `undeclared_parts`; `cached` 0 with the flag set is the client's reading of `prompt_tokens_details`, `omnia-genai`'s. L0 failing a clause of its own — `health` not built, a manifest edited, a finding at the verify — is the adapter item named, read under the large model before any small-model clause is credited to it.
- **The gate.** A `k = 2` put of this round — I0, I1, or I1′ — that **builds `health` to its label** (accepted at or before the forced round covering ≥ 6/8, its wave verified, the host's checks agreeing) opens the ladder under its configuration: `k = 3..6` over `06-28-53Z`, one at a time, under the same server and knobs, then the `--rebuild` of `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z`'s plan; a probe that builds from wave 2 to the label (7/7, 6/6, the host's checks agreeing) stands as the small-model band under `Qwen3-Coder-Next` in place of `05-21-29Z` and `07-04-07Z`, as Round H's rule had it, and a `--rebuild` that builds 7/7 and verifies 6/6 is the arm's first whole build. **No put building `health`** closes the round at its figures: the failure mode that remains is read across I0 and I1 — `find`s refused and edits a marker over the band's are the copying, rung 2's (the model) and rung 3's (the edit addressing) to read; rounds spent to the cap with the edits accepted and the checks run is the finishing, read for whether the rounds were productive; coverage claimed over ignored tests, a store stubbed under a comment, a module or a crate imagined, with the harness clauses holding, is the behaviour the training rule names — and Round J is rung 2.
- **Rung 1 holds** when I1 (or I1′ where put) moves the copy figures by its clause — `find`s refused ≤ 4, edits a marker ≤ 3, `omnia` never misspelled — whether or not `health` builds: the value becomes `GENAI_TEMPERATURE` in `omnia-genai`, defaulted so the Ollama fallback gets it, and the ladder's later rungs run under it. **I1 failing its copy clauses** with the loop no worse says the copying is not the sampling's — the figures recorded as the rung's read, rung 2 next; I1 looping harder than I0 puts I1′, and I1′ failing the same way closes the rung the same way. A value that moves one figure and worsens another (fewer refusals, more rounds to the report) is recorded and not adopted.
- **The control is read first and subtracted.** A clause K already meets — the `cached` reading, the seed verify's wall, a verify answering at the cap — is the server's or item 8's and credited to no item of this round; a clause K fails and I0 holds is the items' by the difference. K building `health` under Round H's artifacts says the server alone moved the arm, and every clause is then read against K's figures rather than `21-00-02Z`'s.
- **Attribution.** The plan, the revision, the model, the server, and the knobs are one across K and I0, so the difference is the items' together, read per clause as above; the sampling alone differs between I0 and I1, so that difference is rung 1's; the model alone differs between L0 and I0, so a clause L0 meets and I0 fails over the same brief is the model's and no item's. A clause failing on `health` alone where the ladder runs is the slice's; failing on three or more slices is the harness's, the clause named across them.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the put not made, or made as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the put made again from its start; a put the restarted server refuses at startup (the override's JSON, the flag unknown to the vLLM on the pod) is the recipe's, the server's log quoted, and the put waits on it.
