**Decision — rung 0 holds and the ten items ship; the gate is met at rung 0 and the ladder runs to its last slice without a build to the label; rung 1 does not hold and no temperature is adopted; the training trigger has fired.**

- **Rung 0 holds.** I0's harness clauses hold whole on `health`'s turn and the seed verify: the seed verify answered at round 49 in 102 s on the breaker's first firing; 0 `400`s under the round's artifacts (K's one was Round H's `find` under Round H's component, the control doing its work), 0 `length`, no verify `exhausted` at the cap without a verdict offered; `restore_files` 0 on `health`, the replacement shape admitted, one `find` refused and none three times with the same arguments, no `unresolved import omnia_wasi_sql`, the root manifest untouched, the forced report refused by nothing (and accepted at 8/8), `cached` 97.0% against the server's 0.970. L0 built `health` to its label with no manifest edited and `undeclared_parts` 0 — and the adapter's new finding fired twice later under the small model exactly where it was written to (k = 5's three `mv`ed modules). **The ten items ship as they stand**, the three `omnia-backends` ones (`50b6336f`) in the release the next `emery-dev` pins. **Two clauses were missed and both are the model's**: `clippy -p health` ran 19 times against ≤ 6 and the turn took 325 s against ≤ 300 — L0 ran it 3 times in 124 s over the same brief. **The round found an eleventh harness item and two readings beside the ten**: the keep-alive race (`transport` at the client on an idle gap of 4–5 s, four verify first attempts absorbed by the retry and one put lost to it twice — `omnia-genai` retries a request once on a connection error met before any byte of the response, or holds the pool's idle timeout under uvicorn's 5 s); the runner checks the template against the seed before a resume, since `manifest_drift` at L0's seed verify was the template's `0.37.0` against `06-28-53Z`'s `0.36.0` and every small-model put of the round resumed from L0's tree in consequence (a deviation recorded, the plan and briefs the same); and `restore_files` taken as a step with no refusal before it (twice on I0's `customers`, three times on k = 4's `orders-reading`, each discarding the edits since) — the tool's description, not the refusal's wording, is where that reading goes. A fourth, the adapter's: a report covering 9/9 (k = 4) and 8/8 (the rebuild's `customers`) over a module that does not compile or fails seven tests of ten says the report gate reads markers, tests, and lists and never the compiler — `cargo check -p <stem>` at the gate would send the turn back with the error where the verify now finds it a wave later.
- **The gate was met twice**: I0 (8/8, 325 s) and I1 (7/8, 154 s), each wave verified with the host's checks agreeing — the harness items alone moved `health` from Round H's forced report refused by `manifest_drift` to a merge, the model and its sampling unchanged between K and I0. K did not build `health` (2/8 at the cap, refused), so the server alone did not move the arm. The ladder ran under I0's configuration, default sampling.
- **Rung 1 does not hold, and `GENAI_TEMPERATURE` is not adopted.** I1 at temperature 0 met two copy clauses (`find`s refused 1, as I0's 1, against the clause's ≤ 4 and K's 4; `omnia` misspelled by neither — the misspelling of Round H did not recur at either temperature under the diverging-line refusal) and failed the third (4.8 edits a marker against ≤ 3 and I0's 2.5), finished faster (154 s against 325) with a lower report (7/8) and **looped harder** — the breaker firing on both build turns where I0's reached neither — which put I1′ at 0.2, lost at its seed verify to a cap-forced verdict with a note in `failures`, no repeat for the breaker to count. A value that moves one figure and worsens another is recorded and not adopted; the server stays at the model's `generation_config.json`, and the copying is not the sampling's.
- **The ladder, under rung 0 alone.** No probe built from wave 2 to the label and the `--rebuild` built 2/7, so **C1 stays the small-model band by the rule's letter** (a build to the label was the clause), with `09-25-05Z` recorded beside it as the farthest the arm has built under any model — `health` from a fresh seed 8/8 and verified, `customers` merged, a verify refusing honestly — the arm's figure for the next card to read against rather than C1's wave 2. What remains at k ≥ 3, read across the four slices and the rebuild's third wave with the harness clauses holding: **the behaviour the training rule names, on every slice** — an API imagined (k = 3's `table_store`, `table_type`, `.tables()`; k = 4's `Field::as_i64`; k = 6's `TableStore` unimported and an `.await` in a closure), modules named and never written (k = 5's `customer::`, `delivery_zones::`, `shipping::`), coverage claimed over what does not compile or does not pass (k = 4's 9/9, the rebuild's 8/8), tests left `#[ignore]` (k = 6's sixteen), and the verify repairing by moving the layout rather than the module (k = 5's three crates, k = 6's `common`, k = 4's `cargo add` at the root) — each caught by the checks or the lists, none repairable by a harness. Beside it **the finishing**: every build turn of the round under the small model ran to round 64 or to the breaker (I0's 325 s against L0's 124; the rebuild's `customers` 571 against 222), and **the copying moved**: `find`s refused 1–7 a turn against Round H's 12–13, edits a marker 2.3–4.8 against 6.4, `omnia` never misspelled — rung 0's diverging-line refusal did what rung 1 was predicted to.
- **Round J.** The protocol's stop rule reads as fired: `k = 2` builds under rung 0, and what remains at `k ≥ 3` is what the checks catch and no harness repairs — so **Round J is the training's first step, Phase 5's harvest** over the large-model cards (`06-28-53Z`, `05-26-59Z`, and the bands before them) into 2-turn trajectories, with the rebuild's and the ladder's refused turns as the repair pairs' rejected side. **Rung 2 — the model — is read beside it at `k = 2` for about a dollar before the first pairs are cut**, since a dense coder that copies and finishes where this one does not changes what the dataset must teach; the operator who reads "the best untrained configuration" as rungs 2–4 run first makes Round J rung 2 instead, and the card's figures serve either. The harness items above (the connection retry, the template-against-seed check, `restore_files`' description, the report gate's `cargo check`) are Round J's rung-0 tail, landed before its probes. **Deviations recorded**: the seed drift and every small-model put resuming from L0's card `05-26-59Z` rather than `06-28-53Z` (the plan, briefs, and `health` seal the same; the template's `0.37.0` and `tokio 1.54.1` the difference); K's one `400`; I0's first put not put (`06-25-55Z`, the drift); k = 6 put three times under the tunnel clause (the race, then the tunnel); the transport first attempts absorbed by the engine's retry on four verifies.

## Round J — the rung-0 tail and the model

[Round I](#round-i--the-harness-debt-and-the-sampling) read rung 0 (holds; the ten items ship), rung 1 (does not hold; no temperature adopted), and ran the ladder under rung 0 to its last slice without a build to the label: `k = 2` builds, and what remains at `k ≥ 3` is what the checks catch and no harness repairs, so the protocol's stop rule reads as fired and Phase 5's harvest is the training's first step. Before the first pairs are cut, this round reads **rung 2 — the model** — at `k = 2`, since a dense coder that copies and finishes where `Qwen3-Coder-Next` does not changes what the dataset must teach, and lands **the rung-0 tail**, the four harness items Round I found, before its probes. Three land. In `omnia-backends` (`crates/genai`), the keep-alive race: the client's pooled connections are dropped after **2 s idle**, inside the 5 s uvicorn keeps one, so a tool round of four or five seconds never hands a request to a socket the server is closing; and a request that fails at the transport before any reply — a connection closed as it was written on, a tunnel dropping — is **put again unchanged after 1, 5, then 15 s**, the completion line counting them as `retries`, and only past the third is the failure the completion's `transport`; the fake provider gains a fault that closes the first connection unread, and the row `dropped_connection_retried` reads `ok`, `retries=1`, one request read. In the eval runner, `--resume` **reads the component's template against the seed** before the label is moved: the root manifest held outside its two stem lists and every static file byte for byte, through `omnia-template` at this workspace's lock, the template `emery_omnia.wasm` built from the same lock embeds, read out of the commit the resume starts at — a seed the template has moved under is **refused with the drift** as the gate would spell it, unless `--drifted` admits it, and the card's RESUME line says so when it does; L0's path, the large model repairing the drift in the seed verify, stays open through the flag. In the engine's SDK, `restore_files` is described to the model as **recovery after a refusal, not a step** — the turn's edits to the file discarded with it, a file whose edits stand not restored — in the tool's description and in the brief's sentence beside it, where Round I read it taken five times with no refusal before it. **The fourth does not land and is deferred**: the report gate is the guest's `check`, which receives the candidate text alone — the shell is the host's tool, its turns never reach the guest, and a WASI guest spawns nothing — so `cargo check -p <stem>` at the gate is either a host-run check `wasi-model` would have to carry or an engine-level repair turn after acceptance, neither a rung-0 item; the design goes to Open, and the reading stands: a report covering 9/9 (k = 4) or 8/8 (the rebuild's `customers`) over a module that compiles nowhere is read on this machine over the merged commit, as Round I read it. Nothing of the engine's release changed: `emery_omnia.wasm` is built from this tree over the engine's working-tree SDK through the root manifest's path patches; `emery-dev` from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-backends` working tree through its four path lines, uncommented for the build and re-commented after; the runner from this tree by `cargo run -p evals`.

**The model.** `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct`, the dense 32B coder, as **`RedHatAI/Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct-FP8-dynamic`** (compressed-tensors FP8, 34.3 GB in 7 safetensors, the Qwen chat template with `<tool_call>`), served by the pod's vLLM 0.32.0 beside the existing recipe and never over it: `--tool-call-parser hermes --enable-auto-tool-choice`, `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, and the model's **32,768 native context stretched to 131,072 by YaRN at factor 4** (`--hf-overrides` with `rope_type yarn`, `original_max_position_embeddings 32768`; `--max-model-len 131072`), the factor Qwen names for 131K and no further, since static YaRN scales every request and a larger factor degrades the short ones. The ceiling is the rung's: Round I's requests ran to 100–200 K prompt tokens on the turns that reached the cap (13 of I0's 336; 101 of the rebuild's 1,190), and a request past 131,072 is refused by the server (`400`, `maximum context length`), which the client reports as `provider` — read below as the model's context, not the harness. The dense model decodes at a fraction of the MoE's 180–216 tokens a second, so the same turn takes longer by the token.

**The method.** Every probe is `--resume 2026-10-22T05-26-59Z --at 2 --target omnia express-orders`, Round I's command — `health` beside `start-http-errors` in wave 2 over L0's tree at `da8fc02f`, the pins the template's, the one seed verify before it, the plan `c2c52172`, the briefs the same — read against I0 (`06-30-16Z`: 325 s, 69 calls, 19 clippy runs, 2.5 edits a marker, 4 guard refusals, 1 `find` refused, 8/8 forced at round 64, verified), I1 (`07-09-12Z`: 154 s, 4.8 a marker, the breaker, 7/8), and L0 (124 s, 25 calls, 3 clippy runs, 2.1 a marker, 8/8 at the first candidate). The knobs are Round I's: `EMERY_BIN=../emery/target/release/emery-dev`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=local`, `GENAI_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:11435/v1/`, `EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS=64`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`, `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS=5`, `GENAI_MAX_TOKENS` at its default; `GENAI_MODEL=openai::Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` for the control and `openai::Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct-FP8` for the rung. The puts, in order: **the staging's read** — the runner put at `--resume 2026-10-21T06-28-53Z --at 2` with no server up, which must refuse the seed naming the `0.36.0` and `1.53.3` pins before anything is spent, and at `05-26-59Z`, which must pass the read and reach the server (the put then stopped at its first request, nothing listening, and not counted); **J0**, the control — the round's three artifacts under `Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` on Round I's recipe, so the items' moves and the model's are read apart; **J1**, rung 2 — vLLM restarted under the dense recipe and the command again; then **the gate**: J1 building `health` to its label opens **k = 4** under the dense model (`--at 4`, `orders-reading` first, the slice I0's configuration covered 9/9 over a module that compiled nowhere — the discriminating read), and k = 4's wave verified opens the `--rebuild` of `2026-10-19T04-08-24Z`'s plan; J1 not building `health` closes the round at `k = 2`. The readings are Round I's: the client's `round answered` and `completion` lines (`retries` now among the fields), `/tmp/g_tools.py` over each `run-1.build.stderr`, the host's checks over each merged commit on this machine, a `/metrics` snapshot before and after each put, the server's log for every `400`. J0 and J1 are a quarter of an hour each of pod time beside the dense model's download; k = 4 half an hour; the rebuild an hour and a half.

**The model, changed before the rung's put** (written after J0 and before J1; the predictions above stand as written). `RedHatAI/Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct-FP8-dynamic` was served on the recipe above and does not make a tool call the server can parse under `tool_choice: auto`: asked to read a file with `read` and `shell` declared, it answers in the content — `{"name": "read", "arguments": {"path": "crates/health/src/lib.rs"}}` bare, or wrapped in `<tools>…</tools>` — with `tool_calls` empty and `finish_reason: stop`, the hermes parser reading `<tool_call>` tags the model does not write; `tool_choice: required` alone yields a parsed call, and the client never sends it. Read the same with YaRN and without (`--max-model-len 32768`, no `--hf-overrides`), with `--tool-strict-level function` and without, over three server starts (`/workspace/vllm-J1.log`, `-J1b.log`, `-J1c.log` on the pod); so under the harness every turn would end at round 1 with prose, which reads the format and not the model, and the rung is put under **`Qwen/Qwen3-32B-FP8`** instead — the dense 32B of the Qwen3 line in Qwen's own FP8 (block-scaled 128 × 128, 32 GB in 17 files), the family's `<tool_call>` format native to its template, **thinking off** through the template's `enable_thinking` (`--default-chat-template-kwargs '{"enable_thinking": false}'`), served as `Qwen3-32B-FP8` with the hermes parser, `--tool-strict-level function`, and the same YaRN at factor 4 over 32,768 (the model's own `max_position_embeddings` is 40,960, the native window plus a thinking budget; Qwen's card names the same factor for 131K). The same probe under it parses every case — the two-step instruction yielding `read` then `shell` as two tool calls in one turn, `finish_reason: tool_calls`. What this changes in the read: the rung's model is a general instruct model and not a code-specialised one, so the copying clauses read a dense 32B rather than a dense 32B *coder*, and a hold or fail is the dense size's at this family's generation, not the Qwen2.5 coder's. `GENAI_MODEL=openai::Qwen3-32B-FP8` for the rung. The Qwen2.5 recipe is kept on the pod as `/workspace/start-vllm-qwen25coder.sh` for the record.

### Predictions

The shared clauses of Round H stand for every put — no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, `schema and answer type disagree` 0 — credited to the server; under the dense model's hermes parser they are the rung's own and read as such. The round's clauses:

- **The staging.** `dropped_connection_retried` passes beside the suite; the runner refuses `06-28-53Z` naming `omnia* = "0.36.0"` and `tokio = "1.53.3"` as `-`/`+` lines of `Cargo.toml` before any request, and passes `05-26-59Z` without a line.
- **J0, the control.** The items alone: **0 completions `transport`** over the put (I0 had four verify first attempts and k = 6 was lost to the race) and every `retries > 0` line ending `ok` or `corrected`; `restore_files` **0** on every build turn, `health`'s and `customers`' (I0's `customers` took it twice); no `400` in the server's log; the seed verify `ok` in **≤ 130 s** on the breaker's first firing. The model unchanged: `health` built covering **≥ 6/8** and its wave verified with **p ≈ 0.7** (met twice of two under the artifacts), **≤ 350 s**, `clippy -p health` **≤ 19**, `find`s refused **≤ 4**, edits a marker **2–5**, the turn to round 64 or the breaker as every small-model build turn of Round I was; `cached` within 1% of the server's hit rate.
- **J1, rung 2: the copying.** Against J0 and I0: edits a marker **≤ 2.5**, whole writes refused by the content guard **0** (I0's 4), `find`s refused **≤ 2**, `omnia` misspelled **0** times, `edits` arguments that are not JSON **≤ 1**.
- **J1, rung 2: the finishing.** The report **accepted at or before round 40** — a dense instruct model answering when its checks pass rather than running to round 64 or the breaker (every small-model build turn of Round I did one or the other; L0 answered at its first candidate) — `clippy -p health` **≤ 8** runs, the turn **≤ 600 s** by the wall at the dense model's decode rate. The gate's clause — `health` accepted covering **≥ 6/8**, its wave verified, the host's checks agreeing — **p ≈ 0.5**.
- **J1, rung 2: the ceiling and the grammar.** **0** requests refused for length on `health`'s turn and **≤ 2** over the put, each at a verify past round 50; **0** `400`s of any other kind; **0** undeclared tools under the hermes parser; the seed verify `ok` in **≤ 200 s**. Generation **50–100 tokens a second** a request against the MoE's 180–216; mean TTFT **≤ 1 s**.
- **k = 4 under the dense model** (put only where J1 builds `health`): `orders-reading` accepted covering **≥ 6/9** and the merged module **compiling on this machine** (`cargo check -p orders` over the merged commit) with **p ≈ 0.4** — the discriminating clause, since I0's configuration covered 9/9 over `cannot find crate omnia_wasi_sql` and `no method as_i64`; **0** API names imagined (`as_i64`, `table_store`, `table_type`, `.tables()`) in the merged module; the wave's verify not refused by `manifest_drift`, the root manifest untouched by `cargo add`.
- **The `--rebuild`** (put only where k = 4's wave is verified): **≥ 3/7** built with every verify honest, **p ≈ 0.3**, against `09-25-05Z`'s 2/7.
- **Observed, never decided on:** each put's wall and tokens against I0's; the dense model's prefix-cache hit rate against the hybrid's 0.97; the `retries` counts and where each fell (after a tool round of how many seconds); which references each turn reads; what the dense model does at a content-guard refusal, if any; how far a probe that builds `health` runs on; the prompt-size histogram against the 131,072 ceiling.

### Decision rule

- **The rung-0 tail ships** — the retry and the idle timeout, the seed read, the description — when the staging's read holds and J0 meets its item clauses: `transport` 0 with every retried request answered, `restore_files` 0, the seed read naming `06-28-53Z`'s drift and passing `05-26-59Z`. **A clause failing names its item**: a `transport` completion with `retries` at 3 is the waits too short for what dropped (the tunnel's reopen is 10 s), read from the tunnel log for whether the tunnel was down; a `transport` with `retries` 0 is a failure the retry does not classify, the error quoted; a `restore_files` taken with no refusal before it is the description read again, the turn's refusals listed to show there was none; a seed read passing a drifted seed or refusing a sound one is the runner's template against the component's, the two locks compared. The fourth item stays deferred whatever the figures, its design in Open.
- **Rung 2 holds** when J1 builds `health` to its label (accepted at or before the forced round covering ≥ 6/8, its wave verified, the host's checks agreeing) **and** meets its copy and finish clauses — edits a marker ≤ 2.5, guard refusals 0, the report at or before round 40 — with no request refused for length on the turn: the dense model is the arm's model, Phase 5's dataset is cut for it, and k = 4 and the rebuild run under it as the ladder's read. **Rung 2 does not hold** when J1 fails the gate, or meets it with the copy figures no better than J0's and the report at round 64 or the breaker — the figures recorded as the rung's read, the arm's model stays `Qwen3-Coder-Next`, and the harvest proceeds for it. **The ceiling** — a request refused for length that loses `health`'s turn — closes the rung the same way whatever the copy figures: the harness's conversations exceed the model's context at the gate's own slice, so the model is not the arm's, and the read on the copying is taken from the rounds before the refusal and recorded as partial.
- **The control is read first and subtracted.** The plan, the tree, the server, and the knobs are one across I0 and J0, the three items the difference, so a clause J0 meets and I0 failed is the items'; a clause I0 already met (`health` built, the root manifest untouched) is credited to no item of this round. The model alone differs between J0 and J1 over the same brief, so that difference is rung 2's; a clause J1 fails that J0 also fails is the arm's, not the model's.
- **The band.** `09-25-05Z` — 2/7 built, two waves verified, a verify refusing honestly — is adopted as **the small-model band under `Qwen3-Coder-Next`**, in place of C1's wave 2: a band is what the next card reads against, and C1's wave 2 says nothing about `k ≥ 3`; Round H's letter (a build to the label) named a figure no small model has made and reads as the arm's goal, not its band. A J1 `--rebuild` building ≥ 3/7 with every verify honest is the band under the dense model, read beside it.
- **The gate.** `k = 2` stays the gate for this round's economy, and k = 4 is its second read where the gate is met; the recommendation for the protocol, whichever way J1 falls, is that a rung's gate reads `k = 2` and `k = 4` together once rung 0 holds — `health` was met twice in Round I and predicted nothing about the slices that need the store API — and the README's protocol paragraph is amended when the maintainer takes it.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the put not made, or made as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the put made again from its start — the retry absorbing a drop of under 21 s and a longer one still the completion's `transport`; a put the dense recipe refuses at startup (the `hf-overrides` JSON, the parser's name, the checkpoint's quantization unknown to the vLLM on the pod) is the recipe's, the server's log quoted, and the put waits on it. The pod is stopped at the round's end whatever the figures.

**Decision — the rung-0 tail ships in two of its three landed items and the third stays open; rung 2 does not hold, and the arm's model stays `Qwen3-Coder-Next`; `09-25-05Z` is the small-model band; the gate reads `k = 2` and `k = 4` together from the next rung on.**

- **The retry and the idle timeout ship.** The staging's read holds (the drifted seed refused naming its pins before any request, the sound one passed, the retry's path walked 1-5-15 s to `transport` with `retries=3`), and J0 met its clauses: `transport` 0 over 226 requests where I0 lost two first attempts of four completions, no `retries > 0` line at all — the race not run rather than absorbed, which is what a 2 s idle timeout under a 5 s keep-alive should read as. **The seed read ships**: `06-28-53Z` refused with the drift as the gate would spell it, `05-26-59Z` passed, both puts resumed over the right tree with the line in the log. **The description does not close its item.** `restore_files` was taken five times on J0's `customers`, three of them with no refusal before — after admitted edits and a clippy run each time, the turn's refusals listed above (two refused `find`s, one not-JSON write, one guard refusal, all before 17:09:46) — and the description as landed says *a refused or mistaken write*, which a model reading a compile error as its own mistake takes as leave; the wording stands (it says what the tool is) and the item goes back to Open sharper: the description names a refusal alone, or the engine declares the tool only on the round after a refusal, so a turn with none cannot take it. The fourth item (the report gate's `cargo check`) stays deferred with its design in Open, and J1 read what it would catch from the other side: a module that compiles and a report covering nothing, where I-round's reports covered everything over modules that did not.
- **Rung 2 does not hold.** J1 failed the gate — `health` accepted at round 26 covering 0/8, its wave lost at fmt — and failed two copy clauses (2.9 a marker with 25 of 29 edits refused; 5 `find` calls refused against ≤ 2) while meeting every finishing and ceiling clause in form: the dense model answers when its chained check passes and does not loop (the report at 26, both verifies at rounds 7 and 9, no breaker fired anywhere), and its context was never near the ceiling (71 K of 131 K). What it cannot do is the copying at its root: three `find`s on the same marker, each the brief's one-line spelling against the formatter's three-line layout, the refusal's diverging line read and not used; then the module written whole six times instead, and the tests never opened. So **the arm's model stays `Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`**, Phase 5's dataset is cut for it, and no k = 4 or `--rebuild` ran under the dense model. The rung's read is recorded with its deviation: the model read is a general dense 32B of the Qwen3 line and not the dense coder the method named, since that coder's tool calls parse under no parser the server has; a dense coder whose template writes `<tool_call>` tags (or the Qwen2.5 coder under a chat template that forces them) is a cheap re-read at `k = 2` for whoever wants it, with the J1 figures as its control. **The rung's new mode is a recipe item**: `--tool-strict-level parameter` would have the server hold `write_files`' arguments to the tool's schema, where `function` held the name and let five shapeless calls through to the SDK's refusal.
- **The control is read first and subtracted.** J0 and I0 met the gate under the same model and server with the three items the difference, so the gate's clause at `k = 2` is now met three times of three under rung 0 and reads as the arm's floor at that slice; `restore_files` on `customers` is J0's and I0's alike (5 and 2), so it is the arm's, the item's wording moving nothing; J1 fails the gate J0 met over the same brief, so the gap is the model's.
- **The band.** **`09-25-05Z` — 2/7 built, two waves verified, wave 3's verify refusing a failing test honestly — is the small-model band under `Qwen3-Coder-Next`**, in place of C1's wave 2, as the rule has it; no band exists under the dense model since no rebuild ran. The next card reads its `--rebuild` against 2/7 with every verify honest, and reads a build to the label as the arm's goal.
- **The gate.** `k = 2` was this round's gate and was met at rung 0 three times without predicting the slices that need the store API, so **the recommendation stands: from rung 3 on, a rung's gate reads `k = 2` and `k = 4` together** (`health` and `orders-reading`, about half an hour of pod time), and the README's protocol paragraph is the maintainer's to amend. Rung 3 — the edit addressing — is what both J0's and J1's copying point at: a `find` that names a marker by its id or an anchor by its line, no copied span, would have taken J1's first `edit_files` and J0's three refused ones.
- **Deviations recorded**: the pod (`kfon5fxhta2bcr` unstartable, `gg4v13ht9fw0t9` created and set up from nothing, stopped at the round's end, its container disk wiped with it); the model (`Qwen3-32B-FP8` for the Qwen2.5 coder, written before J1 with the probe quoted); the staging's read repeated after the round with no server for the quotation; J0's `customers` lost to the restore loop and the test's syntax, the run `bad_request`; no tunnel drop, no `transport`, no `400` to record.
- **Round K.** Phase 5's harvest over the large-model cards (`06-28-53Z`, `05-26-59Z`, and the bands before them) into 2-turn trajectories, with Round I's ladder and rebuild and this round's J0 `customers` and J1 `health` as the repair pairs' rejected side — J1's a new shape for the set, a report covering nothing over a module that compiles. Beside it rung 3 at `k = 2` and `k = 4`, and the Open items this round leaves: the report gate's `cargo check` (deferred, designed), `restore_files` withheld or re-described, `--tool-strict-level parameter` in any dense recipe, and whether `edit_files` should match a `find` modulo the formatter's line breaks — the divergence that cost J1 25 edits and the brief's citations their use.

## Round K — the edit addressing and the table manifest line

[Round J](#round-j--the-rung-0-tail-and-the-model) read rung 2 (does not hold; the arm's model stays `Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8`) and left the gate wider — `k = 2` and `k = 4` together from rung 3 on — with four items in Open. This round is **rung 3, the edit addressing**, and lands the three items beside it that Round J named, before the two gate probes. Every build turn of the small-model arm since Round H has spent its rounds copying spans it cannot copy: J1's three refused `find`s on one marker were the brief's one-line `todo!("REQ-016 start.health-live: the handler")` against the skeleton's `todo!(` broken over three lines by the formatter; J0's four were three matching nothing and one matching twice; I-round's ran 1–7 a turn against Round H's 12–13 once the diverging-line refusal landed. The brief cites every marker and the model copies the citation, so the item is that a marker is addressed by what the brief quotes and never by its lines. Four land, all in the working trees and uncommitted:

1. **Rung 3, the edit addressing** (the engine's SDK, `crates/sdk/src/target.rs` and `target/write.rs`; the adapter, `adapters/omnia/src/scaffold.rs`, `scaffold/brief.rs`, `scaffold/render.rs`, `prose/build.md`). A `Guarded` file carries `markers`, one `Marker { key, block }` per marker the scaffold laid in it, and `edit_files` places a `find` in three steps: **the key first** — a `find` that is a marker's key, trimmed, replaces the block the scaffold laid for it where the file still holds it, and is refused naming the key where the file no longer does (`find names the marker …, but the file no longer holds the block the scaffold laid for it; copy what stands there into find`); **the exact span once** otherwise; and, where that matches nothing, **the span with its layout read past** — whitespace runs dropped outside string literals and `//` comments, one space kept between identifier characters, so a `find` copied from the brief matches where the formatter has broken its lines or moved its indentation, the match's indentation and trailing whitespace mirrored from the file. The key runs before the exact match because every key stands verbatim inside the file's `todo!("…")` literal, and an exact match there would replace the literal's inside. The refusal's `nearest` reads each line trimmed and, where no line holds the `find`'s first line whole, names the opener (`todo!(`) and the line after it. The adapter attaches the keys: a handler's and a consumer's `todo!` text over the stub (`let _ = (&input, &context);` and the `todo!`), a function's over the function from its signature to its closing brace (`render::function_block`, since the behaviour decides the signature too), a design type's key over its empty `pub struct <Name> {}` marker, a test's `#[ignore]` text and its `todo!` text each over the test block; a held module an earlier slice's, so none. The brief quotes each marker's key beside its block (`key \`REQ-001 orders.read: the handler\``), its heading says the `find` is the key alone and the `replace` the whole text that takes the block's place indented as the block is, the SDK's guard paragraph adds the same sentence and the layout tolerance, and `build.md`'s step 3, its `## The markers` lead, and every worked `edits` fence spell the key as the `find`. The SDK suite reads the three steps and the two refusals over a scripted tree (`edit_marker_key`, `edit_marker_scoped`, `edit_relaid`, `edit_nearest_broken`); `tests/target.rs` reads the adapter's keys through `omnia_scaffold_keyed` — the handler's, the test's, and a design type's blocks each replaced by an edit whose `find` is the key alone, and a key of one file plain text in another.
2. **The table manifest line** (`adapters/omnia/src/scaffold.rs`, `layout.rs`, `scaffold/render.rs`, `scaffold/brief.rs`). `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` is laid after the six fixed lines of a stem's manifest where the slice selects the probe idiom **or its roles name `Role::Table`** (`Stem.sql`), a held manifest taking it as a `Place::FileEnd` line the guard then keeps, the brief saying it was added or already held and what for — the probe's `Row`, or `for the SQL store the slice reaches`. Round I's `k = 4` merged `orders-reading` 9/9 over `cannot find module or crate omnia_wasi_sql` eight times, the design's `Store (PostgreSQL via pg:Pool)` paragraph selecting `Role::Table` and the scaffold laying the crate for the probe idiom alone, and its verify ran `cargo add omnia-wasi-sql` at the workspace root. `omnia_scaffold_roles_scoped` reads the line and the brief's clause over the design that selects the role.
3. **`restore_files` described to a refused `write_files` alone** (the SDK's tool description and `build.md`): *recovery after a refused `write_files` call alone, never after `edit_files` or a failed check*, where Round J's wording said *a refused or mistaken write* and J0 took the tool three times after admitted edits and a clippy run. The second cut Round J named — the tool declared only on the round after a refusal — stays in Open, since this one is read first.
4. **`--tool-strict-level parameter`** in the pod's recipe (`evals/README.md`, the cards' README), so the server holds a call's arguments to the tool's schema where `function` held the name and let J1's five shapeless `write_files` calls through to the SDK's refusal.

Beside them the housekeeping Round J left: `omnia_manifest_refused` derives its `-`/`+` lines from `OMNIA_PIN` and `OMNIA_PIN_EDITED` rather than literals the template has moved past, and the protocol paragraph reads the gate as `k = 2` and `k = 4` together over `2026-10-22T05-26-59Z`, `09-25-05Z` the band.

**The method.** Two probes, in order, each `--resume 2026-10-22T05-26-59Z --target omnia express-orders` under `openai::Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next-FP8` on Round I's recipe with `--tool-strict-level parameter` for `function`, `emery-dev` built from the engine's working tree over the `omnia-backends` release Round J's items shipped in, the component `emery_omnia.wasm` built from this tree over the engine's working-tree SDK through the root manifest's path patches, the runner from this tree, the knobs Round J's (`EVAL_RUNS=1`, `EVAL_JOBS=1`, `EVAL_BUILD_RUNG=7200/600`, `GENAI_MAX_ROUNDS=64`, `GENAI_MAX_CANDIDATES=3`, `GENAI_MAX_REPEATS=5`). **`--at 2`**, `health` beside `start-http-errors` in wave 2 over L0's tree at `da8fc02f`, read against **J0** (`2026-10-22T16-59-50Z`: 214 s, 63 calls, 10 `clippy -p health` runs, 37 edits in 22 calls — **3.7 a marker** — **4 `find`s refused**, three matching nothing and one matching twice, 6 whole writes admitted and 0 refused, `restore_files` 0, 8/8 forced at round 64, the wave verified at round 33) and L0 (124 s, 25 calls, 2.1 a marker, 8/8 at the first candidate). **`--at 4`**, `orders-reading` first over the label moved to `customers`' commit, read against **Round I's `k = 4`** (`2026-10-22T07-54-01Z`: 363 s, 64 calls, 32 `edit_files`, 9 clippy runs, the design type, the inputs, the outputs, both handlers, and all twelve scenario tests filled, **3 `restore_files` on no refusal** each putting the module back to the skeleton, **9/9 forced at round 64 over a module that compiles nowhere** — `cannot find module or crate omnia_wasi_sql` eight times, `no method named as_i64 found for Field` — and **wave 4's verify `exhausted`, its forced verdict refused by `manifest_drift`** after `cargo add omnia-wasi-sql` at the root) and the large model (208 s, 46 calls, 9/9, the wave verified in 88 s). The seed verify before each is the runner's one, over the moved label. The readings are Round I's: the client's `round answered` and `completion` lines, `/tmp/g_tools.py` over each `run-1.build.stderr` for the calls, the edits, the refusals, and the `find`s — a keyed edit read off its `find` being a key the brief quoted — the host's checks and `cargo check -p <stem>` over each merged commit on this machine, a `/metrics` snapshot before and after each put, the server's log for every `400`. About half an hour of pod time for the two; the ladder and the rebuild, where the gate opens them, two hours more.

### Predictions

The shared clauses stand for every put — no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, `schema and answer type disagree` 0, `transport` 0 with every `retries > 0` line ending `ok` or `corrected`, `400`s 0, `length` 0, `cached` within 1% of the server's hit rate — credited to the rungs before this one. The round's clauses:

- **The copying, `k = 2`.** `find`s refused **≤ 1** on `health`'s turn (J0 4, I0 1, K 4, `21-00-02Z` 12), edits a marker **≤ 2.5** (J0 3.7, I0 2.5, L0 2.1), keyed edits **≥ half** of the edits over the module and the tests, each landing — the brief's keys are what the model copies when the block is not — whole writes refused by the content guard 0, `restore_files` **0**, `omnia` misspelled 0, `edits` arguments that are not JSON ≤ 1.
- **The copying, `k = 4`.** `find`s refused **≤ 2** on `orders-reading`'s turn, edits a marker **≤ 2.5**, `restore_files` **0** (Round I's 3), the twelve scenario tests filled by keyed edits, no `edits` argument broken by its own escaping (Round I lost two at columns 4,819 and 2,719).
- **The manifest, `k = 4`.** `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` holds `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` before the turn, the brief saying `for the SQL store the slice reaches`; `cannot find module or crate omnia_wasi_sql` **0** times over the merged commit; the root `Cargo.toml` **untouched** beyond the scaffold's two stem lines, no `cargo add` at the root, wave 4's verify refused by `manifest_drift` **0** times.
- **The gate, `k = 2`.** `health` accepted covering **≥ 6/8**, its wave verified with the host's checks agreeing — met three times of three under rung 0, **p ≈ 0.75**; the turn **≤ 300 s** (J0 214, I0 325), `clippy -p health` **≤ 10**.
- **The gate, `k = 4`.** `orders-reading` accepted covering **≥ 6/9** with the merged module **compiling on this machine** (`cargo check -p orders` over the merged commit) and the wave verified — the discriminating clause, since Round I's 9/9 compiled nowhere — **p ≈ 0.4**; API names imagined in the merged module (`as_i64`, `table_store`, `table_type`, `.tables()`) **0**.
- **The finishing, both.** The report at round 64 or the breaker on both turns, as every small-model build turn under this model has been — the rung addresses the copying and not the finish, so a report before round 40 is observed and credited to nothing.
- **The ladder and the rebuild** (put only where both gates open them): `k = 3`, `5`, `6` each built to the label with **p ≈ 0.3** apiece, `--rebuild 2026-10-19T04-08-24Z` **≥ 3/7** with every verify honest against the band's 2/7, **p ≈ 0.35**.
- **Observed, never decided on:** keyed against span edits per turn and which the model reaches for first; the layout-tolerant matches taken (a `find` admitted by the third step), read from the `responded` lines; what the model does at a `Filled` refusal; whether the `omnia-wasi-sql` line is used by the module or stands held to nothing, which `unused_dependencies` passes over by name; each put's wall and tokens against J0's and Round I's; the `restore_files` count on `customers` where a probe runs on to it.

### Decision rule

- **Rung 3 holds** when both copy clauses hold — `find`s refused ≤ 1 at `k = 2` and ≤ 2 at `k = 4`, edits a marker ≤ 2.5 on both, `restore_files` 0 on both, keyed edits landing — whatever the gates say: the rung addresses the copying, and a gate missed with the copying clean is the model's finishing or its API, read as such. **Rung 3 does not hold** when a `find` the brief quoted as a key is refused, when the layout-tolerant step admits a `find` at the wrong place (the file's diff read against the edit's `replace`), or when the figures are no better than J0's and Round I's with the keys unused — the model copying blocks where keys were offered is the brief's wording, re-cut; a key refused `Filled` after the model's own earlier edit is the tool doing its work and no failure.
- **The table line ships** when `k = 4`'s manifest clauses hold — the line laid before the turn, `cannot find module or crate omnia_wasi_sql` 0, the root manifest untouched; an `unused_dependencies` finding naming it is the scaffold laying it where the module reaches the store through `StateStore` alone, read for whether the design's paragraph or the slice's words selected `Role::Table`, and the rule narrowed to the slice's own words if so.
- **The description's item closes** when `restore_files` is 0 on every build turn of the round with no `write_files` refusal before any; a restore after a refused `write_files` is the tool as described; **one taken on no refusal re-opens the item for its second cut**, the tool declared only on the round after a refusal, which is then an SDK item for the next round.
- **The recipe's flag** is read on the `write_files` shapes: 0 calls reaching the SDK with a stray string in `files` or an `edits` key is the server holding the arguments; one reaching it under `parameter` is read from the server's log for what it held and the flag's documentation re-read.
- **The gate is read whole.** Both probes build to the label — `health` ≥ 6/8 and verified, `orders-reading` ≥ 6/9 compiling and verified — and the ladder runs under this round's artifacts (`k = 3`, `5`, `6`, one at a time) and then the `--rebuild`, read against `09-25-05Z`; one of the two does not, and the round closes at the gate with which failure mode remains named — the finish (a report at round 64 covering what the slice had), the API (a crate or a method imagined, the merged module read on this machine), or the copying (the rung's own clauses) — and the next rung runs. A `--rebuild` building ≥ 3/7 with every verify honest is the band in `09-25-05Z`'s place; one building to the label is the arm's goal met and Phase 5's harvest read against it.
- **The control is read first and subtracted.** The plan, the tree, the model, the server's model and sampling, and the knobs are one across J0 and this round's `k = 2`, the four items and the flag the difference, so a clause met here and failed at J0 is the items'; a clause J0 met (`health` built, the wave verified) is credited to no item. Round I's `k = 4` ran under rung 0 alone over the same tree and brief, so the difference at `k = 4` is the items' and the sampling's spread K drew in Round I, read within that band.
- **A tunnel that drops**, a pod that stops, or a server that answers nothing is the put not made, or made as far as it ran: recorded with where it stopped, and the put made again from its start; the engine's retry absorbs a drop of under 21 s. The pod is stopped at the round's end whatever the figures.
- **Deviations are recorded first in the results**, as every round's have been: the pod and its setup, the component and binary each put was built from, a put repeated and why.

### Results

**Run as planned, with five deviations recorded first.** **The pod**: US-CA-2, where the volume `cxsks2la1u` holds Round I's models, had no H200 to offer, so the round ran on a pod created from nothing in a secure-cloud data centre `runpodctl pod get` does not name (`lmbdl4tk8feoru`, `roundk-h200`, one H200 141 GB, a 180 GB container disk and no volume, $5.29/h, created 20:57Z), set up on Round J's recipe (vLLM 0.32.0 in `/opt/vllm-venv`, the venv's `nvcc` 13.0.88 as `CUDA_HOME` with `nvidia-nvvm` and `nvidia-cuda-crt` pinned to it, the weights copied to `/dev/shm`) and served at the third start: the first start lost to flashinfer's JIT finding no `ninja` (`/opt/vllm-venv/bin` not on the server's `PATH`), the second to its link step finding no `-lcudart` or `-lnvrtc` (the venv's `cu13` tree ships versioned libraries alone; three symbolic links under `$CUDA_HOME/lib64` and `lib64/stubs` the fix), the third up at 21:42Z — 45 minutes of pod time before the first put. **The binary**: `emery-dev` no longer exists as a bin target (the engine's #371 made `genai` a feature of `emery`), so the dev runtime was built as `cargo build --release --features genai --target-dir target/genai` and the `emery` it produced put as `EMERY_BIN` (sha256 `10a22dc1…`); the README's `--bin emery-dev` command is stale and an item. The component `emery_omnia.wasm` (sha256 `b379b643…`, 4,300,903 bytes) was built from this tree at `8af0f67` (`build optimisations`, rung 3's items committed minutes before the build) over the engine's SDK at `f8e5f277` through the root manifest's path patches, and the binary from the same engine commit, so both artifacts stand at commits rather than working trees. **No control was put**: J0 is read from history, as the method has it. **The tunnel** was relaunched detached before the first put after its first loop died with the shell that launched it; no put was made over the first. **The HF CLI** needed `--break-system-packages` on the pod's Python. Each put's `/metrics` was read before and after it; the two puts' figures are theirs alone.

**k = 2, `2026-10-22T21-44-45Z`** (the label at `da8fc02f`, `Wave 1 verified`; `health` beside `start-http-errors`, read against J0): **the gate not met — `health` exhausted at round 64, its forced report covering 8/8 refused by `unparsed`, `bad_request` after 662 s, 1/7 merged.** The seed verify `ok` in **159 s** at 63 calls (39 shell, 16 read, 8 list — the checks passed by call 7, then `find -newer`, `stat`, and `ls` until the breaker), `passed: true`. **`health`, 434 s, 68 calls** (28 `edit_files`, 19 read, 19 shell, 2 `write_files`, no `read_doc`), **10 `clippy -p health` runs** (≤ 10 met), **69 edits in 28 calls — 6.9 a marker** against J0's 3.7 and ≤ 2.5 — **8 `edit_files` calls refused** against ≤ 1 (J0 4): three matching nothing, four matching two or three places, and one arriving as `{}`. That one was the turn's first reply: **165 s of silence and 32,768 output tokens, the server's one `length` finish of the round**, the tool call cut at the cap and handed over as `edit_files` with no `edits`, `missing field edits` the SDK's answer — the clause `length` 0 failed by the finish, not the flag. **Keyed edits 2 of 69, both landing**: the second call's two edits named the two handler keys as the brief quotes them (`REQ-016 start.health-live: the handler`, and the seven-id key of `/health/ready`) and filled both handlers at once, 21:50:17Z and 21:50:20Z. Every other `find` was a span: the ready handler's keyed replace carried a `\n` the model spelled as a newline and a bare `n` (`.await` then `n    .is_ok_and(..)`), the third call repaired it as a one-line span, and the module ran clippy clean at the seventh run, 21:52:22Z, with the probe written over `TableStore::query` and `StateStore::get` as the idiom spells it. **The eight test markers were never filled by key**: call 22 took their eight `#[ignore = "<key>"]` lines as eight one-line spans and replaced each with nothing — the key wrapped in the attribute the file shows, matched exactly, un-ignoring eight tests whose bodies still read `todo!` — then 24 edits in calls 23–27 fought over the test bodies with the one `let provider = provider().tables(ScriptedTables::default().on_query(` span, 18 of them refused as matching nothing or two or three places against the model's own earlier edits, until **the test file was written whole at 21:54:10Z (7,420 bytes, all eight tests filled, admitted) and the module at 21:54:37Z (5,594 bytes, admitted)** — the module's whole write re-introducing the stray `n` of the keyed replace at line 104, fourteen seconds after the turn's last clippy run (clean, 21:54:23Z), so the module the report covered was never compiled. The report at round 64 covered 8/8 and was refused by `unparsed` (`crates/health/src/health.rs:104: expected ';'`), the one finding; `cargo check -p health` over the copy fails on that `n` and three errors behind it. `restore_files` 0; whole writes refused by the guard 0; `omnia` misspelled 0; `edits` arguments that were not the shape 1 (the cut reply). Turn tokens: 3.59 M in (97.1% cached), 49,034 out.

**k = 4, `2026-10-22T21-57-10Z`** (the label at `cd5d6429`, `SLICE-004 customers`, the runner sealing `start-http-errors` as the empty commit the chain needs; `orders-reading` first, read against Round I's `07-54-01Z`): **the gate not met — `orders-reading` exhausted at round 64, its forced report refused by `untested_scenarios`, `bad_request` after 698 s, 3/7 merged.** The seed verify `ok` in **120 s**, 36 shell calls — the checks once each by call 10, then `touch` and `find -newer` twenty-six times — `passed: true`. **`orders-reading`, 508 s, 63 calls** (20 shell, 16 `edit_files`, 12 read, 9 `write_files`, 6 `read_doc` — every reference, `layout.md` through `omnia-api.md`), **20 `clippy -p orders` runs** (Round I 9) and no `cargo test -p orders` at all, **23 edits in 16 calls — 1.5 a marker** (≤ 2.5 met), **0 `find`s refused** (≤ 2 met; Round I's 32 calls lost two to their own escaping), **0 `edits` arguments broken by their escaping**, **`restore_files` 0** (Round I 3), **0 keyed**: the model read the module first and copied the formatter's own three-line `todo!(` layout back as its `find`, the design type's `pub struct ListQuery {}` and both handlers' blocks filled as exact spans in five edits by 22:00:04Z — the module grown from 6,849 to 19,609 bytes in fifty seconds, the two handlers written over `omnia_wasi_sql::DataType` and `TableStore::query` with the cache-aside read in front. Then **the module written whole at 22:01:16Z (18,845 bytes) and the test file at 22:01:30Z (8,430 bytes, four of twelve scenarios filled, eight left `#[ignore]` over `todo!`)**, and from 22:02Z the loop: `clippy -p orders`, a type error, the module written whole or edited, clippy again — **the module written whole eight times (18.3–19.1 KB) and edited eleven more**, the tests never touched again, until round 64. The forced report was refused by `untested_scenarios` naming the eight (`crates/orders/tests/reading.rs:77: REQ-033 …` first). **The merged module compiles to one error on this machine**: `cargo check -p orders` over the copy answers E0308 at `reading.rs:463`, `Ok(Some(order))` where the `serde_json::from_value::<Order>(..)?` above it was bound to nothing — Round I's module compiled nowhere. **The manifest clauses held whole**: `crates/orders/Cargo.toml` holds `omnia-wasi-sql.workspace = true` before the turn, the scaffold's line; `cannot find module or crate omnia_wasi_sql` **0** times (Round I 8), the module naming `omnia_wasi_sql` 33 times; the root `Cargo.toml` touched by the scaffold's two `orders` lines alone, `cargo add` **0**, `unused_dependencies` naming the line 0 times; wave 4's verify never ran, so `manifest_drift` there is unread. Imagined API names (`as_i64`, `table_store`, `table_type`, `.tables()`) **0**. `write_files` refused by the guard 0; `omnia` misspelled 0. Turn tokens: 6.36 M in (97.3% cached), 53,088 out.

**The server over the round.** From `/metrics` before and after each put: **k = 2, 128 requests, 5.58 M prompt tokens, prefix hit 0.968, 51,543 generated, 1 finishing `length`; k = 4, 101 requests, 6.85 M prompt tokens, prefix hit 0.972, 55,078 generated, 0 `length`**; about 200 tokens a second and 0.2 s to the first token; HTTP `400` **0** on both; `transport` 0, every completion `retries=0`; no undeclared tool, no prose, no nudge, `schema and answer type disagree` 0; `cached` 97.1% and 97.3% against the server's 0.968 and 0.972, within 1%. The `write_files` shapes: **0** calls reaching the SDK with a stray string in `files` or an `edits` key, so `--tool-strict-level parameter` held the argument shapes; the one `edit_files {}` is the reply the cap cut, read above. The economy: the two puts 23 minutes of pod time (about $2), the round $6.70 with the setup; the pod stopped at 22:12Z.

**Decision — rung 3 does not hold as written and the round closes at the gate, both probes failing it; the table manifest line ships; the description's item closes; no ladder and no `--rebuild`; `09-25-05Z` stays the band.**

- **Rung 3 does not hold.** The first copy clause fails at `k = 2` on every figure — 8 `find`s refused against ≤ 1, 6.9 a marker against ≤ 2.5, keyed edits 2 of 69 against ≥ half — and the figures there are worse than J0's (4 refused, 3.7 a marker) with the keys unused, which the rule names as the brief's wording to re-cut. No key the brief quoted was refused, no `Filled` refusal was earned, and no `find` was observed admitted at the wrong place, so the tool did what it was built to do where it was reached for. **What the two turns show about reaching for it**: the model addresses a block by what the file shows, not by what the brief quotes. The two keys it used are the two handler keys, which stand in the file verbatim as the `todo!` literal it had just read; the eight test keys it came nearest to were used as the `#[ignore = "<key>"]` lines the file holds, which the trimmed `find` does not equal and the exact step took as spans; the `todo!` keys of the tests and the design type's key were never named, their blocks copied instead. A key that is also a whole line of the file lands either way; one that is a bare string in the brief alone is not reached for by this model. The re-cut is therefore not the sentence but the key: make each marker's key equal to a line the file holds whole (the `#[ignore = "…"]` line itself, the `// <id> <subject>: <statement>` line above a handler), or have the placement read a `find` that wraps a key in its attribute as the key, so that copying the line the model sees is naming the marker. Which of the two is the maintainer's call; the first is the adapter's, the second the SDK's.
- **The gate, read whole, fails on both probes, and the failure mode that remains is the finish at both.** `health` covered 8/8 at round 64 over a module one byte from parsing — the stray `n` of an escaping slip, repaired once and written back whole — after the turn's first three minutes went to one reply that ran to the output cap. `orders-reading` compiled to one error over a module that reaches the store through the right crate and names no imagined API, with the twelve tests left at four filled while twenty clippy runs chased the module's types: the API and the manifest failure modes of Round I are gone at `k = 4` and the finish alone remains, the model fixing what the compiler names and never returning to what the gate names. Neither turn reported before round 40.
- **The table line ships.** Every `k = 4` manifest clause held: the line laid before the turn, `omnia_wasi_sql` found 33 times and missing 0, the root manifest untouched beyond the scaffold's two lines, no `cargo add`; `unused_dependencies` did not fire, so the rule is not narrowed.
- **The description's item closes.** `restore_files` 0 on both build turns with no `write_files` refusal before any — on `orders-reading`, the slice that took it three times under Round I's description, over the same tree and brief.
- **The recipe's flag holds on the shapes.** No `write_files` reached the SDK malformed under `parameter`; the flag stays in the recipe. The one argument that reached the SDK empty is the reply the 32,768-token cap cut, which `parameter` cannot complete — a `max_tokens` at the client, or the server's cap left as it is, is the next round's to decide, since one such reply cost three of `health`'s seven minutes.
- **The control is read first and subtracted.** The plan, the tree, the model, the server, and the knobs are J0's at `k = 2`, the four items and the flag the difference, so the worse copying figures are the items' as much as better ones would have been: the keys offered and not reached for cost nothing in refusals (the two keyed calls landed), and the eight refusals are the model's spans against its own earlier edits, as J0's four were; the `length` reply is the sampling's spread, seen once in 229 requests. At `k = 4` the difference from Round I is the items' within the sampling's band: 0 refusals against the two lost to escaping, 0 restores against 3, the crate found against 8 misses.
- **The band and the ladder.** No probe built to the label, so `k = 3`, `5`, `6` and the `--rebuild` are not put, and **`09-25-05Z` stays the small-model band**.
- **Deviations recorded**: the pod (`lmbdl4tk8feoru` created from nothing, two starts lost to the server's `PATH` and library links, stopped at the round's end with its container disk wiped — a next round sets up from nothing again, the fixed `start-vllm.sh` and `setup.sh` kept beside the round's logs); the binary (`emery` under `--features genai` in place of the removed `emery-dev` target); no control put; the tunnel relaunched before any put; `--break-system-packages` on the pod; no tunnel drop, no `transport`, no `400`, one `length`.
- **Round L.** The key re-cut above at `k = 2` and `k = 4` under this round's artifacts, with the SDK logging which placement step took each edit (the `responded` line carries none, so this round's keyed count was read off the `find` text); beside it the finish, which both turns now fail alone: the cap-cut reply, and a turn that fixes what the compiler names and not what the gate names — the gate's findings earlier than round 64, or `cargo check -p <stem>` run by the host as the Open item has it, so the compiler's error and the gate's finding arrive together. The evals README's recipe is amended to `cargo build --release --features genai` with the binary copied under the `emery-dev` name; the engine README's `--bin emery-dev` line and its `AGENTS.md` row for `dev.rs` are the engine's to amend.
